# The UX wave (2026-11-12)

> **Parent:** `docs/charter.md`, the root spec (MAWS's standing purpose, §1), parent pin `dee757515`. **Level name:** the UX wave, a child of the charter beside phases P0 to P5; the charter's §3 paragraph and §19 entry that name it are carried by the pull request that lands this document. **Consumes:** the comparison of 2026-10-23 and the owner's marks on it; P4's UX waves (`docs/doperpowers/ledgers/2026-10-23-P4-UX1.md`, `2026-10-24-P4-UX2.md`, `docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/ux-wave/`); P5 as built (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-org.md`); the project registry (`docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-10-project-registry-design.md`). **Layout:** this file is the root; each child's section is a file of its own under `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-12-ux-wave/`, and the root and those files are one document (Decision Log, "One document in several files"). Children dispatch per their grain hint, each carrying its section as its spec; each child's execution document, ledger and pull request open by citing this document (path, child id, parent pin).

## Purpose

The owner uses MAWS every day to supervise Claude Code sessions, and has kept a list of what gets in the way: marks on a comparison of MAWS with Claude Desktop, later observations with screenshots, deferred board tickets, and findings from P5's sittings. 240 items in all. Some were already built, some asked for the same thing twice, some contradicted each other, and some named a problem without a settled answer.

This wave turns that list into changes the owner can see, under the owner's own bar: **every change a clear improvement, nothing hasty.** A change is made only when the code shows what is wrong and the direction of the fix is settled, by the owner's words, by Claude Desktop's behaviour where the owner named it as the answer, or by a question put to the owner. Where none of these holds, the item waits for an answer, a design round or a hands-on check, and this document says which.

After the wave: requests for the owner's attention are drawn where the work is and stop counting once answered; the transcript reads as the agent produced it, with tool runs folded into one line and the to-human view drawn by the mod; what arrives in a session from elsewhere takes one line; the composer queues and steers on Claude Desktop's model, with the departures U4 names (among them the hold after a Stop, and what Esc does); Review, comments and source control are one simpler flow; a session on another machine can be forked, renamed and archived from this Mac, and an act that cannot reach it says why; and the smaller defects in each surface are gone.

How to see it working: the owner's sitting on the wave's build, following the scene script composed from the children's acceptance (Parent-level acceptance, 2).

## Parent-level acceptance

The wave closes when all of these hold. They are checked as written at recomposition; all children landed is not the same event.

1. **Every item is accounted for.** Each of the 240 ids in the register (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/ux-wave-2026-11/register.md`) has a final state with its evidence: built and accepted by the owner; already fixed, with the commit named; dropped or moved by the owner's word; or waiting in a named place (the visual renewal, a tracker row, a board ticket).
2. **The owner calls each cluster a clear improvement.** The owner goes through the scene script on the wave's build on their own machines: one scene per cluster, composed from the children's acceptance items, each scene naming the items it shows. A scene the owner does not call a clear improvement is reworked or reverted before the wave closes. Agents pre-run every scene under a scratch config home and hand over the evidence first.
3. **What the owner marked `keep` is unchanged.** The 23 `keep` items in the register behave as before; the scene script includes a check for each one a child's work could have touched. One `keep` is narrowed by the owner's own answer of 2026-11-12: the relaunch that restores everything (l7-shell-12) no longer brings back a New chat with nothing in it (U13).
4. **Every reversed ruling is recorded.** Each earlier ruling this wave reverses (Decision Log, "Rulings reversed") has a dated entry in the document that held it, written when the reversing change lands.
5. **Nothing gated got worse.** The final build passes the gates of `scripts/ci-gate.sh` (class 1a), the Electron e2e groups on a Mac under the load rule, and the measured cases master is gated on at that time (the render group and the idle daemon's cases, as P5 left them); a case that was void or over its bound on build F (`f1852de3a`) by the owner's ruling is compared with its reading on F, not with its bound.
6. **Going back is safe.** A profile the wave's build has written still opens on build F with its sessions, and no tab and no pin is lost (UC-14). What F does afterwards is by its own rules: it files the tabs into its per-project sets again, so their order, a split, the floating card and each set's tab in front are F's; it opens the side column again when it loses a floating card that had folded it, and then writes `main.tabs` and `regions`; it records its own remembered session of the side column; and the notice that tabs were merged shows again after each run of F. This is shown by the cross-build run named there before the wave's build runs on a profile the owner uses.

## Grounding baseline

The grounding ran on 2026-11-12 against `03cee6582` (build F plus documents; the source tree equals master `dee757515` outside `apps/desk-hub`). Eleven agents each grounded one surface in the code and in Claude Desktop `app-5.1231.2`, and a twelfth read the debt tracker and P5's records for further candidates; their files stay outside the repository (they describe the owner's screenshots). Twelve writers then wrote the child sections against the same commit and the registry branch, each re-reading the code and Claude Desktop's source for its items; where a section found the grounding or the cut wrong, the section says so and Surprises and discoveries lists it.

| Already built or fixed | 25 |

| Waiting for the owner's answer | 12 |
| Waiting for a design round with the owner | 2, and the second half of a third |
| Waiting for a hands-on check | 7 |
| Left for later, with the reason | 6 |

A further 247 tracker rows were read as candidates and stay in the tracker.

## Design

### The wave's laws

These bind every child. [binding: they are the owner's standing positions and rulings, and each child would otherwise re-derive them differently]

1. **Claude Desktop is the benchmark** (charter D-14 and D-20 as amended 2026-11-12): where an item follows engine behaviour or a basic surface, it is built the way Claude Desktop `app-5.1231.2` builds it, read in its source and cited as `research/SOURCES.md` prescribes. The Claude Code CLI `2.1.322` is the reference for how the engine itself words and delivers things. An item that departs from Claude Desktop on purpose says so and says why.
2. **A pure mods client.** MAWS draws what the engine and the user's mods produce, and adds no drawing of its own in their place. Where no mod can draw (law 5), MAWS mirrors the mod's rule and says which rule it mirrors.
3. **Less on screen.** A row, a label or a card that does not earn its space goes. No setting is added where one behaviour serves.
4. **No complexity for a small gain.** When the grounding found an item larger than it reads, the item's section says so and the cheaper route is taken unless the owner asked for the larger one.
5. **Three routes, one behaviour.** A session reaches the window by one of three routes, and many of the wave's defects exist on one route only. Every item that touches the transcript, the requests or the composer states what it does on each route, and "not on this route" needs a reason:
   - **a session MAWS runs** (in code: lane B): MAWS launched the engine headless and drives it over its wire through the engine host; the user's mods can draw here.
   - **a terminal session** (lane A): the engine runs in a terminal; MAWS watches it through the sminos bridge and reads its transcript file. No mod draws here.
   - **a session on another machine**: either kind, reached over the machine link through that machine's daemon (`mawsd`); drawn from transcript rows. No mod draws here.
6. **The engine is not guessed at.** A claim about what the engine sends is verified against `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/` or a new probe on engine `2.1.322` before code depends on it (CLAUDE.md, working rules). The children name their probes.
7. **Visual language is not this wave's.** Rounder geometry, materials and colours are being designed elsewhere and come after. A child uses today's tokens and shapes; an item that is only visual language is handed to the renewal.
8. **Keep means keep.** A child that would change a behaviour the owner marked `keep` stops and reports.

### Words used in this document

- **Request:** something a running session needs from the person before it can go on or finish: a permission prompt, a question (the engine's AskUserQuestion, MAWS's own ask tool, the sminos ask tool), a plan to approve, an engine dialog or an MCP server's form. In code a request is an *attention item* (`src/shared/attention/`). The cards that are steps of a send (trusting a folder, a relay that is down, a start that failed) are not requests: they keep the composer's place and UC-1 does not count them.
- **Arrival:** anything that enters a session other than the person's typed prompt: a message from another session or a subagent, a background task's notification, a scheduled trigger, an answer to an asynchronous question, a slash command's output.
- **Tool run:** consecutive tool calls with no text between them, drawn as one group.
- **The to-human view:** the report the to-human mod draws: the agent's messages to the person, with the work between them folded under "working record".
- **Card column:** the column of cards that floats over the right of the main area beside the transcript (Environment, Context, Connectors and others; UC-9). **Side surface:** a tool opened beside the transcript (Review, Files, Terminal, Browser, Source control). **Main tabs:** the tab strip over the main area. **Destination:** a page that replaces the main area without being a tab (Customize, Projects, the Artifacts library; UC-12). **The Settings dialog:** Settings, opened over the window, over the tabs or over a destination; not a tab either (UC-12).
- **The machine link:** the connection from this window to another machine's daemon; an *offer* is an act that machine accepts over it (`src/endpoint/offers.ts`).

### The owner's answers of 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-16

Eight questions were put to the owner as cards before the sections were written, nine more with the document, and two that followed from notes on those answers; the answers are recorded verbatim in the register. The owner then approved the document and gave the word to build ("Approve and start", 2026-11-12, about 21:55Z). Answers that came after that word, to questions put when their children started or with a drawn example, are in the same list; eighteen came on 2026-11-13 on six drawn examples (the composer, Settings, Customize, the comment card, Source control, and the sidebar with the title bar and the pages), and the last three the same day, on two cards the first builds raised: paid test runs, and a linked file outside a session's folders. The answers after those in the list came from 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-16: on cards the builds raised, on the drawn examples of the later landings, and some as words the owner typed, in place of an option or with no card. This document dates each answer by UTC; the execution documents date the same answers by the owner's local day, so an answer can carry an earlier day there. Both stand. In short:

- **The to-human view:** the mod draws first, and MAWS mirrors it. The mod draws in sessions MAWS runs; in terminal sessions and sessions on another machine, MAWS applies the mod's fold rule itself. The mod change the owner ruled on 2026-10-24 (the unmerged doperpowers branch `to-human-draw-in-maws`) is redone by this wave on the mod's current main and merged by the owner (UC-13). (U2)
- **Thinking:** its own step, in order, inside the tool run. Two conditions from the owner: thinking keeps the shape it has today and does not look like a tool row; and an opened run must not grow too long. U2 meets the second with a rule for which thoughts get a line, and shows it to the owner in a drawn example beside the alternatives before the group work is built. (U2)
- **Where a request is drawn:** in the transcript, with the composer free: a question in the row of the call that asked, a permission prompt at the transcript's end. The owner added a queue of what waits, in the card column, and confirmed this reading. The jump-to-latest button says when a request waits. (U1)
- **Tabs:** one strip for everything open, across projects and machines, restored after a relaunch; no hidden per-project sets. A group is then a named set of sessions opened as tabs. (U13)
- **Settings, Customize and the Library:** the owner first answered "a page, like Desktop", on a card that was wrong about Claude Desktop: there Settings opens as a dialog over the window and only Customize is a page. On the corrected card, the same day, the owner chose "As Desktop really does". Settings opens as a dialog over the window, over the tabs or over a page; Customize, the Library and Projects open as pages that replace the main area with the sidebar kept. None of them is a tab (UC-12). (U7)
- **Background work in the card column:** two cards, Subagents and Background tasks, each present only while it has something running. The owner added: Subagents is a tree that lists the first level and opens a nested agent when its parent is clicked. (U8)
- **Tools beside the chat:** tabs, one in view at a time, in place of the stacking ruled on 2026-10-24. The owner added that stacking should stay possible when wanted: "make the panel's attribute to be a tab, but they can be splitted, stacked, and so on". The section read that as a second group of tabs that the person can make under the first, and the owner confirmed that shape on a card the same day ("Tabs, plus one group below"; a free arrangement and tabs alone were the other two). (U8 with U6)
- **A failed background task:** only its line in the transcript tells of it; no card, no Needs you entry, no notification. "Same with Claude Code Terminal." (U3)
- **A message waiting behind a running command:** it is moved past the command only on Send now; nothing is moved by a timer. (U4)
- **A message sent while a question waits:** it is delivered as a message and the question keeps waiting. The owner added a Dismiss button on the question, as the sminos question tool has, for the case where the message settled it. (U1)
- **The tab of a session whose machine is not reachable:** three states, in place of "offline" for all. (U9)
- **Thoughts in an opened run:** a thought of ten seconds or more gets a line. The owner added that past thoughts matter less and that the thought in progress should read as a status with its effort and time. Asked on a card whether that status replaces the summary's line there, the owner chose "The label only": the line of a thought in progress reads its tier, effort and time, and the summary is read by opening the line. (U2)
- **A long opened run:** not bounded in height. (U2)
- **The turn line in the folded to-human view:** shown only while a working record of that turn is open. (U2)
- **The engine's next-prompt suggestion:** not built now; logged as debt. (U4)
- **The ask tool of a session MAWS runs:** two steps. In this wave MAWS's own ask is loaded up front and does not block by default; the sminos question tool for such a session comes later, as its own design. (U1)
- **MAWS's artifact and document edit tools:** behind tool search again. (U1)
- **An empty New chat after a relaunch:** it does not come back. A New chat that holds a draft or an attachment, and every started session, comes back. This narrows the owner's `keep` on the relaunch that restores everything by this one case. (U13)
- **"End session" for a session on another machine:** not added. Stop ends a turn, Archive ends the process, and an idle session is released by its machine without anyone acting. (U9)
- **Customize's outer level:** My items and Discover; the kind (plugins, skills, connectors) becomes a filter inside each. (U11)
- **Icons for connectors that have none:** fetched from Google's favicon service, behind a switch in Settings that starts on; the generated letter tile shows while the switch is off or when a fetch fails. The owner chose this against the recommendation, changed the option's "it starts off" to "it starts on", and wrote: "we'll fetch, and mostly, because that's much more intuitive design". It reverses an earlier recorded decision not to make that fetch. (U11)
- **Where "make this the default" writes:** into the Claude Code user settings, for the model and the permission mode, so sessions started in a terminal follow it. How the act is offered was answered on 2026-11-13 (the last entry of this list). (U11)
- **Touch ID for reading another machine's connectors:** none for someone who already administers that machine; every other administrator act there keeps its Touch ID. (U11)
- **The side column and the card column, on a drawn example:** a strip too narrow for its tabs shrinks the tabs not in view to their icons; a second group is made from a tab's menu only, and dragging comes with a later landing; the Subagents card's tree is as drawn with one change of the owner's, "(+N)" as the Claude Code CLI writes it, in place of "N nested" and of the count of what runs under a row; the Subagents card lands now, and a session started in a terminal on this Mac has none until a follow-on landing gives that route its source. (U8)
- **The Browser opening by itself:** it shows the agent's tab, unless the person is using the selected tab. This reverses the owner's choice of 2026-10-08. (U12)
- **The computer-use ticket the owner remembered:** none was found, and a new one is filed on that plugin's board (cua#368, on 2026-11-13). (U12)
- **Google Drive:** asked whether a check may create and delete two test files there, the owner said yes, and ten minutes later deferred all Drive work: "defer drive work for now. we won't use gws, but will rather later use real MCP connectors through Drive MCP and GWS MCPs". The check was not run, and Send to Drive stays as it is. (U12)
- **Comments made in one pass:** sent together, as one message. (U5)
- **The export dialog:** it opens in the folder of the last export, also after a relaunch. This settles the owner's two marks of 2026-10-23 for the later one. (U5)
- **Buttons that ask the agent to commit, push or open a pull request:** none. The owner chose neither option and wrote that they are not needed and that Source control is nearly read-only, for the person to know what is happening. The item is not built. (U5)
- **Source control, on its drawn example (2026-11-13):** nothing is done by hand ("Nothing (Recommended)"); the branch's commits are listed, and a row opens that commit in Review ("List, and rows open"); a pull request is merged or closed on GitHub ("On GitHub (Recommended)"). (U5)
- **The composer, on its drawn example (2026-11-13):** four answers, each the recommended one. "Arrow under a bar": the mark that says a message will wait is the send arrow under a short bar. "Stop in black": Stop stands on the strong fill, the text's colour, and Send on the accent. "Corner, then column foot": the `/btw` card lies over the bottom right corner of the session's pane and, from U4's second landing, at the foot of the card column whenever that column is shown. "MAWS's panels first": with nothing typed, the "/" list begins with the commands MAWS draws a panel for. (U4)
- **The regrouped Settings, on its drawn example (2026-11-13):** three answers, each the recommended one. "Preferences first": everyday preferences come first, then the sessions' and the engine's sections, then Org. "No heading": Org stands without a heading of its own. "Supervisor": the name of the new section that holds the supervisor and the memory budget. (U11)
- **The Customize page, on its drawn example (2026-11-13):** a connector added by hand gets no icon from Google's favicon service ("No, directory only"), and a row under My items › All is the full row of its kind ("Full rows"). (U11)
- **Where "Make … your default" lives (2026-11-13):** the owner chose none of the card's options and wrote: "for model picker, I won't even show 'default' -- The user may not know what the default model is, so just show models only without the default option. so just haiku, sonnet, opus, fable, no default. And as I know, Claude code automatically change the default every time the user change the model, right? Let's just keep it that way. If user want to have fixed default model, then setting or custom hook like we use are what they can do on their side." and "for permission, just keep Claude Code's default, auto. Some user might want to make Bypass permission as default, and let that happen in settings. I don't think too busy composer is good. I'd make composer simple on where we can." The terminal's model picker does save a pick to the Claude Code user settings; the control protocol MAWS drives writes none. The design that follows was ruled by the wave's architect the same day: the model menu lists the engine's models and has no "Default" row; a pick is sent to the session as today and is also written to the Claude Code user settings (`model`) on the machine the session runs on, as the terminal's picker does, so that it starts new sessions in MAWS and in a terminal alike; neither composer menu has a "make default" item; and the default permission mode is one control in Settings, which writes `permissions.defaultMode` and offers Bypass permissions as well, behind the confirmation Bypass asks for wherever it is chosen (ruled on 2026-11-13: the owner's words name it). The earlier answer, "Write Claude Code settings", stands. (U11)
- **A comment's screenshot, on the comment card's drawn example (2026-11-13):** in a message of several comments each screenshot sits under its own comment ("Under its own comment (Recommended)"). (U5)
- **The sidebar, the title bar and the pages, on their drawn example (2026-11-13):** four answers, each the recommended one. "Button on the row": a session's row under the pointer also carries a Hide button, beside the item in its menu. "Ring alone": the usage ring stands alone, with no number beside it. "No": Settings left open is not open again after a relaunch. "Leave the page": a side-surface key pressed while a page is open leaves the page and shows the surface. (U7)
- **Paid test runs (2026-11-13):** asked whether paid live test groups may be run for the wave (a group that runs a real Claude engine on the owner's account; the comment card's landing had changed two of its scenes; its script caps its spend at 1.70 USD, and a run takes about 45 minutes), the owner answered "Once per landing (Recommended)". A paid live group runs once per landing, on that landing's final head, when the landing changed that group's scenes or what the group proves; a second paid run of the same landing is a new question to the owner unless the first failed for a harness reason that is named (UC-10). (all)
- **A linked file outside a session's folders (2026-11-13):** over a screenshot of the file viewer's notice "Refused: path outside the session's directories" for a PDF in the Downloads folder, the owner wrote "another small change idea:  can we just [Image #2] allow it", and then answered a card of two questions, each with the recommended answer. "Files the transcript names": on this Mac a file outside a session's folders opens read-only when that session's transcript names its path; the process that reads it checks the path against the transcript first; a session on another machine keeps the refusal. Ruled on U6c's review the same day: a path is named when the transcript writes it whole as a link or a file chip (U6's O-37), and the check stops a direct request for another path, not a caller that can also send a message into that session; the card's "so the window cannot ask for other files" said more than this, and was corrected to the owner on 2026-11-13. "Own small landing, now": it is built as a landing of its own, U6c, after the tab strip's landing, and the rest of O-37 stays in U6b. The read-only half of the first answer, and the refusal for a session on another machine, were reversed by the answers of 2026-11-15, below. (U6)
- **A terminal beside the chat (2026-11-13):** asked how wide a terminal opens in the side column, after the first Mac runs of the column's landing showed it 46 columns wide at 360 px, the owner answered "Wide, like Review (Recommended)": a terminal opens at 60 % of the room right of the sidebar and its width is remembered with Review's, Files' and the browser's; Subagents and Background tasks keep opening at 360 px. (U8)
- **The Environment card after the agent moves, on its drawn example (2026-11-13):** three answers, each the recommended one. "Cut at the start": a folder path too long for its row loses its start and keeps its tail, so the folder's own name stays in view, with the whole path in the tooltip. "Under the folder row": the muted line "moved from <the launch folder's name>" sits directly under the folder row. "In the folder row": an agent that steps into a sub-folder is shown by the folder row itself, which changes in place with no extra line, so the card keeps its height. (U8)
- **The sidebar and the title bar as built (2026-11-13):** shown pictures of the built sidebar and title bar, the owner answered "Keep as built (Recommended)" to both: a session's row under the pointer keeps its Hide button and its three dots, and the project's name stays left of the tabs, cut at about nine characters. (U7)
- **Previews of files, and the artifact link (2026-11-14, typed):** in reply to a list of the four questions then open, the owner wrote "MAWS preview shouldn't be limited to PDF, and agent's artifact mention opens in the side panel. for the rest, need more detail." The architect's readings, told to the owner and not contradicted: files the agent writes open inside MAWS read-only, PDF first, then Word, PowerPoint and Excel; the mention is a link, and a click opens the artifact in the session's side column, not in a main tab; the agent's own ability to open an artifact pane is removed. (U6: l6-artifacts-01, O-44)
- **The artifact link and the previews, on their drawn examples (2026-11-14):** five answers, each the recommended one. The look of a mention of an artifact and of a link to a file in the agent's text: "As Claude Desktop (Recommended)", accent words underlined only under the pointer. A mention of an artifact that is already open as a main tab: "Show that tab (Recommended)". Whether a file's tab stays among the six things the agent can still put on screen by itself: "Keep all six (Recommended)". Whether the look of Word, PowerPoint and Excel files drawn with nothing installed is good enough: "Yes, as drawn (Recommended)"; the preview ships only if the containment test of its viewer passes. Whether an Excel preview may work the totals out itself: "Compute and say so (Recommended)". (U6)
- **Connector switches, and custom connectors (2026-11-14):** eight answers. On the card that asked where a per-chat connector switch should sit, the owner chose none of the options ("Two switches (Recommended)", a default per machine and a switch per session; "One switch, like Claude Desktop"; "Per-machine default only") and typed: "two switches mean that a connector can be installed like plugin -- user scope and project scope." The same day, each the recommended option: "Claude Code's config (Recommended)" for where "Add custom connector…" writes; "Off by default, on per project (Recommended)"; "Plain text (Recommended)" for the form's value fields; and, after the owner was told that the mechanism first described (an allow list by name) cannot name a connector a plugin brings, four cards: "Engine's project switch (Recommended)", "Last press wins (Recommended)", "On for all projects (Recommended)" for the first day, and "On a press (Recommended)" for the servers of the old Settings list. So a connector is on for all projects, on for one project, or off, and a new one starts off; there is no switch for one chat; the engine's own switch per project carries it, and MAWS carries out "All projects" through the chats it starts; a custom connector is added in Customize and written to Claude Code's own configuration. One condition is the architect's and not the owner's words: connectors that come with a Claude account are not covered until a check on a machine signed in to an account. (U11)
- **Typing in a deck or a document (2026-11-14):** asked whether the first letter or a jamo of a word had ever gone missing while the owner typed in a deck or a document (the comparison of 2026-10-23 reported it, and it reproduced only under automated typing), the owner answered "No, never seen it". The item closes with nothing to build, and its tracker row closes as not reproduced. (U10)
- **Edit on a message above a compaction (2026-11-15):** two probes had shown that the engine accepts a rewind to a message from before a compaction only in the process that compacted, and refuses it after a restart. On the card "Edit, compact" the owner answered "Only while it works (Recommended)", over "Never above a compaction" and "Always, with a reason": Edit above a compaction is offered only until that session's engine restarts, and the window draws it only where it knows that the engine process is the one that compacted. (U3)
- **Requests in the transcript, on their drawn example (2026-11-15):** five answers, each the recommended one. "Column width, bordered": the prompt at the transcript's end is as wide as the transcript's column, bordered and raised. "Each line → Dismissed": a dismissed question's row keeps its lines, each reading "Dismissed". "Answered in the terminal": the row of a question the terminal answered reads so on each line until the answer reaches the agent. "Waiting glyph ◐": the jump button's pill carries the waiting glyph. "Two lines": a question's form in its row has two lines above its first option. (U1)
- **Approve and retry (2026-11-15):** three answers, each the recommended one. "Button greys out": after the press the button is greyed until the agent has read the approval, and only then does the row read approved. "\"You approved cmd\"": the waiting approval's row above the composer reads "You approved `cmd`", with Send now and Delete. "Yes, in the newest turn": a blocked call nobody has approved stays in view under the newest ended turn's line; it is a small landing of its own after U1c (#703). (U1)
- **The to-human view, on its drawn example (2026-11-15):** seven cards. "Fold line": the owner typed, in place of an option, "we actually recently made changes to how '> working record' shows in terinal -- in short, it no longer shows '> working record', but shows '> <summaries>' instead. and i think that's how we should do it. mods already shipped it and is active already in terminal. it uses haiku call instead of forked call, and is cheap enough to use. But let user be able to disable it if they want to cut that token cost, and in that case it'll just be working record." It is read as: the record line carries the mod's summary of the stretch, with a setting in MAWS that turns the summaries off, built with U2b's second pull request and the mod's own; U2b's first pull request draws `› working record`. The six others, each the recommended answer: "Unnamed rows": "! command and errors stay (Recommended)"; "Lone thought": "It folds (Recommended)"; "Mod layout": "Evened out for MAWS (Recommended)"; "Switch frame": "Bordered box (Recommended)"; "Other machine": "This app's switch (Recommended)"; "Lane off": "Let the mod draw (Recommended)". (U2)
- **A failed act on a waiting message of another machine's session (2026-11-15):** "Failed act": "Taken back / Sent now (Recommended)". The line reads "Not taken back: <reason>" for Delete, Edit or ↑ and "Not sent now: <reason>" for Send now. (U4)
- **The stacked Review, on its drawn example (2026-11-15):** six answers, each the recommended one. "Wrap, drop scope word (Recommended)": the toolbar's totals no longer name the scope, which the pressed chip above names, and what does not fit on one line wraps. "Words wide, glyphs narrow (Recommended)": in a narrow column a file's Viewed is a check box and "Open file ↗" is "↗". "The card's usual width (Recommended)": the comment card under a line is the same 300 px card as on a page or a document; in Split its row spans both sides, and the card stands under the side that holds the line. "One line (Recommended)": a comment that waits to be sent shows one line under its line. "No, the line says it (Recommended)": the chip of a comment on a removed line reads like any other, and "Before the change:" stands above the quoted line. And the option labelled "Show fewer" (Recommended): once Source control's list of commits is shown whole, the line that showed it becomes "Show fewer" and folds it back to eight rows. (U5)
- **An agent of a terminal session that ended with no record of how (2026-11-15):** asked what such an agent's row says in the Subagents panel while the session itself still runs (the terminal's bridge says the agent no longer runs and the transcript holds no result for it, or the agent that started it has ended), the owner chose the recommended option, "ended": a new plain word for these two cases, on this Mac and for a terminal session on another machine alike, with "ended with the session" kept for an agent whose terminal has really gone. The other two options were "ended with the session" for these cases too, and "stopped". (U8)
- **A file outside a session's folders: editable, and Open File… (2026-11-15):** on three cards about what may open a file from outside a session's folders the owner answered "Keep it as it is (Recommended)" (a file the agent opens by itself opens only when the conversation links it); typed in place of an option, on a link inside a shown Markdown file, "I'd go even further to make it editable too. There are lots of situations where I have to open a file from external folder that need to be edited or be looked at. I don't see a good reason to block it from editing or reading."; and answered "Yes, the same rule (Recommended)" (a link in a subagent's own transcript opens as one in the main conversation does). On the two cards that followed: "Every outside file (Recommended)": any file MAWS shows from outside the folders can be edited and saved, and nothing is written until Save is pressed; and "Yes, add it (Recommended)": a command Open File…, in the File menu with ⌘O and in the Files list's menu, opens a file the person picks beside the chat. U6e builds the editing and Open File…; U6b-4 builds the two links. (U6)
- **The bar of a file from outside the folders, on a drawn example (2026-11-15):** four answers, each the recommended one. "No label if savable (Recommended)": a file that can be saved shows its full path and no label, and one that cannot reads "Read-only"; "Dark text (Recommended)" for that label; "Label opens a panel (Recommended)": the label is a button whose panel gives the reason; "Right-click menu only (Recommended)": the Files list offers Open File… in its right-click menu alone. (U6)
- **A file outside a session's folders on another machine (2026-11-15):** "Open and edit there too (Recommended)" and "Pick a file on that machine (Recommended)": for a session on another machine such a file opens and is edited there, and Open File… picks a file on that machine, as a landing of its own after the one for this Mac (U6f), limited to a person who may already open a terminal on that machine. On the four cards of that machine's file chooser, the same day: "One folder at a time (Recommended)", "That machine's home (Recommended)", "Shown, after the others (Recommended)" and "Owners only for now (Recommended)". (U6)
- **A link inside a previewed file, and the Files list in a narrow column (2026-11-15):** two answers, each the recommended one. A click on a link inside a previewed Word or PowerPoint file: "Nothing; show the address (Recommended)". Where the Files list appears when its button is pressed in a side column too narrow for the list and the file: "In the file's place (Recommended)". (U6)
- **A PDF, an HTML page and media drawn in a file's tab, on their drawn example (2026-11-15):** four answers, each the recommended one. Whether an HTML file's tab also lets its code be read: "The page alone (Recommended)". What the tab of an HTML page does when the page names files beside it, which MAWS does not load: "Say so (Recommended)", one quiet line under the file's bar, "Shown without the files beside it". What the row of a file that is still not drawn says: "True sentences now (Recommended)"; each row says why, and a file too large to copy from another machine has no button. Where Chromium's own PDF toolbar shows: "Hide beside chat (Recommended)", hidden beside the chat and kept in the main area. (U6)
- **Artifacts, on their drawn examples (2026-11-15):** eight answers, each the recommended one. Whether a PDF's spacing matches the editor's: "Yes, match the editor (Recommended)". How tight Compact is: "As specified: 90 % / 75 % (Recommended)". How Letter and A4 behave in a document's Export menu: "Always show the page in force (Recommended)". What is behind the things drawn above the box of the chat bar over an artifact: "A surface only while needed (Recommended)". The glyphs of the bar's two buttons: "Expand glyph and × (Recommended)". How another session's chat appears over an expanded side surface after the chat was shrunk to the bar or hidden: "As you left it (Recommended)". What a card of "Make something new" shows: "Icon, name, one line, Blank (Recommended)". The mark of a Space that has instructions: "The word "Instructions" (Recommended)". (U10)
- **The summaries on the to-human view's fold line (2026-11-16):** two answers, each the recommended one. Where the switch that turns the summaries off is, and what it stops: "A switch in MAWS (Recommended)", under To-human in the mods list, on by default; turned off, sessions MAWS starts make no new summaries from their next start, and a session started in a terminal follows the mod's own setting. What a fold that was already summarised reads once the switch is off: "Only new folds (Recommended)"; it keeps its summary, and only folds closed after that read `working record`. (U2)

None of the register's questions is open. The four that were open when the first landings started (PDF preview and what an artifact link opens, U6; Korean typing, U10; where connector switches sit, U11) were answered on 2026-11-14, and every card put since is answered. The register also lists the decisions the sections made beyond the owner's words that the owner is most likely to see differently. Nothing is with the owner now. One question is known and not put: what the preview does with an Excel workbook if a single cell cannot be marked "not computed" (l6-artifacts-01; U6), put only if the viewer's qualification finds that. What the owner is still asked to look at, on a drawn example or on the built window, is listed with the ordering ("Looks the owner is asked for before a landing").

## Children

Each child's section is a file under `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-12-ux-wave/`. A section holds the child's purpose, its acceptance (numbered, observable, per route), its edges, the contracts it owns or consumes, its design inheritance, and one entry per item (today, after, why this and not that, not included), then its probes and checks, the rulings it reverses with the sentence to record, what waits, and what is not in the child. An executor reads this root and its child's file. The summaries below say enough to see the cut; the files bind. Items are named by their register ids: `l<n>-…` a mark on the comparison, `O-nn` an observation, `#nnn` a board ticket, `S-nn` a sitting finding, `X-nnn` a tracker row.
