**U1 · Requests.** A request no longer takes the composer's place, and stops being announced the moment it is answered. A question's form is in the transcript in the row of the call that asked; a permission prompt is a card at the transcript's end, three buttons and under half its old height, answered by a click or by ⌘↩, ⇧⌘↩ and Esc under Claude Desktop's guards; the composer and Stop stay usable throughout. What waits out of view is named by the "Waiting for you" card in the card column and by the jump button. A terminal session's asynchronous question behaves as one and shows its answer on its own row. A message sent while a question waits is delivered and leaves the question waiting, and every question's form has a Dismiss button. From a notification or the hover card a waiting permission can be denied, and allowed once when it is a read or a shell command shown whole. A call auto mode blocked gets "Approve and retry". Owns UC-1; consumes UC-2, UC-3, UC-6, UC-7, UC-9. Four pull requests: UC-1 and what does not draw in the transcript (U1a); the placement, after U3's reader and U8's slot (U1b); l4-approvals-12, whose probe of a classifier block is done (U1c); and which of MAWS's tools load up front (U1d).

**U2 · Transcript structure.** Consecutive tool calls read as one caption line, folded by default while the turn runs and after it, in Claude Desktop's wording; opening it shows today's rows with thinking in the order it happened and in the shape it has today, a line for each thought of ten seconds or more (the owner's choice of 2026-11-12). A closed turn's line says what the turn did by kind instead of counting calls, and a subagent's report no longer reads as a peer session's turn. The to-human view is the mod's where a mod can draw and MAWS's mirror of the mod's per-stretch rule elsewhere; MAWS's own report bar, its per-turn "Working record" row and its "To you" label go. Owns UC-13; consumes UC-1 (a folded run's line says when one of its calls waits for permission), UC-2, UC-3. Two landings, the second in two pull requests. U2a: the runs, the thoughts and the turn's line, the same on every route; a call a Stop interrupted is a stopped step of its run, and the file reader reads a refused call's kind as the wire's reader does. U2b, after it: the to-human view, with UC-13's raise and the mirror, itself cut in two on 2026-11-15: the mirror on every route first, then the cutover to the mod's drawing, which also brings the mod's summary onto the record line with a switch that stops new summaries. The owner looked at the drawn example the group work (O-29, O-27, #479, O-15) waited for on 2026-11-12; nothing in MAWS waits for the mod's pull request.

**U3 · Transcript rows.** Whatever enters a session from elsewhere reads the same on every route and takes one line: a peer's or a subagent's message is a dim line that opens on demand and folds with its turn; the end of a background task is one line coloured by outcome, and a failed one raises no error card; a peer's message is never drawn as the owner's bubble, nor the owner's steer as a peer's message; a slash command's output is a light line under the command. Code blocks wrap inside the text column, message actions sit under the message, an answer can be copied and forked from, a compaction in progress says so, ⌘F finds in the session and ⌥⌘↑ and ⌥⌘↓ move between prompts. Owns UC-2; lands first in the transcript's model (UC-3). Three pull requests: the reader with its three callers and the arrival rows (U3a); then the rest of the rows (U3b); then find in the session and the keys that move between prompts (U3b-2). Four engine probes come first.

**U4 · Composer.** Enter always sends. While the agent works, Enter hands the message to the running turn and ⌘Enter keeps it for after the turn; a message not yet read is drawn once, in a stack above the composer, where it can be sent now, taken back with ↑, edited or deleted. Send now delivers without killing the running command (the engine moves it to the background), and Stop still stops and holds what waited. One small icon serves send, stop and "this will wait"; the slash palette lists names; the mode menu sets Bypass apart; the model chip reads as a name; pasted text stays text; `/btw` is a small card at the bottom right. Owns no contract; consumes UC-2, UC-3, UC-6, UC-7, UC-9, UC-14. Probe P1 ran on 2026-11-12, and two further passes on 2026-11-13 under the environment MAWS's launch sets: the engine keeps the command alive in every case, and behind a command it ends the turn and answers the message in a new one; every message waiting at the next step goes together and is drawn as the one message the engine makes of them; a message sent now from an after-the-turn row moves a command only once the engine has registered it as a task, about three seconds into the call. A further probe, on 2026-11-15 for U4b, bounded the second of these: the turn is cut, and the messages are one, only while the command is not yet a task; later the messages are read in the same turn, each its own. U4b sends an after-the-turn row down the request's path, so the early press moves the command too. A waiting message does not move a running command to the background by itself: the owner chose Send now alone (2026-11-12). The owner looked at the drawn example on 2026-11-13 and took the recommended answer on each of its four questions: the queue mark is the arrow under a bar, Stop stands on the strong fill, the `/btw` card lies over the pane's corner and then at the column's foot, and the `/` list has MAWS's panels first.

**U5 · Review, comments and source control.** One comment card everywhere (a document, an HTML page, a diff line, a page in the in-app browser) with one checkbox for where the comment goes, drafts that survive, and comments that reach terminal sessions and sessions on other machines, where today none can be sent. Review is one scroll of the whole change with comments on its lines; new files are counted; "This branch" means one thing; Source control becomes a page that is read (where the work is, what is uncommitted, the branch's commits and which of them are pushed, the pull request and its checks), and a commit opens in Review; MAWS no longer stages, discards, commits, pushes or opens a pull request by hand, and no button asks the agent to (the owner's answers of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13). Owns UC-5; consumes UC-3, UC-4, UC-7. Four pull requests: the card and its sends first (U5a; U10 and U12 code against them); then Review and source control, cut in three, on whose drawn example the owner answered six choices (2026-11-15): the stacked Review, in the window only (U5b-1); Source control as a page to read, with its reads over the machine link (U5b-2); and the side thread for a session on another machine (#686).

**U6 · Files, links and the keyboard.** A file opens beside the chat, as a tab inside the Files side surface, and moves to the main area only when the person moves it. Files shows what changed and finds a file by name. A link the agent writes opens in MAWS whatever its form, and when it cannot, the notice says why. A Word, PowerPoint or Excel file the agent wrote is drawn inside MAWS, read-only, with the Mac's own app one click away; a mention of an artifact reads as a link, and only the person opens it. "Open in" hands a file or the session's folder to an editor, a terminal or Finder. Chords reach the side surfaces, Back and Forward, and zoom; one table stands behind the menus, the shortcut sheet and every child's new chord. A file outside a session's folders opens when the session's transcript names it, the agent sent it or the person chose it with Open File…, and can be edited; on another machine the same holds for a person who may open a terminal there (the owner's answers of 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-15; U6c, U6e, U6f). Owns UC-6; consumes UC-4, UC-7, UC-12, UC-14. Its landings: the keymap's shape first and alone (U6a, landed; U1, U3 and U4 add chords against it); then U6c, the file a transcript names, after U13a (landed); U6d, the artifact link (landed); U6e, a file from outside the folders made editable, with Open File…, and U6f, the same for a session on another machine; and the rest (U6b), after U8's selector and on top of U6c, cut in five: a file beside the chat (U6b-1); a PDF, HTML or media file drawn in its tab (U6b-2); Files' Changed group and Go to file (U6b-3); every form of link, and "Open in" (U6b-4); the rest of the keyboard (U6b-5).

**U7 · Sidebar, title bar and destinations.** A session can be hidden with one click, on its row or in its menu, and brought back; a new session is named by the first six words of its prompt; a row of a session on another machine opens in a new tab like a local one; the title bar's project name no longer runs under the first tab; usage is a small ring in the title bar; Settings, Customize, Projects and the Artifacts library stop being tabs: Settings opens as a dialog over the window, the other three as pages beside the sidebar. Owns UC-12; consumes UC-1, UC-9, UC-14. Two landings, both on master: U7a (S-11, #407, O-03, O-38 with #559, O-16) first, which waited for nothing; U7b (O-40, O-47, UC-12) after U7a and U13's strip. With U9 neither order is imposed: both build to one order of a session row's menu, and the second to land merges. The owner answered the four choices of the drawn example on 2026-11-13, each as recommended: a row under the pointer carries a Hide button, the usage ring stands alone, Settings is not open again after a relaunch, and a side-surface key leaves an open page.

**U8 · Card column, background work and the working folder.** The column shows Environment (open), Context and Connectors, and, only while there is any, the waiting requests, a tree of running subagents and a list of running background tasks; Artifacts moves to the title bar's "More surfaces" menu. Finished work no longer takes the top of the two panels, and a panel for watching work opens narrow. Once an agent moves into a worktree of the same repository, the Environment card, Review, Source control, Files and a new terminal follow it. (The engine probe of 2026-11-12 showed that the engine records such a move, a shell's `cd` included.) The tools beside the chat become tabs of the side column, one in view, and the owner can stack a second group of tabs under the first (O-36, the owner's answer of 2026-11-12; built with U6). Owns UC-4 and UC-9. Five landings: the working folder's selector with today's behaviour, first and small (U5 and U6 branch from it); the column and the panels; the live working folder in two, a repair of today's build at the moment the engine moves a transcript (U8c-1) and then the live signal (U8c-2), after a probe of what the engine reports when the agent moves; and a terminal session's subagents on this Mac (U8d), which gives that route the source its Subagents card needs. Until U8d lands, a session started in a terminal on this Mac shows the Background tasks card and no Subagents card; with it, a terminal session that this Mac drives through its bridge shows its subagents as a session MAWS runs does.

**U9 · Sessions on other machines.** A remote tab says "Connecting" while the link comes up and keeps its transcript and the reader's place through every reconnect; its row reads Working when its turn starts. From this Mac the owner can fork a session there (the machine's owners, with no Touch ID on a machine they own), rename it, and archive and unarchive it; a Stop that cannot reach the machine says so; the Touch ID sheet names the machine. Owns UC-7, which settles for every act on a session whether it works on another machine or says why not. One pull request. It and U7's first landing both edit the session menu, and neither order is imposed: both build to one order of the menu, and the second to land merges. The words for a link that is not up are three states, by the owner's answer of 2026-11-12, which reverses the ruling of 2026-10-27.

**U10 · Artifacts.** An agent's edit to a document is no longer refused because of a formatting clean-up nobody asked for, and a document's history gains no revision from someone who only looked. The agent can make a document fit a page, publish an HTML page from a file, and remove a draft it abandoned (with an Undo for the owner). Over an artifact the chat shrinks to the composer's bar and can be hidden, with "Show chat" in the header. "Make something new" is four cards with one design-system choice that binds. A Space can carry instructions for agents. Owns no contract; amends X9 by dated lines (UC-15). An early pull request of its own for the two fixes to writes nobody made, reviewed by the panel (U10a, landed). The rest (U10b) is cut in four pull requests: an HTML page from a file, a document's page setting and the Export menu (U10b-1); an agent's delete of an artifact, with the owner's Undo (U10b-2); the chat as a bar over an artifact, with "Show chat" (U10b-3, after U1b); the four cards of "Make something new", the design-system choice and a Space's instructions (U10b-4).

**U11 · Settings, connectors and the Org pages.** A connector that needs a sign-in can be signed in from the chat that uses it, whichever kind it is. The dialog that lists a chat's connectors is one line per connector. A new chat shows its plugins, connectors and skills before the first message. Settings is regrouped under two headings, App and Sessions & engine, with Org after them, each row reads in one line, and a search finds a row by its words. On the Org pages every refusal says what to do next, every sheet dims the window, and the Channel says when it is offline and opens from the palette and the View menu. Customize has My items and Discover at its top level, with the kind as a filter, and every plugin, skill and connector has an image: a connector's site icon where Google's favicon service has one, behind a switch that starts on. A plugin's connectors are listed with the others and sign in from there. A connector is on for all projects, on for one project, or off, and a new one starts off; the on/off is the engine's own switch per project, and "All projects" is carried out by MAWS through the chats it starts. A custom connector is added in Customize and written to Claude Code's own configuration. A model picked in a chat is also written to the Claude Code user settings, so it starts the sessions that follow in MAWS and in the terminal alike; the model menu has no "Default" row, neither composer menu has a "make default" item, and the default permission mode is one control in Settings. Another machine's connector list stays current without a Touch ID per read. Owns no contract. Its landings: U11a, the ten items first designed, at any time (the owner saw the regrouped Settings on 2026-11-13); U11b, the default writer, after U4a and U11a; U11c, Customize, after U11a (the owner's look at the drawn page is done); U11d, connector icons, after U11c; U11e, the connector list without Touch ID (#455, ruled on 2026-11-13), at any time; U11f, the sign-in of a dynamic connector on a session of another machine, after U9; U11g, the custom connector form and the Connectors dialog's sentence; U11h, a plugin's connectors under My items (X-184, cut out of U11c on 2026-11-14), after U11c and U11g; and three with no name yet, in this order after U11h: the old list's move on a press, the record with the default and the switches, and account connectors after their check. The owner answered the choices on both drawn pages on 2026-11-13. Settings: the everyday preferences first, Org without a heading, and the new section named "Supervisor", each as built. Customize: a connector added by hand gets no icon from Google, a row under My items › All is the full row of its kind, and the defaults as this paragraph gives them, ruled by the architect from the owner's own words.

**U12 · In-app browser, computer use and delivery.** An agent asked to open and test a page uses the Browser side surface without being told which tool reaches it; a page the agent shows again is reloaded; the Browser gains two device sizes and a screenshot to the clipboard; a pane the agent opens from a session on another machine appears on the device the person is at (#435, after U9); while an agent drives the Mac through computer use the session says so and offers Stop; when the Browser opens by itself it shows the agent's tab unless the person is using theirs. Its Drive item is deferred out of the wave by the owner's word of 2026-11-12 (ticket #400). Two changes only the computer-use plugin can make are written as tickets for that project and filed there (cua#368, cua#369). Owns no contract; amends X9 by a dated line (UC-15).

**U13 · Tabs, New chat and session groups.** One strip holds everything open, from every project and machine, the same after a relaunch. New chat adds one tab beside the others and brings an empty one back instead of adding another; a New chat tab that arrives late takes nothing from the tab opened meanwhile; the title bar's chip names the current project and starts no session. Owns UC-14 and the strip's model (UC-8). The one strip is a plan; session groups, the sidebar's views (O-42) and the folded sidebar (O-24) are designed with the owner on top of it, six questions in order, none decided.

**U14 · Checks on the owner's machines.** Seventeen questions the code cannot settle, each answered on the owner's own machines with its outcomes and what each changes; four need the owner in person (about forty-five minutes; the fourth is a look at which variants of Claude Desktop the owner's account shows, and at how its Settings opens), the rest an agent on the mini. A spike: its deliverable is `docs/doperpowers/evidence/ux-wave-2026-11/checks.md`, never a merge.

## Cross-child contracts

Each contract names its owner (the child whose landing defines it) and the children it binds. The statement here is what every child may rely on; the owner's file carries the full content (types, tables, wording), and the others code against it and do not re-decide it. Where a child's file and a statement here differ, the statement here binds and the difference is reported to the wave's architect.

**UC-1 · A request waits only until it is answered.** Owner U1; binds U2, U3, U7, U8, U9, U10, U12, U13. A request counts as waiting, everywhere it can show (its own drawing, the session's row and tab, Needs you, badges, notifications, the counts told to another device, the feed another machine reads), only until the person answers it. Once answered it raises nothing, whether or not the engine has yet confirmed the tool's first frame. The predicate is `waits(item)`, true only for a `pending` item, in `src/shared/attention/kinds.ts`, with `waitsOnAnswer` beside it; `isOpen`, which counts an answered and unconfirmed request as open against charter §4.6, is removed. The attention service publishes only waiting items, so a machine on an older build that reads the feed is right too. U1 also gives the predicate by tool-use id, `waitingByCall` (for U2's runs), and by agent, `waitingAgents` (for U8's "Approval needed"), beside `waits` in the same file; in the renderer's attention store `selectPendingOf` keeps its name and now means "waits", and `selectWaitingByCall` and `selectWaitingAgents` stand beside it. On the terminal route an item stays `pending` until the bridge reports it settled, and "answered" there means the bridge accepted MAWS's answer; U1's file states the route's rule. "Accepted, not settled" has one holder, on the session's machine: a window on another machine mirrors what its machine says and holds no mark of its own. For a terminal session of another machine, whose items no feed carries, the device that answered keeps its own `accepted` record, and a third device waits for the settlement. Against a session's machine on build F, that machine's own windows and its digest show today's behaviour until it is updated. A terminal session's digest is a reader of the predicate too: it reads `waiting` and the count of pending requests from the requests its list holds as waiting and that are not asynchronous questions, so an open asynchronous question, or an answer accepted and not yet settled, leaves the session `working`. Where a session is shown without its transcript (the chat over an artifact folded to its bar, or hidden; U10), a waiting request is not drawn there: one line, "Waiting for you · N", opens the conversation, and "Show chat" carries the mark. No child adds a second definition of "waiting".

**UC-2 · One reading of every arrival.** Owner U3; binds U1, U2, U4, U8, U9. One pure reader, `readArrivals(row)` in `src/shared/engine/arrival.ts` (and `readNext`, the same reading in order, which reads an arrival with two carriers once), classifies what enters a session other than the person's typed prompt. Its input is the raw wire frame or transcript row, never an X1 event: X1 lowers the origin to one word, and what proves who sent a row is on the raw row alone. That proof is the origin object, with one exception: the engine gives a scheduled trigger no origin object on any route, so that class is read from the fields the engine writes in its place (`turnOrigin: 'scheduled'` on the file's row, the terminal's `scheduled_task_fire` row, and on the live wire the `command_lifecycle` frame whose `wakeup_source` is `schedule_wakeup`). Three callers use it: the engine host's normaliser for the live wire, the transcript-file backfill, and the reader that serves a terminal session on another machine (the desk web app's session view draws through that same reader and follows it, one reading); a session MAWS runs on another machine arrives as events its own engine host made, so it is right once that machine runs the wave's build. Classes: a peer session's message; a subagent's message or hand-back; a background task's notification, by outcome, and one with no outcome (a monitor's event, a check-in); a scheduled trigger; the sminos bridge's own wake prompt; an answer to an asynchronous question (with the call's id and the answer in words, or "dismissed"); MAWS's own consent message for a call auto mode blocked (`consent`, the one line of U1's "Approve and retry"); a slash command's output; the person's own steer delivered by a fallback route, which stays the person's. The reader classifies; where a delivered row is placed stays with each caller. The class reaches the window on the existing companion events and one optional field, `arrival`, on X1's `user.message`: an amendment of X1, recorded in the charter when U3 lands. A reader ignores an entry of that field whose class it does not know, so a class added later (U3's second landing adds `command`) does not fail the event on an older build. Arrivals are drawn one line each, in the CLI's wording, and the divider row gains `opener`, the class of what opened the turn. U1 fills a question's row from the answer class; U4 words a waiting answer from it; U2 reads the classes for the to-human fold and names a turn from `opener`; U8 relies on the arrival's line (the task notice, or the report line of an agent that handed back) as the trace of work that left its card.

**UC-3 · The transcript's model has one writer at a time.** Owner: the wave's architect; binds U1, U2, U3 and the children that insert. `src/renderer/src/transcript/model/` and `transcript/view/` are changed by three children in this order: U3 lands its reader and arrival rows first; U1's placement and U2's grouping start from a master that contains them. U3 owns the arrival and message rows and `transcript/view/Transcript.tsx`; U2 owns `groups.ts`, `classify.ts`, the group row, the turn line and `src/renderer/src/owner/`; U1 owns the question, ask and plan rows, the region at the transcript's end, and the waiting state of a call's row and of the jump button. Rules between them:
- A waiting request's slot is not a boundary of a tool run: a slot that arrives while a run is open is drawn directly after that run's row, in arrival order when several wait, and the run keeps its row and its id; with no run open the slot stands in its place (U2a builds this in the group machine and edits no row of U1's). From U1b on no slot row is derived from the engine's request events, so the group machine's carry finds none and stays as it is; the row kind stays, because desk web builds such rows itself (U1's file, UC-3). A row that holds a waiting form (a question's, an ask's or a plan's, drawn in the transcript) ends a run, is never inside a fold (a run's, a turn's, the to-human view's working record), and asks no mod's site while its request waits.
- A call that waits for the person's permission stays in its tool run, and the run stays as the reader left it: a request neither opens nor closes it. While the call waits, its row reads "Waiting for approval", and so does the line of a folded run that holds it, in the warning tone (U1's l4-approvals-04; U2's A3 and A4); the prompt is answered at the card at the transcript's end. This is how Claude Desktop's default transcript renderer treats a group with a call awaiting approval; the renderer Desktop offers behind a server gate draws the group open while a step waits, and that rule was not taken (Decision Log, 2026-11-12, "A run is not opened for a waiting call"). In the to-human view, mod-drawn or mirrored, a run inside a folded working record stays folded with it, and the card at the transcript's end is the request's whole drawing. The run that holds the waiting call is one row with one id from before the request to after its answer: while the prompt is still docked (before U1b), its slot row stands directly after that run and never inside it or between its calls. The words "Waiting for approval" are one function, `waitingLabel` in `transcript/model/copy.ts`, which U2a builds for the run's line and U1b's row calls. An allowed call is then an ordinary member of its run; a call that reads denied or auto-denied is a row of its own, and so is one refused because auto mode was unavailable; a call stopped by the person's Stop, or interrupted or cancelled by the engine, is a stopped member of its run (U2's file, O-29).
- In a session whose mods surface is attached, U3's arrival rows ask the engine's `UserMessage` site and its sent-message row asks `ToolUse`, with the engine's props, so that a mod can fold or draw them as it does in the terminal; a mod that keeps the engine's drawing leaves MAWS's line.
- A row that folds still opens under the store's `jumpTo`, which find, the prompt chords and the waiting-requests card use. Where MAWS owns the fold (a run, a turn, the mirrored to-human view) the jump opens it. Where the mod owns it, MAWS can neither read nor change the fold, so the jump's target is *revealed*: `jumpTo` records it, and while it is revealed MAWS asks no site for it and draws it itself (U3 writes the set, U2's site asks read it).
- Insertions by other children are single rows or filters, made on a master that holds the last landing (the inserting child merges master): U4's filter that removes the held tail and the bubble of a message not yet read (a prop on `TranscriptSlot.tsx` and `Transcript.tsx`, a parameter of `withQueueTail`, and the mark it reads, the turn's `waited` and the row's `unread`, in `transcript/model/reduce.ts`, `derive.ts`, `rows.ts` and `schema.ts`), and with Send now the divider's cause `message` and its words, a moved call's label with one condition in `statusOf`, the mark a process's exit leaves on a turn that holds a moved call, and the one bubble of messages the engine joined (the same files, `copy.ts` and `cells.ts`), all made on a master that holds U3a; from U4a's fix wave, the moved call's fact on its cell (`rows.ts`, `schema.ts`, `cells.ts`), one line in `transcript/view/cells/CellLine.tsx`, by which a cell whose moved fact is set draws its label before the shell cell's description, `opensTurn` in `transcript/model/head.ts`, the joined message's place, and in `transcript/lazy.tsx` the export `chunksSettled`, which settles once every chunk has loaded or failed; U4b's one export in `transcript/model/copy.ts`, `askQuestionTitle`, with one call each in `AskCapsule.tsx` and `serialize.ts` in place of the same expression, and for U4b's joined answer three more in `transcript/model/`: in `reduce.ts` the record of the answers of every envelope found in a user message, in `user-text.ts` the person's words read without the envelopes, and in `derive.ts` the hiding of a row that is envelopes alone, each for a question answered in the transcript; board ticket 704 removes those three, adds one call in `reduce.ts`, the split of a person's message where events enter the window, and has the record of an answer and the hiding of a row take its one test of an envelope; board ticket 711 adds one call in the ask row's derivation in `derive.ts` (U1's row), which joins an answer that came in an envelope to its question by its place; U2a's fifth and sixth insertions, by rulings of 2026-11-13: in the ask row's derivation in `derive.ts` (U1's row), an ask whose result is a refusal that reads stopped reads cancelled, as withdrawn, which U1b keeps when it merges after it; and one argument at one call site of U3a's `transcript/surface/Tools.tsx`, so that a mod's rewrite of a call draws the artifact by the title its row read; U5's user row that draws several anchored comments in one message; U1's one optional prop of `EditDiff`, `whole`, in `transcript/view/cells/EditDetail.tsx`, by which a request's card can show the whole proposed change; U10's third action on the `artifact` row; U9's two insertions in `Transcript.tsx`; one string each from U9, U11 and U12 in `transcript/model/copy.ts`.

**UC-4 · The session's working folder.** Owner U8; binds U1, U5, U6. *The working folder* is the checkout the agent's main loop is working in now: the launch folder until the engine's own record shows that the main loop moved into another worktree of the same repository (through the engine's worktree tool, which is Claude Desktop's rule, or by a `cd`, which U8's probe of 2026-11-12 showed the engine records: the first row of the main transcript after any move carries the new folder), then that one. A move inside a checkout, a subagent's move and a move into another repository do not count. One selector answers it: `workingFolder(record)` in `src/shared/sessions/working-folder.ts` and `useWorkingFolder(appSessionId)` in the renderer. Review, Source control, Files, a terminal's starting folder, "Open in" and the Environment card read it; none reads `record.worktree?.path ?? record.directory` itself (the code spells it on 21 lines in 20 files today, four of them exceptions, and the endpoint's `checkoutOf` in `src/endpoint/targets.ts` reaches the same answer in another form and moves with them), and a test keeps it so. What is about the record and not about where the agent is (the archive and delete dialogs, the Resume picker, the index's writes) stays on the record. The Files tree's roots are not such a reader: the tree keeps every root it has today (the launch folder, the added folders, the worktree) and the selector only says which one the agent is in. The root Files opens first, and three displays of the record's directory alone (the location chip of a session MAWS runs, a request's folder line in its card and in the sidebar's preview), are left by the first landing, because moving them cannot be shown equal to today, and are sorted by the second (U8's file lists them). Of the folder line less is left to sort: U1's first landing removes it from a request's docked card, its Overview card and its inline card, and keeps it in the hover card and in the sidebar's fallback preview, which is never drawn in the app; those two are what the second landing sorts. Two landings, the second cut in two: the selector with today's behaviour, first and alone, so that U5 and U6 branch from it; then U8c-1 (#621), a repair of today's build at the moment the engine moves a transcript, and U8c-2 (#593), the live signal, which is the folder of the main transcript's newest row. The branch shown for the working folder is the one git reports there, for the card and for the record's label alike: the engine's rows keep the launch checkout's branch after a `cd`. A `relocated` row is never read as the working folder. The record's own folder fields keep the earlier build's meaning and move on such a row as today; the launch folder and the agent's folder are two optional facts beside them, read through the one selector (U8's file, l5-code-14). The second landing also widens the folders a session's files may be read from (`recordDirectories`, `src/services/files/allowlist.ts`, `src/endpoint/targets.ts`), because Files and Review must read where the agent is: that is a security boundary. Its bound: the folders added are two, the launch folder and the working folder. Each is learned from a fact and becomes the root of a read or a write only through the one admission, evaluated at the act: it must be a work tree of the repository the record's own folder is in, passed through the checks every root passes today (the real path, the session's scope, and the rule that a caller on another machine never gets the machine's home as a root); a folder read from a transcript is never trusted without them. The admission binds every command that checks a folder against a session. The landing takes the review panel with a reviewer on that boundary.

**UC-5 · One comment card.** Owner U5; binds U3, U4, U9, U10, U12. A comment on a document, an HTML page, a diff in Review or a page in the in-app browser is written in the same card with the same draft model. The card has one destination control, a "Send to main" checkbox: unchecked by default, ticked by default in Review and for a session started blank for the comment, and ticked and locked for a session on another machine until a side thread can be opened there (UC-7). Unchecked, the comment opens a side thread. A ticked comment is sent through the composer's own send path (`sendToSession`), which is what makes comments work for terminal sessions and sessions on other machines; the window stops calling `side.sendToMain`, and the command stays registered and uncalled, because removing a command a machine owns would end the link to a machine on build F (its removal is logged as debt). Several comments made in one pass are sent together on Review, the in-app browser and HTML pages; documents, decks and designs stay at one unsent comment per artifact. A draft survives the card closing, except the person's own Close, and does not survive a relaunch. After a send to main the card's place says "Sent to <title>" only for a confirmed delivery; an unconfirmed hand-off, which the terminal route can give, has its own line, which says the comment may not have arrived, stays, and has one action, Show, to the session's pending send. A composing card says which session receives it when that is not the one on screen.

**UC-6 · One keymap.** Owner U6; binds every child that adds a chord. `ACTIONS` in `src/shared/keymap/actions.ts` is the one list of the app's keys; the application menu, the shortcut sheet, Settings › Keyboard, the palette and the tooltips are drawn from it, and a child writes its key nowhere else. U6 lands the table's shape first, as its own small pull request: `ActionSpec` gains an `'edit'` menu placement, `submenu`, and `fixed` (a key its own editor handles, listed and never dispatched). **Focus first:** a region that has its own meaning for a key keeps that key while it holds the keyboard (the page's find and history in the browser, an editor's own commands, the deck's stage); everywhere else the key is the app's. A chord an editor may keep is a `menu` row; a chord that must win inside editors and terminals is a `renderer` row with scope `app`. Existing chords do not move (⌘0 Overview, ⌘K palette, ⌘L focus the composer, ⌘. stop, ⇧⌘J next Needs you, ⌘/ shortcuts, ⌘B, ⇧⌘B, ⌘\). A chord that answers a request acts on a fresh press only, never on a key repeat (U1's four request rows, by the option `fresh` of a binding in the dispatcher), so a chord held through one answer does not answer the request that takes its place. The wave's new keys, each row added by the child named (U6's file holds the collisions found and what each becomes):

| action | default | child |
|---|---|---|
| Send; new line | Enter, always; Shift+Enter, Option+Enter (fixed keys of the editor) | U4 |
| Queue for after the turn | ⌘Enter while a turn runs; sends when idle (`composer.queue`; the reply fields keep `composer.send`) | U4 |
| Send the first waiting message now, or stop | Esc in the composer while a turn runs (fixed) | U4 |
| Take back the newest waiting message | ↑ in an empty composer, before the prompt history (fixed) | U4 |
| Model menu, effort, mode menu | ⇧⌘I, ⇧⌘E, ⇧⌘M | U4 |
| Search prompt history | Ctrl+R, composer focused | U4 |
| Find in session; next, previous | ⌘F; ⌘G, ⇧⌘G (Edit › Find) | U3 |
| Previous, next prompt | ⌥⌘↑, ⌥⌘↓ | U3 |
| On a permission prompt in view: allow once, allow lasting, deny | ⌘↩ (with an empty draft), ⇧⌘↩, Esc; under Claude Desktop's guards, ahead of the composer's own ⌘Enter and Esc | U1 |
| On a question's or a plan's form | ↩, digits, Esc, only with focus inside the form; Esc on an asynchronous question sends nothing | U1 |
| Terminal, Review, Files | ⌘J, ⇧⌘D, ⇧⌘F | U6 |
| Go to file | ⌘P | U6 |
| Attach selection to chat | ⇧⌘L | U6 |
| Back, Forward | ⌘[, ⌘] | U6 |
| Zoom in, out | ⌘=, ⌘−; Actual Size has no chord (⌘0 is Overview) | U6 |
| Project channel | no default chord; a View menu row | U11 |

**UC-7 · An act on a session works on another machine, or says why not.** Owner U9; binds every child that adds or changes an act on a session (U1, U3, U4, U5, U10, U11, U12). Every act a person can take on a session is, for a session on another machine, in exactly one of three states: carried by an offer; answered by main itself; or declared unavailable, with its control drawn disabled and one sentence. No control is drawn enabled where the act cannot work now, and no act ends without its effect or a sentence that names the reason; one function in `src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts` words both. `src/endpoint/offers.ts`, `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts`, `src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts` and `src/endpoint/capabilities.ts` are U9's files. U9 adds, behind one capability (`rl2.session-acts`): Fork (in place, optionally from a prompt and with a first message; the machine's owners; signed unasked on a machine the member owns), Archive, Unarchive, Rename, the owner's read of archived sessions (cut to the sessions the caller may manage, by an entry of its own in `src/endpoint/calls.ts`), Dismiss (`attention.acknowledge`), taking back a waiting message (`session.steer.cancel`) and a pane's answer (`maws.pane.answer`). An act known only later goes into the asking child's pull request as an insertion in U9's files, with a capability of its own, and U9 reviews it: U4's new command for Send now (after its probe); U5's side thread (a fork with the thread's lineage, under Fork's authorisation but the side thread's own rule for a running turn; cut out of U5b on 2026-11-15 into a landing of its own, ticket 686, and declared unavailable with today's words until then); U5's three reads, of a branch's commits, of one commit's diff, and of the working tree against a commit with its new files counted (l5-code-06 for the first two; the third, ruled 2026-11-15, is what "This branch" and Uncommitted are read with, l5-code-03 and l5-code-04; calls under the checkout rule, one capability for the three); U11's sign-in of a dynamic connector (its landing U11f), as a command of its own (a machine's requirement is looked up per command, so one command cannot need a capability for one input and not for another), whose input carries, for a press from a row of MAWS's own list, the revision of the configuration that row showed; and U12's one-shot request from a session's machine to one device on the client port, behind a capability a device lists once it serves that kind (a device on build F lists the old client-port capability and would answer "no client"). Fork's point is a prompt: `rewindTo` is the id of one of the person's prompts and the fork holds what precedes it, so "Fork from here" on an answer passes the next prompt, or null. A remote pane stays mounted while its machine is unreachable; cards and side surfaces there keep their last content and draw no error for a read the link could not carry. A machine on an older build does not list the capability, and the control says to update it.

**UC-8 · Shared files have an owner.** Binds all. A child that must edit a file another child owns keeps the edit to the smallest insertion, names it in its section, and merges master once the owner's landing is there; when two children each insert in a third's file, whoever lands second merges master first. The wave takes master by merge and never rebases a branch: reviewers pin ranges on it, and a rebase moves them.

| files | owner | others insert |

| `src/renderer/src/request-card/`, `composer/docked/`, `tools/maws/ask/`, `sessions/TrustCard.tsx`, `SetupCard.tsx`, `src/shared/attention/`, `src/main/attention/`, `src/services/attention/`, `src/services/lane-a/attention.ts` | U1 | U2 (U2a: one condition on the `tool.result` branch of `services/attention/service.ts`, `event.seq !== null && event.reseeded !== true`, so that a denial read from a transcript file or from a rebuild raises no attention, with a new test file beside it; made on a master that holds U1a's and U3a's edits of that file), U3 (O-34's branch in `services/attention/service.ts`, a file U1a and U3a both edit, so the second of them to land merges master first; one tool name in `request-card/tools.ts`), U8 (transitional, in `tools/maws/ask/connect.ts`: a Question sheet that opened by itself remembers the tab it replaced and gives it back when its questions are gone; U1b removes it with the automatic open, O-17), U7 (in `tools/maws/ask/connect.ts`: `asked` on the ask sheet's open, by which the sheet closes neither a page nor the Settings dialog; U7b) |
| `src/renderer/src/session/SessionPane.tsx` | U1 | U2 (U2b: the report bar's mount and its import leave, and the view switch is mounted a second time, directly above the holder notice), U4 (the transcript slot's prop, and the `/btw` card's mount with the positioned box it needs), U10, U11, U12 (one line each) |
| `src/renderer/src/transcript/` | per UC-3 | per UC-3 |
| `src/shared/engine/arrival.ts` and its three callers | U3 | U2 (U2a, on a master that holds U3a: three insertions in the two producers, `src/engine-host/normalizer.ts` and `src/engine-host/fs/backfill.ts`: the optional `thinkingDurationMs` on a finished thinking block's event in each, and in the file reader's result producer `denial` read from the row's `toolDenialKind`, which this spec had given to U1c), U8 (the exit a gone terminal leaves: in the transcript follower, `src/engine-host/fs/service.ts` and `chain-follower.ts`, beside the backfill, and for another machine's rows in `lane-a/plane-transcript.ts`), U4 (in `src/engine-host/normalizer.ts`, on a master that holds U3a: the mark of a turn's end that a message sent now caused, and the engine's replay of joined messages; U4b, in `arrival.ts` itself: `readAnswerEnvelopes`, the reader of the envelopes inside a joined message), board ticket 704 (in `arrival.ts`: U4b's slicing functions as one, `envelopeSlices`, and `personsText` over the members; the split's call in the normaliser's emitter and at the end of the backfill's `recordToEvents`; the person's words at a rewind's boundary in the normaliser, and in `chain-follower.ts` the one-line form of a rewind anchor's last prompt read as the person's words), board ticket 711 (in `arrival.ts`: the consent sentence built from the constant of `src/shared/engine/grant.ts`, which the file exports again with the test beside it) |

Where U7a's and U1a's insertions meet, in the listener of a terminal session's state, the rule after the merge is (ruled 2026-11-13, at the chain merge): the digest's phase and tool come from a current snapshot only, and its waiting count is what the attention list holds, at all times (UC-1: the list is the one holder). So inside a reconnect the digest keeps the last snapshot's phase and tool, and the count for the list's five-second grace; after the grace the count reads 0, as the list does (a tracker row). **UC-9 · What the card column holds.** Owner U8; binds U1, U4, U7. The column floats over the right of the main area when a session's tab is active, no side surface is open, the person has not turned the cards off, and there is room. It is not drawn while a side surface or a destination is open, and no request, agent or task opens the column or one of its panels by itself (an agent's `show_pane` and the Browser's own opening are U12's and unchanged); so the waiting-requests card is an aid, and the jump button's mark (U1) is the signal that always shows. The cards, in order: Waiting requests (only while a request of the session waits, UC-1; U1 supplies the component through the slot `sessionCardWaiting`, U8 mounts it first); Environment (open by default); Context; Connectors; Subagents (only while an agent of the session runs; a terminal session on this Mac shows no Subagents card until U8d gives that route its source, and with U8d shows it while this Mac drives the session through its bridge); Background tasks (only while a background task runs). A card with nothing to show is absent, not empty. Artifacts is an entry of the title bar's "More surfaces" menu, not a card. The three cards that fold remember it per device under the layout key `cards.open`. While a `/btw` side chat is open, U4's card sits at the column's foot through a slot U8 gives; when the column is not drawn U4 places the card over the pane's corner. Until U4's second landing registers the card in that slot, it lies over the pane's corner in every state of the column. A session with no record in this window has only the three conditional cards.

**UC-10 · Building and landing.** Binds all.
- **Base.** Every child branches from a master that contains the project registry's app branch (`registry-app`), which changes 54 renderer files including the sidebar, New chat and `sessions/actions.ts`. It landed on 2026-11-12 (master `1ed236bcb`, pull request #571); a child branches from that commit or a later one.
- **Where work happens.** A child is built in a git worktree on a branch `ux-wave/<child id>` (with a suffix where its section names several pull requests).
- **This MacBook.** No local typecheck, no multi-file eslint or vitest, and no local Electron on the owner's MacBook, at any load: one-file eslint and one-file vitest only. Typecheck, lint, the unit suite and the probes run on GitHub Actions through `scripts/ci-gate.sh <worktree>` (class 1a); Electron e2e groups run on a Mac under the load rule; a new or reworked e2e scene runs alone on a Mac before a final gate. An engine probe that needs a live engine runs off the MacBook: on another Mac, or, when it needs only the engine and its wire, on a cloud VM with the same engine version. An executor's own checks while it builds run on the wave's leased cloud VMs, in a checkout of the branch's pushed head: a milestone proves itself by the typecheck of each project it changed, eslint on its changed files, and the unit tests of the folders it touched and of those that import what it changed, and by the same set again after a merge of master; the whole unit suite runs once per landing, by the builder of the last milestone (ruled 2026-11-13); its worktree on the MacBook carries no installed dependencies (Decision Log, "Where the wave's checks run"). The proof of a merge of the chain's head adds the unit tests of `src/shared/machine` and of the repository's root `test/` folder, the latter in a call that passes its test files as an explicit list: neither `--dir test` nor a bare `test` argument keeps a run to that folder, and both run the renderer's suites and another package's tests and take 25 minutes or more. Class 1a on GitHub Actions stays the gate of record.
- **Paid live groups.** An e2e group that runs a real Claude engine on the owner's account (`p1-live`; its script caps its spend) runs once per landing, on that landing's final head, when the landing changed that group's scenes or what the group proves (the owner's answer of 2026-11-13, "Once per landing"). A second paid run of the same landing is a new question to the owner, unless the first failed for a harness reason that is named.
- **The final Mac runs.** A landing's final Mac run set is every scene group of its first full set, run on the final code. A group's earlier run stands only when the diff from the head it ran on to the final head touches no file that group reads, and the pull request says so with that diff's stat (ruled 2026-11-13 by the session that keeps master). After a merge of master into the branch the test is concrete (the same session, the same day). With I the merge's incoming change and B the branch's own change against master, a run made on the head before the merge stands only when all three hold: (a) I and B share no file; (b) I touches nothing under `e2e/` or `test/support/` and no build or configuration file (`package.json`, the lockfile, the electron-vite, vitest and playwright configurations, `scripts/e2e-*`; a change under `.github/` alone does not count, since it changes where a group runs and not what it tests); (c) no directory two levels under `src/` holds changes from both. When (c) fails, the groups whose scenes exercise that area run again, and `default` when the area is the renderer or main; when (a) or (b) fails, the whole set runs again. The pull request states I's stat, the three answers and the groups run again. Because the first landing after this rule changed scene files, the wave's branches land as a chain: each holds the one before it, runs its whole set once on that tree, and its pull request opens when its predecessor is on master. Ruled the same day by the same session: every link of the chain runs `e2e:default` on its merged head, whatever the three tests say (a merge that brings only files under `.github/`, or only documents, needs no new run); a later merge of master into that head is judged by the three tests and the readings below.
- **Readings of that test.** Each was accepted by the session that keeps master and is shown in the pull request, the first three with the search or the diff they name; class 1a runs again in every case. *Spec-only* (2026-11-13): an incoming change confined to `e2e/*.spec.ts` files, with no support, fixture or configuration file, counts only for the groups whose script lists one of those files, and for `e2e:default` when default selects one, provided no other file imports a changed spec file; a fix that also touches product code is judged by (c) as written. *Harness-page* (2026-11-13): a change confined to one harness page's own files, in an incoming merge or in the landing's own fix, counts only for the groups whose spec files open that page's route, provided the harness entry is the only importer of the changed files, the plain build holds no harness entry, and a changed file imports nothing new, or only a file already in that page's import graph; a new import of a product module from outside the graph makes it a product change, judged by (a) to (c). *Test-file* (2026-11-13): an incoming change confined to unit test files under `src/` (nothing under `e2e/` or `test/support/`, no configuration and no test setup file) counts for class 1a and `unit:darwin` only, also when the branch changed the same test file, provided nothing outside test files imports a changed file; no Electron group runs again for it. *Late fix* (2026-11-14, accepted for the destinations landing, U7b, pull request #652): after that landing's whole set had run, a fix changed one product file, the Settings dialog's, and its test; class 1a, `unit:darwin`, `e2e:default` and the groups whose scenes open Settings ran again on the fixed head, and the other groups' runs stood on the head they had run on. The same reading had been accepted earlier that day for U9 (pull request #650): after its whole set had run, a fix changed one renderer file, the archived sessions sheet's, and its test; the specs that open an archived sheet belong to the group `remote` and to `default`, so class 1a, `unit:darwin`, `e2e:default` and `e2e:remote` ran again on the fixed head, and the other groups' runs stood on the heads they had run on.
- **Cross-build runs** (the rule as the session that keeps master restated it on 2026-11-16). A landing owes two cross-build runs, one with each of the two older builds as the exact peer (build F, `f1852de3a`, and `2c7044231`), in three cases: it takes a wire minor or changes a stored form; it newly sends an entry on the wire between the app and its host; or it changes a form it sends that an older window parses, or a form stored in the engine's transcript. A wire manifest that did not change does not lift the duty in the second and third cases. A landing that takes only a link minor owes none. UC-14 holds what each run must show for a stored key.
- **Scenes, and the Mac they run on** (ruled 2026-11-13 by the session that keeps master; binding for every landing from then on). (R1) A landing whose diff adds or edits an e2e spec, or a file only specs import (`e2e/support`), runs each group that lists that spec once on the project's own Mac runner as well as where the group usually runs: in that runner's macOS guest by default, on the runner itself (`--lane no-hosted`) when the guest cannot take the group. It is one run, on the final head or on a head the rules above let stand, and the pull request names it; a group that already runs there needs nothing more. R1 covers the Electron groups whose usual place is one of GitHub's hosted Macs; the cross-build group and the groups that run on the pool are outside it. Since pull request #646 (2026-11-14) the gate adds these runs itself, bounded by the run's own e2e groups, and a call that lets an earlier head's run stand gives its reason. (R2) A scene settles on a state the app shows, never on two reads that agree, a fixed sleep, or a read made straight after a resize or a change of layout. (R3) On such a run, a failure whose signature matches an existing tracker row, on a scene the branch does not touch, is named in the pull request with the row and run again once; a second red in a row, or a new signature, stops the link. The evidence is #639 (2026-11-13), the repair of two scenes that ran green in their own groups and red in a landing's `e2e:default`: a control run of the two groups on the project's own runner (run 43507479208) passed the terminal-session scene and failed the Settings scene, which passes on GitHub's hosted Macs, so a scene's result can follow the machine it runs on. Both causes were accepted: the Settings scene read a pane's width before the split had followed a resize, and the terminal-session scene settled by time before the Environment card's pull-request row was read.
- **Lanes** (learned on the first chain, 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14). `unit:darwin`, the voice group and a cross-build run with a peer run on GitHub's hosted Macs; the cross-build group has no run on the project's own runner. `--lane vm` sends to the runner's guest only the groups on the guest's list, and a group off that list goes to a hosted Mac under it, so that group's R1 run takes `--lane no-hosted`, queued behind the landing's `default`. A `default` launched alone names with `--default-beside` the other groups of its set, so that it leaves out their specs as it would beside them. A live group and a measured group run on the project's runner itself and do not start while its guest runs tests, so while such a job is queued nothing else that runner can take is launched.
- **Learned on the first landings.** A landing that rewords a row sweeps every spec file for the old words, not only the scenes it meant to change: a text locator can now match a second element, and a scene may name a tool by a word the row no longer shows (U2a, 2026-11-14: two scenes were red on a Mac at its reviewed head, which its own sweep had missed, and a third was found by reading). Two more are stated where their contracts stand: how a ruling is worded (UC-11), and how an insertion into a frozen tool text is read (UC-15).
- **Review.** Each child's execution document gets an adversarial review before its executor starts. Each branch gets `doperpowers:review-code` before it lands: the panel (xhigh) for a branch of panel size or a delicate change, a single reviewer otherwise. The session that keeps master runs the xhigh panel for U6a and U9; the wave's architect runs it for U13a, U3a, U8b and U10a. Each pull request's body carries the verifier's verdict and what was dismissed or deferred. Delicate by this document: UC-4's second landing (both of its pull requests, U8c-1 and U8c-2), U10's early pull request, U6's route that serves a session's file to a frame (U6b-2) and the landing of its Word preview (a new privileged scheme, a proxy on the window's session, and a frame that parses files nobody vetted; l6-artifacts-01), U13's reader of the stored layout, U9's offers, U11's default writer, which writes a file every terminal session reads.
- **Landing.** Nothing lands on master, and no build of the wave reaches the owner's machines, without the owner's word; the machines stay on build F until P5's verdict. Between U4a and U4b, "Send now" on a session of another machine is refused with a sentence, so no build is offered to the owner's machines between those two landings without saying so. A child's pull request is reviewed and landed by the session that keeps master (the P5 architect at this writing), by its gated merge: a merge commit built with `git merge-tree`, class 1a green on that exact tree, master unmoved, then the push.
- **The record.** A child's pull request carries the edits to the documents its items amend: each reversed ruling amended where it stands, dated, naming the owner's words (Decision Log, "Rulings reversed"), and each contract amended by a dated line (UC-15). At landing the register's rows are updated, and the board tickets and tracker rows the child closes are closed through the board scripts.

**UC-11 · The repository is public.** Binds all. No screenshot of the owner's, no text of the owner's sessions, no account name and no email address enters the repository, a pull request or a ticket. The comparison's screenshots, the grounding files, the owner's layout files and the vendor's sources stay outside it. A drawn example shown to the owner uses invented labels, paths and text. A ruling's text is copied into the repository, so it holds no fact about the owner's machines (2026-11-14: a rejected alternative in U7b's document stated such a fact, taken from the ruling's own message, and was reworded before its pull request).

**UC-12 · The destination and the Settings dialog.** Owner U7; binds U1, U6, U8, U10, U11, U12, U13 (U1 and U12 by U7b's insertions in `tools/maws/ask/connect.ts` and `tools/browser/agent/connect.ts`). Customize, Projects and the Artifacts library are *destinations*: pages that take everything right of the sidebar, the side column's place included, and are not tabs. Settings is not a tab either: it is a dialog over the window, over the tabs or over an open destination (the owner's answer of 2026-11-12, "As Desktop really does"). The layout store holds one `Destination` with `openDestination` and `closeDestination`, and, beside it, the dialog's state with `openSettings(section?)` and `closeSettings`. The strip holds neither: opening or closing one opens, closes, activates and reorders no tab. Any act of the person's that brings a tab to the front closes both, through the store's one commit function (U13's file names it). Those acts include the keyboard move to the other pane (`pane.focusOther`) and opening, closing, floating, docking and focusing the split card. Focus that only follows where the keyboard or a press already is does not count. Neither does what an agent or a mod asks for: an agent's `show_pane`, the Browser following an agent, MAWS's ask sheet and a mod's pane close neither, and what they bring forward is in front behind the destination or the dialog; wherever such an open is deferred between the request and the commit, who asked travels as data with the request (`asked: 'agent'` on the panel host's `addTool` and `selectTab`) and is applied at the commit. An act on the side column (a side-surface key, the Session cards control, markup) leaves a destination first. Opening a destination closes the dialog; ⌘W closes the dialog when it is open, otherwise the destination, and never the tab behind. Four tab kinds retire, and a stored tab of one is dropped at read. While a destination is open the card column and the side column are not drawn, and nothing of the side column's state is written: Back returns the tabs and the side column as they were. What a destination hides stays mounted, and behind it nothing counts as on screen (U7's file names the readers that answer so, with their owners); behind the dialog everything stays drawn, dimmed, and the session there still counts as on screen. A control on a destination can leave it for a given tab (a new chat, a new artifact), and the Library keeps its narrowed view for the return. The open destination is stored under a layout key of its own and restored after a relaunch, because the relaunch that restores everything is the owner's `keep` (l7-shell-12); the dialog's state is held for the run only, as whether the Overview is open is. At a start, a retired Customize, Projects or Artifacts tab that was in front opens its page; a retired Settings tab in front opens none, and the stored destination is not restored over it; otherwise the stored destination is restored only while the stored tabs have the one-set shape this build writes. That is a test of shape, not of who wrote the value: after a stay on build F that left such a layout with no retired tab in front, the destination opens again and the person leaves it with Back, which is accepted, and no marker is stored for it. U6's Back and Forward take a destination as a place and the dialog as none; U11 fills Settings and switches Customize's view and filter through `openDestination`: after the owner's answer on Customize's structure (O-14), the `customize` member of `Destination` is `{ kind: 'customize'; view: 'mine' | 'discover'; show: 'all' | 'plugins' | 'skills' | 'connectors' }`, and `openDestination` with the same kind changes these two fields.

**UC-13 · The to-human advertisement.** Owner U2; binds U1 (for O-19). MAWS tells the engine's mods what it draws through the launch setting `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN`, whose value is `TO_HUMAN_CONTRACT_VERSION` in `src/shared/owner/contract.ts`: 8 today. U2's second landing (U2b) raises it to 9 in the same change that removes MAWS's own drawing of the report for sessions it runs, and never before (after U2a every launch still advertises 8); from then a launch advertises 9 only when MAWS has decided that the mod draws that session's view, and 8 when MAWS will draw it itself. The mod's view (doperpowers `to-human`) draws on MAWS's surfaces when the value is 9 or more and steps aside for any other non-empty value, so build F, which advertises 8 and still draws its own report, is not drawn twice whenever the mod's change is merged. The mod's other readers do not change: the message tool registers at 8 and up; the notifier, the streaming hook and the sminos ask mod stay out for any non-empty value. MAWS decides at each launch who draws the view, from a declaration the mod ships in its installation (a file that says it draws when the client advertises 9; never a version number, and no constant in MAWS names a release of the mod), and publishes it as `drawnBy: 'mod' | 'maws'`: the mod when the launch advertised 9 and the loaded mod carries the declaration, MAWS everywhere else (a terminal session, a session on another machine, a session at rest, a session under a mod without the declaration). A launch advertises 9 only once that advertisement is durably recorded for it, and 8 when it cannot be; the recorded advertisement of a running launch is never replaced by that of a later launch the host refused (restated 2026-11-16; U2's O-13); an advertisement that cannot be known is never read as "MAWS draws" for a live launch. A launch whose lane is off advertises nothing, and where the plugin is still on the mod then draws as it does in a terminal (ruled 2026-11-15 on U2b's review; U2's O-13). Because the number follows that decision, exactly one side draws in every combination of MAWS and mod versions, including a mod release that cannot hide a run, which ships no declaration; who draws is decided again after every plugin reload, from the launch's number and the declaration of the plugin then loaded. So no MAWS work waits for the mod's merge. A later change of the ask mod's row (O-19) takes the next number of the same sequence.

**UC-14 · What the wave stores, build F can still read.** Owner U13; binds U4, U5, U6, U7, U8, U11, U12. Going back to build F after a trial of the wave's build is expected (UC-10), and F reads stored layout strictly: an unknown version makes it open default tabs and overwrite the file. So `main.tabs` keeps version 3 and its envelope, always written with one set under the key `none`; a stored value with several sets is merged into one strip on every read (U13's file gives the function). The same holds for every stored value F knows: F drops a tab of a kind it does not know and keeps a top-level key it does not know, but a value of a known kind or key that breaks F's schema fails the whole read (one such tab costs the saved strip; an unknown version of `regions` costs the sidebar's, the panel's and the Overview's stored layout). So, while F may run on the same profile: no field is added to a stored tab of an existing kind, to `main.tabs`, to `regions` or to the stored side surfaces, and no version of them moves; no new value is written where F's reader has a closed set (U11's new Settings section is never stored while Settings is still a tab: the list of sections a tab may store stays F's ten); a new tab kind is fine; new state gets a layout key of its own (`cards.open`, `panelTools.widths`, `panelTools.groups`, `sidebar.hidden`, `destination`, `catalog.favicons`, `library.exportFolder`, U6's open files); a child that retires a tab kind adds it to the reader's retired list. The two settings files are stricter than the layout: F's reader refuses a key it does not know, an action id it does not know in `keymap`, and any other version, and then shows defaults for every client setting and refuses to write them (it does not overwrite the file). So in this wave neither settings file changes its schema or its version; law 3 already adds no setting, and a switch that an answer of the owner's adds is stored under a layout key of its own. One switch is kept elsewhere, accepted on 2026-11-15 for U2b's second pull request (in build): the switch for the fold-line summaries, which the owner asked for and for which law 3 is amended, is one key of the index's own facts, because the daemon reads it at every launch and no service reads the layout file. No column is added for it; build F's schema has no such table and F reads none of it, and the other older build reads that table by key and never this one (U2's O-30). A rebinding of an action F does not know is not stored: such a row is not rebindable until F is no longer a build to go back to (U6's file, "Stored rebindings"). Two rules siblings code against: only a tab holds a session open (a group's membership holds nothing; ruled 2026-11-15 by the session that keeps master, for ticket 686, the side thread of a session on another machine: when that landing is on master the rule reads "a surface that shows a session holds it open", so that the Side pane may hold such a session, within four bounds: one thread; the same function as a tab; released on close, on a switch and on the window's close, with tests; and held once when a session is shown in both. Until then the rule stands as written); and showing a tab shows the tabs, through one commit function in `stores/layout.ts`. Proof before the wave's build runs on a profile the owner uses: a cross-build e2e run with F as the peer, covering every key the wave writes and not `main.tabs` alone, and F's read of both settings files after the wave's build has written them, and a rehearsal of the new reader over a copy of each of the owner's machines' layout files, kept outside the repository. The cross-build proof has a coverage table, kept by the landings and begun in the execution document of U13's first landing: each landing that stores a new key, or relies on build F reading a key it writes, adds its row and its scene. The first landing's rows are `main.tabs` (the tab ids and the pins), `panelTools.surfaces` at a value that is not the default, `regions`, and both settings files, by a scene that changes one setting in each file through Settings, has F read and write them, and reads them back. A scene that was skipped is not evidence. The run of record runs the cross-build group with build F as the exact peer (`--peer-exact f1852de3a`, a flag of the gate since pull request #614), on the landing's final head or on the last head that changed anything build F reads, and the landing's pull request names it. A landing that owes this run also owes one with the other older build as the exact peer (UC-10, Cross-build runs). The rehearsal is the test `test/layout/owner-files.test.ts`, run with `MAWS_LAYOUT_REHEARSAL=<dir>`; it prints generated labels, numbers, booleans and one word from a closed list, and never a value read from a file. U8's second landing adds the rows of its three new keys, `cards.open`, `panelTools.widths` and `panelTools.groups`, with `panelTools.activeNoSession` and the other values of the side column it reinterprets. U7's first landing adds the row of `sidebar.hidden`, and its second the rows of `destination` and of the four retired tab kinds, each with a scene of its own; the second also gives the rehearsal an expectation of the page a file's own front calls for, computed apart from the reader, and the outcome `destination-mismatch`. U11's landing of the connector icons adds the row of `catalog.favicons`, the switch "Connector icons": set off through Settings on the wave's build, F runs and writes its layout, and the wave's build reads off. U11's landing of the connector switches adds the row of `machine-connectors.json`, a file of the machine's beside its settings files, and of the place lists it drives: the newer build switches a connector off for a project; build F, on the same profile, shows it off in its Connectors dialog and switches it on; the newer build then starts a chat there, leaves it on and sends nothing, and keeps leaving it alone until the next press on that connector in MAWS. U11's landing of the old list's move adds the row of `engine.mcpServers` after a move by press: F no longer lists the moved server in Settings and loads it from Claude Code's configuration. Neither settings file changes for either. U4's first landing adds the row of a take-back's stored copy, the one stored value of the composer's work that build F can meet: for the seconds a take-back lasts the session's stored draft holds the message with one attribute, `takeBack`; build F's composer opens it as an ordinary draft, and a draft store of build F refuses the two saves that make and settle it, so nothing is staged there. Its two scenes: a stored copy whose message still waits, and one whose message the engine has taken. U5's first landing adds the row of `library.exportFolder`, the remembered export folder, with its scene; the table is begun by U13a, so that row goes in with whichever of U13a and U5a lands second. U8's live working folder (U8c-2) adds two nullable columns to the index database and two optional keys to the session's record, which build F neither shows nor changes; its guarantee for a window of build F attached to a host of this build is the narrower one its execution document writes service by service (its §C): for a session whose agent moved, that window can name the original checkout while it lists the worktree's changes and files, nothing can be changed from it, and the pairing ends when that window is updated. What a stay in F leaves of the side column: tabs and pins are kept; one tab per tool comes back, because F never removes a tool's tab; and the arrangement in two groups is not kept.

**UC-15 · The `maws` tool texts are frozen, and the instructions have a budget.** Owner: the wave's architect; binds U5, U6, U10, U12. Charter X9 freezes the `maws` tool server's tool names, descriptions and result shapes; each changes only by a dated line in the charter's X9 record, written in the pull request that makes the change, and `src/shared/maws/tools.test.ts` pins the list by name and order. Known changes: U10 (the tool `artifact_delete`; `path` on `artifact_publish`; the op `set_page`; `designSystem` on `artifact_create`; `space` on `artifact_describe_type`; export formats; Space instructions in three answers), U12 (`show_pane`'s description, result and one refusal; a paragraph of instructions on the in-app browser), U5 (`reply` on `artifact_edit`), U6 (one sentence of instructions on file links; and, by O-44, `show_pane` without the `artifact` pane and `at`, one refusal added, and the invitation to call it gone from the instructions, which are 635 characters after it, from `artifact_create`'s description and from the kinds' guides), board ticket 711 (one refusal added to `ask`, for an ask that quotes the opening of the engine's consent sentence: "An ask may not quote a permission grant. Ask the question in your own words."). The server's instructions (`MAWS_SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS`, 699 characters today) are read by the model in every session MAWS runs. The engine cuts such a text at 4,096 characters by default (`cli.pretty.js`, the default of `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_MCP_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH`, applied to a server's instructions as to a tool's description). The wave keeps the total under 2,048, half of that, as its own margin; each child states the characters it adds, and the architect keeps the sum. An insertion into a frozen text is read as the whole sentence it produces before it is frozen: for `path` on `artifact_publish`, a sentence to be inserted "directly after" a phrase left, read literally, no stop after that phrase and a `.;` before the next clause, and it was ruled in as a clause ending in a semicolon, with no word changed (U10b-1, 2026-11-15).

## Ordering and dependency map

Nothing is dispatched before two things hold: `registry-app` is on master (UC-10; it landed on 2026-11-12 as `1ed236bcb`), and the owner has given the word to build (given on 2026-11-12, about 21:55Z: "Approve and start"). A child's pull requests are named with a letter where it has several.

It rewires readers in files other children own (Review, Source control, Files, the terminal, the composer, the transcript's slot), which is why it landed before they started; U5 and U6b code against it.
