**The order to master.** From 2026-11-13 the landings reach master as a chain: each branch holds the one before it, and each runs its whole Mac set once, on its merged head (Decision Log, 2026-11-13). The first chain is on master whole; its last link was U2a, on 2026-11-14. U6d, U11g and U11c followed it one by one. The second chain is in build, in this order: U10b-1, U6b-1, U10b-4, U10b-2, U6e, U8d, U2b's first pull request, U5b-1, U1b, U4b, U1c, U3b, U11h, U6b-2, the split of a joined answer (board ticket 704) and U5b-2. The first fourteen are merged, each on the one before it; the last two join in that order. U2b's second pull request is built on U3b's head. The fix of the answer envelope's form (board ticket 711, pull request #717) is cut from master and lands before the chain, whose landings then merge it. The Tracking map holds each one's state.

**U14** needs about forty-five minutes of the owner's time for four checks, and an agent on the mini for the rest; some checks need the wave's build of the child that adds their log lines or their fix. Its findings feed U7 (S-03, S-08), U13 (l6-artifacts-13), U3 and U9 (O-33, O-35, O-39, #429), U5 (O-46), U10 (l6-artifacts-09, l6-artifacts-04), U11 (#476, S-09), U6 (l5-code-07, O-37), U12 (Stop during a computer-use call) and U1 (l1-sessions-08).

**Engine probes** (law 6) come first inside their children: Send now and the move to the background (U4, eight cases); what the engine reports when the agent changes folder (U8); a classifier block on MAWS's own wire (U1); the live forms of a task notification, a scheduled trigger, a bridge delivery and a command's output (U3, four); a `memory_recall` frame and what the mod answers on MAWS's surface at 8 and at 9 (U2; the mod's answers are U2b's first step, and U2a's execution document asks a later pass for four recordings, each kept as journal and as transcript file, on which nothing waits: a Stop during a run with calls in flight, a denied call inside a run, a permission card's "Deny and stop the turn", and a classifier block); a dynamic server's sign-in (U11, on the mini); the browser probes (U12; its Drive check is withdrawn with the Drive work, which the owner deferred on 2026-11-12). On 2026-11-12 the probes that need only the engine and its wire (U4's, U1's, U3's first, second and fourth, U8's, U2's checks 2 and 9) were started ahead of dispatch on a leased cloud VM (Decision Log); their findings are written back into the children before those are dispatched. Their fixtures and findings are on master since 2026-11-13, as one pull request (#602); the branches the probes were made on are gone from the remote, and U3a and U8b read the files from master. On 2026-11-13 a second pass on a cloud VM ran three more checks: whether a terminal session takes the comment text (U5's P7), and U11's two, the default model and mode from the user settings file and what Google's favicon address answers. It also ran the cases of Send now that the first pass did not run (U4). Its findings and fixtures landed on master as pull request #606 (master `5c9cfa0c5`), and the three checks are written into those two children's files. A third pass, on 2026-11-13, ran the two cases of Send now still owed (U4, eleven runs: a turn's ordinary end with several messages waiting at the next step, and Send now on an after-the-turn row beside a message at the next step). Its findings are a dated section of `findings-uxwave-u4-send-now-cases.txt`, with the fixtures `u4p3-*`, on master with pull request #609, and U4's file holds the results of all three passes. Two facts of that pass for whoever codes against its files. The first pass's Send-now fixtures were recorded without `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS=1`, which MAWS sets, so they hold no session-state frame; the fixtures to code against are the second pass's (`u4p2-a-1` to `-3` for the plain case). And in the second pass's files a result file that is not an engine journal has the extension `.ndjson`, because every `.jsonl` under `fixtures/uxwave/` is read as a journal by two unit tests. U11a's execution document names four probes of its own. P1, a dynamic connector's sign-in through MAWS's redirect, ran in a gate job on the Mac mini runner on 2026-11-13 (engine `2.1.322`, runs 43357272365 and 43358963647): a server of MAWS's own list and a plugin's server both sign in through MAWS's redirect (`DYNAMIC_REDIRECT = { plugin: 'maws', own: 'maws' }`), so the sign-in is built as designed for both kinds; its findings are on branch `ux-wave/U11a` and reach master with that landing. P2 (where the engine records a switched-off connector for a session in a linked worktree) and P3 (a skill's name on disk against the engine's name for it) were run on the wave's probe VM on 2026-11-13 (on master since pull request #609, the third probe pass), and their results are in U11's section: a switched-off connector is kept under the repository's main root, and a plugin's skill runs under its manifest's name. P4 (the 45 seconds of "Reading the channel…") needs a Mac other than the owner's MacBook. After 2026-11-13: three probe passes on the connector switches by scope and a fourth on a plugin's connectors (U11; engine `2.1.322`) are on master, the fourth as pull request #666 with its findings in `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u11-plugin-connectors.txt`; so is the containment spike of the viewer frame for U6's preview of Office files (pull request #664). The second landings' own probes are done and are on branches of their own, not on master: `ux-wave/U1b-probes`, `ux-wave/U1c-probes`, `ux-wave/U3b-probes`, `ux-wave/U4b-probes` and `ux-wave/U8d-probes`. Two more sets of recordings are on branches and not on master: board ticket 704's probes of the joined answer, on its branch (thirteen recordings, each a journal and the engine's transcript file whole, with `findings-uxwave-u704-envelope-split.txt`), and board ticket 711's probe of the answer envelope and the auto mode classifier, on `ux-wave/711-envelope-probe` (`findings-uxwave-711-envelope-consent.txt`).

On 2026-11-16 two more for U2b, on the switch for the fold-line summaries, each with a drawn picture and each answered the same day. No choice is before the owner now.

How many children run at once is decided at dispatch, against the shared GitHub Actions budget and the review load.

## Risks and mitigations

- **A fix that is not a clear improvement.** Mitigation: Parent-level acceptance 2; each child's section names what the owner will see; the looks listed above come before the landing they concern.
- **Three children in the transcript's model.** Mitigation: UC-3's order, ownership and rules.
- **The registry branch moves the base.** Mitigation: UC-10's base rule; the sidebar, tabs and composer sections were written against that branch's diff and are re-read at dispatch. The branch landed with the product code those sections read: between the head they were written against (`de7870388`) and the landed master, `src/` and `e2e/` differ only in e2e fixture code.
- **An engine behaviour assumed, not seen.** Mitigation: law 6 and the probes; an item whose probe fails falls back as its section says.
- **Reversing earlier rulings silently.** Mitigation: the Decision Log's table and Parent-level acceptance 4.
- **A trial of the wave's build damages what build F reads.** Mitigation: UC-14, and Parent-level acceptance 6.
- **A section rests on a reading of Claude Desktop that is wrong.** It happened twice in the cards put to the owner (Decision Log, "Corrections"). Mitigation: a claim about Claude Desktop that an owner-facing question rests on is re-read in the source by the architect before the question is sent; each section cites file and line.
- **Review and CI load from thirteen children and more than twenty pull requests.** Mitigation: staged dispatch; the class 1a gates run off this MacBook; small early pull requests (U6a, U8a) are single-reviewer size.
- **The wire registry's declaration nears the compiler's limit.** The TypeScript compiler refuses to write out an inferred declaration longer than about a million characters (TS7056), and each landing that adds a command grows the registry's largest part. Measured on 2026-11-14 on two landings' trees: the commands part at about 835,000 characters, a margin of about 16 per cent, growing by about a thousand per landing; written as one inferred type, the whole registry would already be past the limit. Mitigation: master names the registry's parts one by one; a landing that trips the limit takes that piecewise form further and never suppresses the error; the tracker holds the row, owned by the wave until it ends and by the P5 work after, with the candidate fix (a registry type written out by the wire manifest's generator).
