## Surprises and discoveries

From the grounding and from the writing of the sections (2026-11-12); each is cited in the item it changed.

- Observation: four items about requests that keep "waiting" after they were answered have one cause on the wire route, and a different one for a terminal session.
  Evidence: `src/shared/attention/kinds.ts` `isOpen` is `pending || delivered`; `src/engine-host/broker.ts` `confirm()` leaves `delivered` only at the first frame that names the tool use; charter §4.6 says delivered is not an alarm state. A terminal session's item never becomes `delivered`: it stays `pending` until the bridge reports it settled (U1's file).
- Observation: the to-human mod draws nothing inside MAWS, the change the owner ruled on 2026-10-24 was never merged, and merging it as written would draw the report twice on build F.
  Evidence: the mod's render hooks return early when `MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN` is set (`src/shared/owner/contract.ts` advertises 8); doperpowers branch `to-human-draw-in-maws` (`d17d9b73`, 2026-10-23) is not an ancestor of its `main` and removes the deferral outright; F's report bar and report rows consult no mod. Hence UC-13's gate at 9.
- Observation: thinking reads out of order because of the fix of 2026-10-23 that flattened tool groups.
  Evidence: commit `5ab559bcd`; `src/renderer/src/transcript/view/rows/Group.tsx` draws a group's thinking after all its calls under "Internal activity".
- Observation: clicking a project does not start a session; the title bar's project chip does.
  Evidence: `src/renderer/src/app/Toolbar.tsx`, the chip's click calls `createSession(project.id)`; a sidebar project row only selects and expands.
- Observation: Claude Desktop opens Settings as a dialog over the window, and Customize as a page; the first card put to the owner said both were pages.
  Evidence: `app-5.1231.2/web/app/v1/index-m4Lmn-QT.js`, the settings route's component (page, blank host or redirect; about lines 22700 to 22815) and the settings store's `openFromRoute` (22500 to 22541): the route redirects to the host page with `#settings/<section>` and opens the modal; the full page is the fallback.
- Observation: Claude Desktop draws a question in its call's row, and a permission prompt at the transcript's end, only behind a feature gate; its default is a dock above a composer that stays usable.
  Evidence: U1's file, under l4-approvals-08 (the gate `claudeai_cc_epitaxy_session_presets_main`). The owner's answer stands: it is the owner's own mark.
- Observation: in auto mode nothing a host can do grants one exact shell command once.
  Evidence: `~/claude-code-bundle/2.1.322/cli.pretty.js:478076-478081`: a hook's allow of a shell command is sent back to the classifier; the classifier's prompt treats a user's go-ahead after a block as consent to that action (`:450631`). Hence "Approve and retry" as a message (U1, l4-approvals-12).
- Observation: "Send now" stops a running command only because MAWS sends a plain interrupt; the engine can move the command to the background instead. Claude Desktop makes that move itself in two forms, by a timer and at Send now, and both are behind one server switch that reads off when it has not been fetched.
  Evidence: `cli.pretty.js` sends `interrupt` with `send_now: true` and a `message_uuid`; `src/renderer/src/composer/queue/QueueList.tsx` calls `controls.interrupt({cancelEngineQueued: false})`; Desktop's switch `steerDetachOn`, tested at `main-process/index.chunk-BjORVu2_.js:46951` and `:47072`, set from the gate at `web/app/v1/c8a3f7430-DPEJjikp.js:10-14`.
- Observation: once the agent moves into a worktree, every code surface keeps reading the launch folder, although parts of MAWS already follow the move.
  Evidence: 21 lines in 20 files outside tests read `record.worktree?.path ?? record.directory`. The index re-files a session on a `relocated` transcript row and the record's branch follows the newest row, but the window's surfaces do not. What the engine records when the agent enters a worktree is U8's probe; its `vcs_state_changed` frame is no such signal (it fires for a commit, a push, a merge or a rebase, with that command's folder).
- Observation: a failed background task raises an error card because every failed task notification becomes an error item, and its Dismiss is dead for a session on another machine.
  Evidence: `src/services/attention/service.ts`, the task-notification branch; `attention.acknowledge` has no offer.
- Observation: no comment can be sent today for a session on another machine, and "Send to main" is refused for a terminal session.
  Evidence: the message is built in main (`src/services/side/content.ts`, `send-to-main.ts`), and `side.sendToMain` and `side.thread.open` are both in `REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE`.
- Observation: a link to a file outside the session's own folders is refused on this Mac too, not only on another machine.
  Evidence: the files service admits only the folders in the session's record (`src/services/files/allowlist.ts`); P5 Decision Log 237 is narrower than the cut's line said.
- Observation: the stored tab layout is version 3, and build F discards a layout whose version it does not know.
  Evidence: U13's file, "As stored" and "As read". Hence UC-14.
- Observation: who sent a row can be proved only from the raw frame.
  Evidence: X1 lowers the origin object to one word (U3's file, UC-2). The file route draws a peer's delivery as the owner's bubble for two separate causes.
- Observation (withdrawn 2026-11-12): "a `Thought for Ns` duration exists only for a thinking block MAWS saw streaming." It is true of MAWS's code and false of the engine: see the observation below on the engine's recorded duration.
  Evidence as it was read: MAWS's `blockRow` computes the duration from the block's start and end events, and so has none for a terminal session, a session on another machine, or history read from disk.
- Observation: Claude Desktop's find in a session is the transcript's own search, which loads earlier history and opens folded rows; it is not the browser's find.
  Evidence: U3's file, l2-transcript-08.
- Observation: a fork made over the machine link cannot name its starter as owner.
  Evidence: the proof that makes a starter the owner is keyed on the action name `session.spawn` in four places (U9's file, UC-7.4). Hence fork for the machine's owners only in this wave.
- Observation: signing in a connector a plugin brings is larger than it read, and has never been seen to complete.
  Evidence: the schema refuses a plugin connector's name and has no `dynamic` scope (U11's file, l8-settings-01); a probe on the mini comes first. (It ran on 2026-11-13: a plugin's server and a server of MAWS's own list each completed the sign-in through MAWS's redirect.)
- Observation: MAWS has no spreadsheet export, so nothing can be converted to Google Sheets.
  Evidence: U12's file, l9-browser-delivery-09; the cut's line named Excel.
- Observation: in a session MAWS runs every thought has text; the grounding and the first version of U2's section held that almost none does.
  Evidence: MAWS's journals on the owner's MacBook, 2026-10-31 to 11-12: 85 of 85 thinking blocks carry text (engines `2.1.316` and `2.1.322`); 129 of 129 carry none on 10-19 to 10-21 (engine `2.1.303`). The cause is `84ada7285` of 2026-10-24: the engine host asks for thinking summaries at session start (`src/engine-host/session.ts`, `thinking_display: 'summarized'`). Terminal sessions and the probe harness do not ask, so their thoughts have no text (94% over sixty session files), which is what the grounding read. Found by U2's check 6, run before dispatch.
- Observation: the engine records every thought's duration, on the wire and in the transcript file, and MAWS reads neither; the grounding held that a terminal session's thought has no known duration.
  Evidence: `thinking_duration_ms` on all 85 `assistant` frames that hold a thinking block in MAWS's journals (engines `2.1.316`, `2.1.322`), equal to the block's start-to-end time within 0.31 s; `thinkingDurationMs` on every thinking row of the four hundred most recent session files from engine `2.1.287` on (none before `2.1.284`); no occurrence of either name under `src/` at `03cee6582`. U2's rule for thoughts now reads it; tracker row 631 (a thought's start time in a terminal session) stays open only for the label that ticks while a thought streams.
- Observation: when the agent enters a worktree through the engine's tool, the engine moves the session's whole transcript file into the worktree's own project folder, and moves it back when the agent leaves.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-U8-working-folder.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (engine `2.1.322`, headless and terminal). MAWS's index re-filed the record correctly; whether its readers that follow the file by path keep reading was not tested and is U8's new check. This is today's behaviour on build F, not the wave's.
- Observation: after a `cd` into another worktree the engine's rows carry the new folder and still the launch checkout's branch; `gitBranch` is refreshed only by the worktree tool.
  Evidence: the same findings, steps s4 to s5b (every row `master` inside a worktree on another branch). MAWS's record takes its branch from the newest row today; UC-4 now takes it from git at the working folder.
- Observation: the engine recalls no memory on the owner's configuration, so the frame #351 would draw never arrives.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-U2-memory-recall.txt`, on master since pull request #602: no recall with the owner's user settings (automatic memory off) nor with memory on and the feature gate as the owner's sessions have it; five recalls only after the gate was turned on in a scratch copy of the cache. #351 is deferred.
- Observation: whether a thinking block has text is decided by MAWS's request alone, on every model the owner uses.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-U2-thinking-text.txt`, on master since pull request #602: eighteen launches on four models split exactly on the request (`summarized` with text, `updates` with none), on the wire and in the file.
- Observation: under MAWS's launch the engine started every subagent in the background (`async_launched`), and a subagent whose run ended while its own shell still ran was reported completed and started again under the same task id when the shell ended.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-U8-subagent-tasks.txt`, on master since pull request #602, three sessions. It bears on U4's probe case of a message waiting behind a running subagent call, which that probe answers, and on #359's row, which now keeps such an agent in the Running part.
- Observation: the engine's send-now never kills what the agent is running, but behind a shell command it ends the turn and runs the message as the next one; only a foreground subagent call is moved with the message read in the same turn.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u4-send-now.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (engine `2.1.322`, launched as MAWS launches it; five of five and four of four). U4's table, A3 and its reversal sentence said "in the same turn" for both and are corrected. A message at priority `now` behaves the same, so charter §4.2's sentence of 2026-09-28 is stale on this engine. Amended 2026-11-13: the four foreground runs were recorded without `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=true`, which MAWS's launch sets, and under it the case does not occur; and a message at priority `now` behaves the same only once the running command is a task (the Surprises of the second and third passes, below).
- Observation: a Stop after a Send now does not stop the command the engine moved; the engine does that only for sessions of its own chat product.
  Evidence: the same findings, case (h), two of two; `cli.pretty.js:600846-600870` behind a check of the session's origin. U4's sentence said the opposite.
- Observation: `background_tasks` answers `false` for a shell call until the engine has registered it as a task, three and a half to four and a half seconds after the call's frame.
  Evidence: the same findings, case (e): three of three false without a retry, three of three true with one. A three-second timer alone, as U4 first had it for the automatic move, fails on its first try.
- Observation: a call blocked by a rule that always denies and one blocked by a rule that can be overridden are identical on the wire and in the file; and through the gateway the classifier's reason is always the same stand-in sentence.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-u1-classifier-block.txt`, on master since pull request #602: fourteen blocks. The grant sentence got the retry through five of five under the second kind and none of three under the first. U1's row therefore cannot withhold its button for the first kind, and no longer shows the stand-in reason.
- Observation: in one session MAWS ran on 2026-11-12 the main loop sent every one of its 82 shell calls without a description, while its subagents' 34 all had one.
  Evidence: MAWS's journals (key names and counts only); the engine's two copies of each input agree, so nothing was dropped on the way. In terminal sessions 5.5% of shell calls have none. It is probably what the owner saw in O-49; U2's check 4 looks for what made the model leave it out.
- Observation: a subagent's thought is forwarded on the live wire with its text and without a duration; its own transcript file has the duration.
  Evidence: 50 of 50 forwarded thinking frames in MAWS's journals on `2.1.322`. U2's rule gives such a thought no line inside a run until the file is read.
- Observation: holding Yjs's clean-up flag off around each apply does not stop the clean-up for an apply that runs inside another transaction; three such applies exist in the product.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u10-format-cleanup.txt` and the experiment patch beside it, on master since pull request #602: with the hold, one accept and one restore each wrote two rows. U10 takes its fallback, the flag off for good (amended 2026-11-13: U10a cancels the clean-up with a listener and leaves the flag as Yjs sets it; the observation of that date below). The same check found the real trigger of the refused edit (a bold run cut so that part of its text survives) and observed, on the accept path, the replica echo that l6-artifacts-09's first finding describes.
- Observation: the diff library unmounts an open widget whenever it is given a new data object, even with identical hunks; today's Review builds one on every re-read.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-u5-diffview.txt`, on master since pull request #602, in the app's pinned Electron. U5 keeps each file's data object while its text is unchanged and holds a commented file's diff still while a card is open in it. The library also exports a view with range selection, so it is not the reason range comments are left out.
- Observation: the engine sends the `user` frame of a task notification only when the model takes it in during a running turn; told while idle, which is the common case, the wire carries no such frame.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u3-tasks.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (engine `2.1.322`, launched as MAWS launches it): 10 of 10 during a turn, 0 of 14 while idle; without `--replay-user-messages`, none at all. The earlier `system/task_notification` frame carries the engine's sentence and a run id that the told turn's `result` names. U3's O-28 reads the idle line from it. The section had refused that frame on a `2.1.283` fixture in which it held only the description.
- Observation: the engine registers every foreground shell call of about three seconds or more as a task and sends a task notification when it ends; MAWS raises "Background task failed" for any failed one.
  Evidence: the same findings (four of four commands of five seconds); 46 of the 51 such frames in MAWS's journals belong to foreground calls; `src/services/attention/service.ts`, the `task.notification` branch. A defect of build F; probably much of the owner's O-34.
- Observation: a finished background agent arrives twice, as a hand-back message and as a task notification; when the session is idle the notification is written to the file only and the engine reports an empty turn for it.
  Evidence: the same findings; the audit's count in terminal sessions (about 976 hand-backs beside 1,434 completed-agent notifications). U3 draws one line (the report), and U2 draws no turn line for the empty turn.
- Observation: a scheduled prompt carries no origin object on any route, and its text is not on the live wire.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-u3-schedule.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (four fires headless, three in a terminal); 54 of 54 fires in the session files. UC-2's rule that the origin object is the proof has this one exception.
- Observation: in a session MAWS runs the sminos bridge only observes; every send arrives through the engine's inbox as a peer's message, the owner's own included, and a steer is refused.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-u3-sminos.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (8 of 8 sends; 4 of 4 steers refused with "lane-b"). The bridge's own carriers and the answer to an asynchronous question exist only in terminal sessions. The owner's own send to a session MAWS runs is drawn as a peer's today (read in the code, not run), which is the opposite of O-41's fault and is now in that item.
- Observation: after a compaction that keeps a segment, the engine sends that segment's last command output again under the same uuid; and a command's output reaches the wire before the replay of the command itself.
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-u3-commands.txt`, on master since pull request #602 (three of three). A likely cause of #330's output under the wrong command; U3's reader drops a repeated uuid.
- Observation: Claude Desktop's Code tab has two transcript renderers and two request layouts, each pair split by a server gate that reads off when nothing is fetched; several behaviours this document called Desktop's are the gated variant's.
  Evidence: the audit of 2026-11-12 of every statement the children make about Desktop, against `app-5.1231.2` (kept with the grounding outside the repository): of 242 statements checked, 48 did not plainly hold. The renderer gate `__gb__3413676205` (`web/app/v1/cf74bc599-BVVr4KcU.js:15946-15987`, `c96d752d0-CjYhuD9j.js:131511-131527`) decides whether a run opens while a call waits. The layout gate `claudeai_cc_epitaxy_session_presets_main` decides the placement of requests and with it the "Needs your input" pill, the "Also waiting" list and whether a typed message answers a question. Hence the amendment of the same day, the corrected `typed-reply` question, and U14's check of which variants the owner's Desktop shows.
- Observation: Claude Desktop's Stop never cancels what waits; the branch that would is dead code in this build.
  Evidence: `main-process/index.chunk-BjORVu2_.js:22660` tests a function that returns false (`index.chunk-BV3uOqbK.js:252052`). U4 had given Desktop's dropping of waiting messages as the reason for its own hold.
- Observation: Claude Desktop does not bound an opened group of tool calls, in either of its transcript renderers: no cap on steps, no maximum height, no inner scroller.
  Evidence: U2's check 8, read in the source on 2026-11-12 (the disclosure component both renderers share, its panel's classes, the default renderer's loop over the group's runs, and the stylesheets). The question to the owner on a long opened run was sent before this reading; its recommended answer, full height, is Desktop's.
- Observation: Claude Desktop hands a Space's instructions to the agent in the system prompt of a session that belongs to the Space, unseen; MAWS's sessions do not belong to a Space, and U10 keeps the chip and the tools' answers as a stated departure.
  Evidence: U10's file, l8-settings-13 (the reading the Desktop audit left for before U10's dispatch, done on 2026-11-12). Taken from it: the tools' answers carry the instructions again after a compaction, which is what the system prompt gave Desktop and a chip does not.
- Observation: build F reads its two settings files more strictly than its layout: an unknown key, an action id it does not know in `keymap`, or another version fails the whole read, and F then shows defaults for every client setting and refuses to write them.
  Evidence: `ClientSettingsSchema` and `MachineSettingsSchema` in `src/shared/ipc/schema/settings.ts` are strict objects with a literal version; `keymap` is a partial record over `ACTION_IDS`; the read and write rules are in `src/shared/settings/file-store.ts`; these files and `src/shared/keymap/` are the same at `f1852de3a`. Found by U6a's author on 2026-11-12; UC-14 now covers it.
- Observation: for a session MAWS runs, the engine host's folder already follows the agent, one turn late; U8's item said it was kept from the first frame only.
  Evidence: `systemInit` in `src/engine-host/session-status.ts` sets `engineCwd` on every main-loop `system/init`, and the probe of 2026-11-12 showed that frame repeats each turn; `composer/Composer.tsx` and `composer/frame/bindings.ts` read the status folder ahead of the record. Found by U8a's author.
- Observation: the code spells a session's folder in more forms than the count the spec gave, and one of them is in the endpoint.
  Evidence: 26 matches of three patterns in 24 files under `src/` at `85e934368` (21 lines of code in 20 files, five comments), plus `checkoutOf` in `src/endpoint/targets.ts` and Files' `defaultRoot`, which reach the same answer without the spelling. Found by U8a's author and confirmed by the adversarial review of its document.
- Observation: the message the owner saw drawn as their own (O-41) came by a carrier nobody had listed, and for a background session sminos spawned it is the usual one.
  Evidence: the row behind O-41's screenshot is a `user` row of origin `human`, `promptSource: 'typed'`, `sessionKind: 'bg'`, in a seat's transcript on the owner's other Mac (read by structure only, 2026-11-12); 49 such rows in that file, 2 in the MacBook's files since 2026-10-25; `push_member` in sminos.py resumes a stopped seat with the frame as its prompt. The grounding had counted deliveries by the origin marks of the bridge and the inbox, and this carrier has neither. UC-2's carrier table and U3's O-41 are corrected, and U3's first landing reads the carrier (Decision Log). Found by the architect's trace.
- Observation: the e2e spec that U9's A4 named for its scene drives a machine that is not on the machine link's own protocol (RL2), so none of what the item changes runs there.
  Evidence: `e2e/machines.spec.ts` reaches its second profile's daemon over a socket link: no RL2, no hold, no remote fold. `remoteLaneBIn` in `src/renderer/src/lane-b/remote-session.ts` takes a session as one MAWS runs on another machine only with a `remote.session` state or a machine the org rows cover. A4's scene is now in `e2e/remote.spec.ts`. Found by the author of U9's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a Stop that goes out on a channel that then stalls is never answered "offline".
  Evidence: `src/main/machines/remote/acts.ts` marks an act whose channel was lost after it went as ambiguous and queries it, and the device's adapter answers the pending sentence at the bound. Only an act made while no channel is current waits under the hold and then settles offline. U9's A11 is amended. Found by the author of U9's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the project's "Archive sessions" already reaches for sessions on other machines; only the link's refusal stops it today.
  Evidence: `archiveProjectSessions` in `src/renderer/src/sessions/actions.ts` filters the row's sessions by "its machine is up", not by "this Mac". With `session.archive` offered, the same code would end processes on other machines, so U9 changes it to walk this Mac's records, where its file had said "as today". Found by the author of U9's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: `session.update` cannot be offered over the link by a row alone, for two reasons.
  Evidence: `'session.update': NO_RULE` in `src/endpoint/by-effect.ts`, so a row would answer `no-rule`; and the services' handler in `src/services/index/register.ts` throws for any request that carries a reader, which every act from another device does (`dispatchAs` in `src/endpoint/actions.ts`). Hence the rule for Unarchive in U9's file (UC-7.4), ruled by the session that keeps master. Found by the author of U9's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a ceiling answer given at the endpoint is not bound to the record that is forked.
  Evidence: the endpoint's decision follows a record's owners, access and shares, not its permission mode (`src/endpoint/authorize.ts`); `fork` in `src/services/sessions/service.ts` reads the source again inside its serialized operation, and `forkIntentOf` copies the requested mode from that reading. The fork's ceiling check is made in the services' fork, where U9's file had pointed at the endpoint. Found by the adversarial review of U9's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: build F reads the one-set value and loses no tab, but it does not keep the strip as the wave's build left it.
  Evidence: at `f1852de3a`, `refile` and `moveToSet` in `src/renderer/src/stores/layout-model.ts` file each session's tabs into its row's set one at a time, each after the active tab of the set it joins and in one pane, so the order of the tabs, a split across two sessions and the floating card are F's own after its first launch. `hydrate` in `stores/layout.ts` writes nothing for the filing itself; but a floating card that had folded the side column is lost with its split, F then opens the side column and writes `main.tabs` and `regions`, and it writes `panelTools.surfaces` as soon as it remembers a session for the set it shows. U13's "nothing is lost" and Parent-level acceptance 6 now say "no tab and no pin". Found by the author of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the e2e helper `newChatIn` cannot serve a press that reuses an empty chat.
  Evidence: the helper's poll in `e2e/support/sidebar.ts` waits for a session tab whose id the strip did not hold before the press; five scenes of `e2e/sessions.spec.ts` press New chat a second time with the first chat left empty. The helper stays unedited and those scenes press the control directly; U13's file had said the helper keeps working unchanged and had not listed that spec. Found by the author of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: three files read the per-project tab sets in ways U13's file did not name.
  Evidence: `panelSessionForArtifact` in `shell/transfer.ts`, the read of `main.current` in `customize/CustomizeView.tsx`, and `switchSession(null)` in `harness/panel/PanelHarness.tsx`; from a search for `main.current`, `.sets[` and `setCurrentProject` outside the two store files at `85e934368`. UC-8 gains the insertions. Found by the author of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the ordinary close cannot be used to close an empty chat for the person: it decides on the draft, then waits for main, and only then removes the tab.
  Evidence: `requestCloseTab` in `keymap/actions-runtime.ts` awaits the close guard before it takes the tab out, and `beforeCloseUnstarted` in `sessions/setup-actions.ts` reads the draft and then awaits `session.intent.discard`; text typed in that wait is discarded with the intent and leaves no reopen entry. U13's O-01 now closes such a chat by a step of its own that takes the tab out first. Found by the adversarial review of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a stored `main.tabs` can need its envelope rewritten without any tab being merged (one set under a project's key; an empty set or a set of duplicates beside `none`), and the side column's remembered session is lost at the second launch if it is not.
  Evidence: `panel/surface-model.ts` reads `contexts[main.current]`; a read that rewrites `contexts` under `none` while `main.tabs` keeps a project's key as `current` leaves the next launch to look under that key. U13's O-01 now writes such a value back in the stored form, without the notice. Found by the adversarial review of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: `focusPane` is called both when the store follows the keyboard and when the person chooses a pane, and six of its callers are of the second kind.
  Evidence: `pane.focusOther` in `app/shell-actions.ts`, and `floatSplit`, `dockSplit`, `openCard`, `closeCard` and `focusSplitCard` in `shell/floating.ts`, against the focus and pointer handlers of `main/Pane.tsx` and `shell/FloatingCard.tsx` and `focusCompactCard`. UC-12 and U13's rule 7 now name the six as acts that bring a tab to the front. Found by the adversarial review of U13a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the reader of another machine's rows has a second consumer the spec did not name: the desk web app draws its session view through it.
  Evidence: `apps/desk-web/src/depth/source/desk-source.ts` builds the same `PlaneTranscript`, and `apps/desk-web/src/conversation/asked.ts` and `tail.ts` import `askAnswers` and `deliveredLines`; `apps/desk-web/e2e/acceptance.spec.ts` pins the "From" label O-41 reverses, which was ruled for that view. The desk web app follows, one reading; "Out of scope, standing" keeps it with that exception. Found by the author of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: two e2e specs use the "Background task failed" item, which O-34 removes, as the means of another proof, and U3's list of tests lacked four places.
  Evidence: `e2e/support/supervisor-scratch.ts` (`tailFixture`, `TASK_FAILED`): `e2e/supervisor.spec.ts` and `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts` prove by that item that a tail journaled while no app ran reaches main's listeners. The four places: `e2e/agent-tools.spec.ts` (scene G3), `harness/agents/agentsProbes.ts`, `e2e/p1-live.spec.ts`, `apps/desk-web/e2e/acceptance.spec.ts`. Found by the author of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: UC-2 said a `command` arrival needs no entry on its event, while O-18 has the row and the mod's site read `run` from the reader; X1's `user.message` has no field for it.
  Evidence: U3's file, Contracts ("`command` and `own` need none") against its O-18 ("it now gets the command's name and arguments from `run`"). U3's first landing carries no `run`; the second adds a `command` entry, and the rule for an entry of a class a build does not know is fixed now. Found by the author of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the root's UC-2 said an answer carries "the answer in words, or 'dismissed'", and U3's `answer` class had no mark for a dismissal.
  Evidence: U3's `Arrival` union and its row table; `answeredWords` in `src/renderer/src/lane-a/conversation/ask-answer.ts`. A dismissal reaches a row only with the model's next tool result or the person's next prompt (the sminos bridge, 1.47.0). The class gains `dismissed`. Found by the author of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: on the live wire a turn the engine opens exists before any frame says what it is for, and the model cannot turn an engine's turn into the person's.
  Evidence: `Normalizer.systemInit` emits `turn.started {origin: 'engine'}` at `system/init`; `Batch.close` in `reduce.ts` takes a result's origin only when it is not `human`; `u3-tasks-r1.jsonl`, seq 355 and 356 (an `init` and a `result` with `num_turns: 0`, adjacent). So U3's first landing removes an engine turn that held nothing, where U3's file had left the empty turn to U2's drawing rule. Found by the author of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a notice's "Open output" had no wiring, and U3's UC-7 row said more than the code gives: the background work list passes no Output handler for a terminal session's job.
  Evidence: `TranscriptActions` in `src/renderer/src/transcript/view/context.tsx` has no action for a notice; `onOutput={derived !== null ? null : …}` in `src/renderer/src/tools/agents/background/BackgroundTool.tsx`, with the comment "a lane A job's tail is remote-only". The button is drawn for a session MAWS runs only. Found by the adversarial review of U3a's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: on the terminal routes a task's end is decided by two readers, the job ledger and the arrivals reader, so a card and the transcript's line can part.
  Evidence: `TABLE`, `fold` and `notification` in `src/services/lane-a/jobs.ts` (and its copy in `apps/desk-hub/fake-machine/jobs.ts`): the ledger ends a monitor on the engine's expiry line and ends a job on evidence the transcript has no line for, where the arrivals reader gives a block with no `<status>` no outcome. The ledger is kept in step with sminos and is not changed; U3's file names the two differences. Found by the authors of the execution documents of U3a and U8b, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a terminal session on this Mac has no source for its subagents' states, where U8's file said the task graph already serves a terminal session.
  Evidence: `src/services/lane-a/jobs.ts`: "What the vectors' `subagents` and `mcp` pin (agents' outcomes, MCP names) is not ported", and `derive` answers `{ subagents: [], jobs, mcp: [] }`; `tools/agents/store.ts` takes a feed only for a terminal session on another machine. That route shows no Subagents card until U8d (#599). Found by the author of U8b's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: counting a subagent's shells and monitors under it needs a key the code reads nowhere, carried through three wire shapes.
  Evidence: no match for `owned_by_subagent` or `parent_task_id` under `src/` beyond the untyped pass-through of `src/shared/wire/frames.ts`; `task.started` and `tasks.snapshot` in `src/shared/graph/events.ts`. The graph alone cannot name the parent of a task known only from a snapshot, so the optional key stands and the wire's minor is raised. Found by the author of U8b's execution document and its adversarial review, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: U8's A7 expected an agent of a gone terminal session to read "ended with the session" in the transcript, and the transcript's agents row reads an agent ended by an exit as stopped.
  Evidence: `endedByExit` in `transcript/model/derive.ts` sets every agent not ended to `'stopped'`; "ended with the session" exists only in `tools/agents/copy.ts`. The words are the panel's only; the word on the transcript's row is U3's second landing's. Found by the author of U8b's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the exit X-168 needs is not one the transcript follower can make today.
  Evidence: `endBackfill` and `unendedTurnExit` in `src/engine-host/fs/chain-follower.ts`: the exit is made only at the end of a backfill, with no holder at that moment, and only when the last turn never ended, where X-168's case is a process that ends after its turn. The change is in `src/engine-host/fs/`, which U8's file had not named. Found by the author of U8b's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: the control of l5-code-16, the Environment card's change line, is drawn by a file of U5's.
  Evidence: `Environment.tsx` renders `<ToolSummary toolId="review" component={ReviewSummary}>`, and the button is in `tools/code/review/ReviewSummary.tsx`. UC-8 gains the insertion. Found by the author of U8b's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: after a stay in build F, a session used there shows one tab for every tool opened there.
  Evidence: at `f1852de3a`, `closeSurface` takes a tool out of `stacks` and leaves its ref in the session's list, and the wave's prune of such refs runs at the first read only. Tabs and pins are kept and the arrangement in two groups is not; UC-14 and U8's file say so. Found by the author of U8b's execution document, 2026-11-12.
- Observation: a window of this build attached to an engine host on build F loses a plan's chosen mode: F's host drops the key it does not know, and the plan is implemented in the earlier mode.
  Evidence: the tolerant reader (`src/shared/ipc/tolerant.ts`; the header of `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`: "one that lacks an event or an object key drops it"), and that spec's update scenes, which attach this build's app to the peer's kept engine host. The host's status carries its wire beside the app's (`HostStatusSchema`), so the window can tell. Hence a plan's mode menu only under an engine host of this build's wire (U1, l4-approvals-11). Found by the adversarial review of U1a's execution document and confirmed by its author.
- Observation: against a session's machine on build F nothing holds "accepted, not settled", so that machine's own windows and its digest show today's behaviour until it is updated.
  Evidence: the mark's one holder is the list of a terminal session's requests in the services of the session's machine, told by its driver and by its endpoint (`laneA.accepted`); a machine on build F has no holder and its endpoint tells none. A device of this build that answers there still follows the machine's `done` (U1, UC-1). A limit, and nothing is added on this side for it. Found by the author of U1a's execution document at its revision.
- Observation: for #455, the grounding's "make the read a call" is the larger change, not the smaller.
  Evidence: a call may carry only `list` or `view` (`src/endpoint/authorize.ts`, step 5), so the row would need a new grade and a flag for the right and a second name for devices on build F, and it would pass the read-only and too-old rules an act does not. The exception belongs in `stepUpRequired`, where the registry branch has since added the same kind of exception (`UNSTEPPED_PROJECT_ACTS`); the grounding read a commit in which every `machine-admin` act was stepped up. Found in the writing of U11's answered design.
- Observation: a device asks for Touch ID only when the machine answers `step-up`, and nobody had written that down.
  Evidence: `if-asked` in `src/main/machines/remote/acts.ts`, at build F too. So the endpoint's one line changes what a device on build F does, and main needs no change; the card's "removes code" is true only of the sheet, and only once machines on F are gone. Found in the same writing.
- Observation: the directory does not name Google's favicon service for most connectors.
  Evidence: in the copy of 2026-10-30, 12 records have an icon in the directory's own storage, 10 name Google's service, 132 a claude.ai address, about 630 a vendor's host, and 262 none; the grounding had "the rest … name Google's favicon service or a vendor site". So the fetch cannot be of the addresses the directory names: the site is derived from the connector's own stated URL, as Claude Desktop derives it, and because Desktop's first address answers with a redirect, which MAWS's guarded request refuses, MAWS asks the second address directly. Found in the same writing.
- Observation: no writer of the Claude Code settings file keeps a hand-made layout.
  Evidence: the grounding asked that the writer "keep the rest of the file (and its formatting) untouched"; the engine and Claude Desktop both parse the file and write it back as two-space JSON, and the design does the same and says so (U11, l8-settings-11, the writer's rule 5). Found in the same writing.
- Observation: two facts about defaults today that the grounding did not have: a new chat's permission mode is copied from the user file when its tab is created, and the default a new chat names is always read on this Mac.
  Evidence: `seedPermissionMode` in `src/services/sessions/service.ts`, so a default changed later does not reach an empty chat that is already open; and the read that names the default carries no machine, also when the chat is set to another machine (U11, l8-settings-11, "Today"). Found in the same writing.
- Observation: the engine does not read its user settings file key by key: one `permissions.defaultMode` it does not know makes it skip the whole file, the model with it, and under MAWS's launch it says so nowhere a person sees.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u11-user-defaults.txt`, on master since pull request #606 (engine `2.1.322`): with a model set and an unknown mode, `system/init` reported the engine's own default model and `default` mode, and the error was only in `get_settings.errors`; a terminal stopped at a "Settings Error" dialog. U11's reader therefore marks no default for a file the engine reports an error for. (Since the ruling of 2026-11-13 on the defaults there is no mark: the Settings row shows no default for such a file and says that it has an error.) Found by the second probe pass, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: in plan mode the engine ran every turn on another model when the model setting was the alias `haiku`, while `system/init` named haiku.
  Evidence: the same findings, 3 of 3 runs; with a full model id the turn stayed on that model. So "Your default" is right about the setting and can differ from the model a plan-mode turn uses (U11, l8-settings-11). (Since the ruling of 2026-11-13 the mark is gone; the fact now bears on the model a pick writes.) Found by the same pass.
- Observation: Yjs's `cleanupFormatting` flag gates the writer's own clean-up as well as the one after an apply, so the fix first ruled for l6-artifacts-07 (a), the flag off for good, removes both, and a host of build F then refuses a write.
  Evidence: U10a's execution document, "What the code holds today" and decision 1 (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U10a-unmade-writes.md`, branch `ux-wave/U10a`): in `@y/y` 14.0.0-rc.28 `cleanupFormattingGap`, `cleanupContextlessFormattingGap` and `cleanupYTextFormatting` each return at once when the flag is false; measured through the real document service, a replica built with the flag off whose writer deletes a whole bold word leaves both markers in its update, and a host that builds its documents as build F does refuses that update and evicts the document. U10a cancels the clean-up after an apply with a listener and leaves the flag as it is. Found by U10a's author, 2026-11-13; its adversarial reviewer confirmed the order of the event against the pinned source.
- Observation: the first probe pass recorded its Send-now fixtures without the session-state events MAWS turns on, so those fixtures hold no state frame at all.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u4-send-now-cases.txt` on master (pull request #606), case 4 and "Launch": the second pass adds the environment keys MAWS's launch sets and the first pass left out, `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS=1` among them. The fixtures to code against are the second pass's (`u4p2-a-1` to `-3` for the plain case; its main case repeats the first pass's result under MAWS's environment). Found by the second probe pass, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a computer-use call's `timeout_ms` is not a time a host can count, so MAWS cannot tell when a cancelled cell has ended.
  Evidence: U12a's execution document (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U12a-browser-computer-use-delivery.md`, branch `ux-wave/U12a`), from the plugin's source: the budget is suspended around approvals and a helper's start and resumes with what was left (`executionBudget` in `src/repl-host/budget.mjs`), calls run one after another, so a queued call's budget starts later than its row, and a cancelled call never stops its cell (`supervisor.mjs`). So the line U12 shows after a Stop that withdrew a running call has no timer and leaves on evidence or at Dismiss; cua#369 asks for the missing signal. Found by U12a's adversarial review, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: under MAWS's own launch environment a foreground subagent call does not occur, so the case the first probe pass recorded as "moved, with the message read in the same turn" pins nothing for a session MAWS runs; the call that is moved with the message read in the same turn is an MCP call.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u4-send-now-cases.txt` on master (pull request #606): with `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=true` the subagent call ran in the background four times of four (`u4p2-k-agent-1`); an MCP call was moved within 6 ms as a task, with no flag on its result, and the message was read in the same turn (`u4p2-k-mcp-1`). U4's table, A3 and its reversal sentence named the subagent call and are corrected. Found by the second probe pass, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: the engine delivers every message that waits at the next step together, as one user message under the last one's uuid, both at a Send now that names one of them and at a turn's ordinary end; its transcript file then holds one line for them.
  Evidence: the same findings: `u4p2-two-1-1` and `u4p2-two-2-1` (both go, whichever is named, each with a `started` of its own), and the third pass's `u4p3-end2-1` to `-3` and `u4p3-end3-1` (two and three messages at an ordinary end; on master with pull request #609). Until now MAWS drew one bubble per message live and one bubble after a reload; U4a's document draws the one message the engine wrote. Found by the second and third probe passes, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a message at priority `now` moves a running shell command only once the engine has registered the command as a task, about three seconds into the call; written earlier it moves nothing and is delivered when the command ends, while the send-now request written at that same early moment waits for the task and then moves the command. A `now` message is never joined with one that waits at the next step.
  Evidence: the third pass's section of the same findings: `u4p3-promote-late-1` to `-3` (moved 5 to 9 ms after the write, three of three), `u4p3-promote-1` to `-3` (nothing moved, three of three), `u4p3-mirror-1` (the request: moved 1.03 s later). U4's file and its reversal sentence said that a `now` message and the request behave alike. Found by the third probe pass, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: after the engine has moved the command, a plain interrupt does nothing at all, and the Stop square's end has the same frames as a Send now's; only the `cancelled` lifecycle of the withdrawn message tells the two apart.
  Evidence: `u4p2-stop-moved-1` (the interrupt's answer still lists the message, the end is the Send now's, and the message runs) against `u4p2-stop-moved-hold-1` (the message's `cancelled`, then the same end). So "You stopped" is read from a stop that took effect and not from an interrupt having been asked (U4a's document, decision 34). Found by the second probe pass and U4a's author, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: the draft store belongs to the machine's services, not to the engine host, and on one Mac the two can be of different builds in both directions.
  Evidence: `session.drafts.*` is of owner `machine` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts`) and is served by `src/services/index/`; after an update the services restart onto the new build while the earlier engine host serves on, and `watchDaemon` in `src/main/hosts/daemon.ts` leaves a unit of another bundle running. So whether a machine can take a waiting message back is learned from its draft store's own refusal, not read from the engine host's wire fingerprint. Found by U4a's author, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: engine 2.1.322 lists 51 slash commands and the first eighteen are its bundled skills; in the engine's order `/model` is the 36th, and the panels MAWS adds fall behind the palette's cut at 40 rows, where nothing typed can reach them.
  Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/uxwave/u4p1-a-seeded-1.jsonl`, seq 4; `ROW_LIMIT = 40` in `composer/mentions/SlashPalette.tsx`. Hence the owner's answer of 2026-11-13, "MAWS's panels first". Found by U4a's author while drawing the owner's example.
- Observation: the same session groups differently live and from its transcript file today, for two reasons: only the live route knows that the person allowed a call, and only the wire's reader reads what kind of refusal a result was.
  Evidence: `classifyTool` reads `humanDecision`, which `deriveTurn` sets from `permission.resolved`, an event the file reader never emits; the file reader's result producer writes `denial: null` (`src/engine-host/fs/backfill.ts`) though the transcript row carries `toolDenialKind` (`test/fixtures/transcripts/w1-steer-next-interrupted.jsonl`), and the engine makes the wire's `non_execution_kind` from that same field (`cli.pretty.js:237652-237668` of `2.1.322`). U2a ends both differences (U2's file, O-29). Found by the author of U2a's execution document and its adversarial review, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: the person's Deny and the person's Stop leave different kinds on a call, and a Deny that ends the turn leaves exactly what a Stop leaves.
  Evidence: a deny with no interrupt gives the call `permission-rule` and the turn goes on (`pass3/question-deny.jsonl`, the result at seq 112, with the classification MAWS's broker sends; `pass3/plan-outcomes.jsonl`, seq 132 to 134); a deny with `interrupt: true` gives `user-rejected`, the interrupt marker and an aborted end (`plan-outcomes`, seq 506 to 510). All 28 `user-rejected` results of the main loop in the probe fixtures are followed by an aborted end with nothing of the model between, so no recording shows a rejected call the model went on from. Found by the author of U2a's execution document, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a transcript file of a session MAWS ran records no turn ends, so there a rejected result decides how its turn closes.
  Evidence: no `turn_duration` row in `uxwave/u3-tasks-r1.transcript.jsonl` or `test/fixtures/transcripts/w1-steer-next-interrupted.jsonl`, where a terminal session's file has them; such a turn closes at the next turn's start, as stopped when `wasInterrupted` (`transcript/model/reduce.ts`) finds the interrupt marker or a `user-rejected` result. Hence the one condition U2a adds there. Found by the author of U2a's execution document, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a call's result can arrive before a later `tool_use` block of the same API message, so "a block after the result" does not show that the model went on.
  Evidence: eighteen probe recordings, `subagent.jsonl` and `pass3/plan-outcomes.jsonl` among them: the engine starts a call before the model's message is whole. U2's rule for a rejected call therefore counts a later block only when it belongs to another API message than the call's. Found by the author of U2a's execution document, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: an event's `seq` does not by itself tell a frame that came over the wire from an event rebuilt from the transcript; and build F's attention service asks neither.
  Evidence: `eventBase` in `src/engine-host/fs/backfill.ts` stamps `seq: null` on every event made from a file, but the normaliser's `transcript_reseed` case restamps the rebuilt events with its journal row's `seq` and marks them `reseeded: true`. So the condition under which a refusal raises attention reads both fields (UC-8, U2a's insertion in the attention service). F's service raises `auto_mode_unavailable` for a result of that kind wherever it came from, so with this build's engine host feeding F's services, such a call read from a file raises the item there until the next human prompt. Found by the author of U2a's execution document, at the architect's ruling on its second review, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a row that is only listed, and the desk's web view, are named by the listing's title, which the index's projection does not write, so S-11's six words do not reach them by the cut.
  Evidence: `readMember` in `src/endpoint/rl10/members.ts`; `title: report.title` in `src/endpoint/listings.ts`; `title: fields.title` in `apps/desk-web/src/cards/board.ts`. U7's file, S-11, says how the words reach a listed row: through U9's listing rule, for a session MAWS runs on that machine. Found by the author of U7's execution document, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: only a session MAWS runs has a datum for when a turn began, and a listing's `issued` moves for a rename, so the rule O-16 first gave for a hidden session's return could not be built as written on two of the three routes.
  Evidence: `turnStartedAt: null` in `digest` (`src/services/index/source.ts`), and `bridgedDigest` returns a phase, the activity and the pending requests only; `#evaluate` and `#sign` in `src/endpoint/listings.ts` sign a listing again whenever any field changes. Hence the rule in U7's file: an entry returns only after the window has seen the session not working since the hide. Found by the author of U7's execution document and by its adversarial review, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a machine named by the session rows counts as listed whether or not the hub's snapshot is complete or the machine is pending, so a session that was named and is now absent is not a session that is gone.
  Evidence: `applyRows` in `stores/sessions.ts`, `takeRows` in `ipc/events.ts`, and the case of `ipc/events.test.ts` that keeps a named machine's restored tabs while the rows are incomplete or the machine is pending. A hidden entry of another machine's session is therefore never dropped for absence, and the key is bounded by count (U7's file, O-16). Found by the adversarial review of U7's execution document, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: an open that an agent or a mod asks for reaches the layout store's activating opens, and one made before the layout is read is queued in the panel host and applied later, outside any synchronous scope of its caller.
  Evidence: `showPane` in `tools/maws/panes/connect.ts`; `pending`, `passBarrier`, `applyAdd` and `activateIn` in `panel/host.ts`; three more listeners bring a side surface forward without the person (`tools/browser/agent/connect.ts`, `tools/maws/ask/connect.ts`, `engine-surface/panes.ts`). So UC-12's "any act of the person's" needed who asked as data on the request, and not a synchronous wrap alone. Found by the author of U7's execution document and by its adversarial review, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: eight readers ask whether something is on screen, by six different rules, and none of them knew of a page.
  Evidence: U7's execution document, its table of what is on screen behind a page: `session/visibility.ts`, `attention/navigation.ts`, `app/plugin-toasts.ts`, `engine-surface/panes.ts`, `panel/ToolBody.tsx`, `library/ArtifactTab.tsx`, `tools/browser/covering.ts` and `tools/browser/agent/connect.ts`; six of them are in files other children own or no child owns (UC-8). Found by the author of U7's execution document, after its adversarial review named five, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: the title bar's right end is not the window's right edge while a side surface is open, so a page drawn over a kept side column would leave the usage ring in mid-window with a blank strip beside it.
  Evidence: the side column is the grid's full-height right column (`panel/PanelRegion.tsx`), and the title bar spans the two columns beside it; seen on the drawn example of U7's frames. Hence a page takes the side column's place too (UC-12), and the ring stands at the title bar's own right end. Found on the drawn example, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a machine's listing of a session did not follow that session's record: after an archive or a rename, a device with no tab of the session open kept the row and the old title.
  Evidence: `test/endpoint/listing-follows-record.test.ts`, written as the first step of U9's first milestone and run on the merged base before any change of U9's: both halves failed. The endpoint makes a listing in two places, both from sminos's member frame and neither reading the record: `#report` in `src/endpoint/rl10/members.ts` (the hub's) and `#listings` in `src/endpoint/calls.ts` (the answer to `endpoint.listings`). The `session.changed` the endpoint forwards rides the feed of a session whose tab a device holds, so it does not reach a device that holds only the listing. U9 builds the listing rule of S-02 on both paths (U9's file, Probes and checks, 2). Found by the executor of U9's first milestone, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: the engine drops a plugin's MCP server, with no notice, when a server configured in a settings file names the same address; and a plugin's server is told from a server of MAWS's own list only by its name.
  Evidence: engine `2.1.322`, the function near `cli.pretty.js:476051`: a plugin's server is compared by its normalised URL with the file-configured servers and then with the plugins loaded earlier, and a duplicate is left out of `mcp_status`; the one trace is the debug line "Suppressing plugin MCP server … duplicates manually-configured …". Servers passed with `--mcp-config` are not compared. A plugin's server is listed with scope `dynamic`, as a server of the launch's own list is, so the prefix `plugin:` of its name is all that tells the two apart. `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u11-plugin-server-dedup.txt` and the part "UX wave U11a, M1" of `spikes/p4-aggregator/findings-m4.txt`, on branch `ux-wave/U11a`: in gate run 43357272365 the scratch plugin's server was dropped, and in run 43358963647, with the file-configured servers removed first, it was listed and signed in. It bears on who is offered Sign in and on X-184's rows (U11's file, l8-settings-01 and X-184). Found by U11a's probe P1 (the sign-in spike, run through the gate on the Mac mini), 2026-11-13.
- Observation: after a sheet that had been opened from a menu item over a dialog closed, the keyboard's focus fell to the page: the menu item that opened the sheet was gone from the document, and the dialog under it did not take the focus back.
  Evidence: the stacked scene of `e2e/widgets.spec.ts` (#523: a confirmation over the Plugins dialog), red at the first Mac run of U11a's scenes; `useDialogFocus` in `src/renderer/src/app/dialog-focus.ts` remembered the menu item as the layer's opener. U11a's landing changes that file, which no child owns: when a layer closes and the control that opened it is gone, focus returns to the topmost dialog still open. Returning it to the menu's own trigger is the better target and is a tracker row. Found by the first Mac run of U11a's scenes, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: main greyed another machine's Needs you items at the link's first loss, so every short loss of the link took a pending approval out of the count and the dock badge and then put it back.
  Evidence: `src/main/lane-b/remote-attention.ts` sent a machine's items `greyed: true` from the link's `down`, while U9's section says that nothing is greyed while a machine reconnects; the first Mac run of the scene asserted "machine offline" seconds after the link was cut. Ruled as one phase rule for main and the window (Decision Log). Found by the first Mac run of U9's scenes, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: a session on another machine that was never renamed was listed under its first prompt, not under the title its machine reported.
  Evidence: the listing rule of U9's first milestone took any non-empty title of the record, and the index fills an untitled record's title from its first prompt (source `none`), so every never-renamed session of another machine was listed so in a scene of `e2e/remote.spec.ts`. A title of source `none` now counts as no title, with a failing-first case in `test/endpoint/listing-follows-record.test.ts`. Found by a Mac run of U9's scenes, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: under the gate's Node 22 no module that imports `node:sqlite` loads in a test project that runs under happy-dom, so a suite green on the build VM's Node 26 failed in class 1a.
  Evidence: Vitest externalizes Node's built-ins by `builtinModules`, and Node 22's list leaves out the scheme-only `node:sqlite` (class 1a run 43371763994: `Cannot bundle built-in module "node:sqlite"`). The DOM projects now take `node:sqlite` through an alias every landing shares, `domAlias` in `vitest.config.ts`, to the shim `test/support/node-sqlite.ts`. Found by U10a's class 1a run, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: whether a send-now behind a shell command cuts the turn depends on when it is pressed: in about the first three seconds of the call, before the engine has registered the command as a task, the turn is cut and the waiting messages are one user message; later the command is moved and the messages are read in the same turn, each its own. And a request written before the engine has queued the message it names answers `nothing_waiting` and moves nothing.
  Evidence: 22 runs on engine 2.1.322 (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u4b-send-now-early.txt` and the fixtures `fixtures/uxwave/u4b-*.jsonl`, on U4b's branch): the frame-first pair answered `delivering` in 19 of 19 runs, 1.5 s into the call (5 of 5) and 10 s into it (5 of 5) among them; the request first answered `nothing_waiting` in 3 of 3. Found by the probe for U4b's conditional step, 2026-11-15.
- Observation: the engine joins an answer's envelope with a prompt that waits beside it into one user message, when a turn ends by itself and on an early Send now, and does not join them at a tool boundary; the reader of a whole message read none of the joined texts as an answer.
  Evidence: the recordings `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/uxwave/u4b-env-*` with `findings-uxwave-u4b-envelope-join.txt` (on U4b's branch): joined in 10 of 10 runs, the members in writing order with one newline between them, under the last-written member's uuid; not joined at a tool boundary in 4 of 4. Found by the probe for U4b's joined answer, 2026-11-15.
- Observation: the window parses a path link's destination as a URL before a click reads it, and for six link destinations the path the click asks for was not the path the transcript wrote.
  Evidence: U6c's test that holds the window's extraction and the daemon's rule together found them; a mirror of the Markdown pipeline written from its source had agreed on every case its author listed. A destination is now named only when the window's parse reads to the same path (U6, O-37, "U6c as built"). Found by U6c's first milestone, 2026-11-13.
- Observation: an open the agent asks for already opened a file from outside the folders when the transcript named it; this document said such an open keeps the refusal.
  Evidence: `show_pane` opens a file's tab through the same opener as a click, and the viewer's read asks under U6c's rule; until U6e the request carried nothing that told an agent's open from a click (U6, O-37, "What became of U6c's list for U6b's design"). Found by the author of U6b's cut, 2026-11-14.
- Observation: the engine's auto mode classifier took a sentence quoted inside MAWS's answer envelope, a user-role message, for the person's consent. With the engine's consent sentence at the end of a question's text, the retry of a blocked call was allowed in 2 of 15 runs; with the sentence as a picked option's label, written as a quoted string, in 5 of 5. In one allowed run the retried command carried an added suffix, although the sentence approves "this exact command only".
  Evidence: `findings-uxwave-711-envelope-consent.txt` with its journals, on the probe's branch `ux-wave/711-envelope-probe` (engine `2.1.322`, auto mode, one fresh session per run, invented content). With the pick written by its place the retry was allowed in 0 of 5 runs, and with the sentence as the first option's label of an ask in the session and the envelope reading `1. option 1`, in 0 of 5; the counts are small and do not exclude a rare pass. Found by the probe for board ticket 711, 2026-11-16.

## Decision Log

- **2026-11-12 · Verification.** This document: one independent review of the design and the cut by a frontier-model reviewer, and one adversarial review of the cut, the contracts and the edges for buildability; findings are judged, not adopted wholesale. Each child's execution document: an adversarial review before its executor starts. Each branch: `doperpowers:review-code`, the xhigh panel for a branch of panel size or a delicate change (UC-10 names them), a single reviewer otherwise. A new or reworked e2e scene: run alone on a Mac before the final gate. The wave: the owner's sitting (Parent-level acceptance 2).
- **2026-11-12 · Scope.** Tracker rows enter the wave only when they ride with an item (13 of 260 candidates); desk web's candidates stay out; the visual renewal takes O-04, #342 and PR #144. Rejected: taking every tracker row that touches a surface, which would have doubled the wave with rows nobody asked for.
- **2026-11-12 · The owner's answers** to the eight first questions, as summarised under Design and recorded verbatim in the register. On placement the owner picked no option and wrote an addition; the architect's reading of it was put back to the owner and confirmed the same day.
- **2026-11-12 · One document in several files.** The child sections came to about 124,000 words, too much for one file that every executor reads. The root holds what binds everyone (purpose, laws, contracts, ordering, the record) and a summary of each child; each child's section is its own file under `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-12-ux-wave/`. This departs from the composite template's single file. Rejected: cutting the sections down to fit, which would have dropped the per-route behaviour and the reasons that make each fix checkable.
- **2026-11-12 · The mod's gate (UC-13).** The mod's view draws on MAWS's surfaces at 9 and steps aside below, instead of the unconditional change of the 10-23 branch. Reason: build F advertises 8 and draws its own report, so the unconditional change, merged before the wave's build is installed, would draw the report twice. With the gate the mod's pull request can merge at any time. The branch is redone by hand on the mod's current main (its base is eighteen commits behind in that directory).
- **2026-11-12 · Settled between sections at assembly.** (1) A call waiting for permission stays in its run (U2's rule); rejected: the row standing outside its run (U1's draft), which departs from Desktop for a case that is rare under auto mode. As first written this point also drew the run open while the call waits, "as Claude Desktop does"; that half was amended the same day (below, "A run is not opened for a waiting call"). A row that holds a question's, an ask's or a plan's form is never folded and asks no mod's site while it waits. (2) On a permission prompt in view, ⌘↩ with an empty draft, ⇧⌘↩ and Esc act ahead of the composer's own keys, under Claude Desktop's guards (U1's rule); U4's table follows. (3) Arrival rows ask the engine's `UserMessage` site where a mods surface is attached (U2's need); rejected: U3's draft, which kept that site for the person's prompts only, because the mod then could not fold arrivals and the mod-drawn view would differ from the mirror (laws 2 and 5). (4) Offers: U9 adds the rows known now; U4, U5 and U12 insert theirs later in U9's files (UC-7). (5) File ownership as UC-8's table. (6) Up and down between messages follow Claude Desktop, with its guards, as U3 wrote it.
- **2026-11-12 · Corrections to what the owner was told.** Each was sent to the owner the same day. Settings in Claude Desktop is a dialog, not a page: asked again. The in-row question in Claude Desktop is behind a gate: the owner's answer stands. "Approve grants that exact call once" cannot be built in auto mode: it is "Approve and retry", a message. A link outside the session's folders is refused on this Mac too. Send to Drive converts documents and decks, not spreadsheets. The shortcut for Files is ⇧⌘F (⇧⌘E is effort). The "Thoughts" card and the drawn example's fourth frame assumed that thoughts with text are rare; in a session MAWS runs every thought has text: the card was replaced by "Thoughts 2". While a question waits, Claude Desktop does not by default take a typed message as its answer: it dismisses the question, which then reads "Not answered"; "Replied in chat" belongs to the layout behind a server gate. The question was put again with three choices. Claude Desktop's default question card answers at one click on an option; the preselected first option is the gated layout's. Claude Desktop's Stop does not drop what waits: it is a plain interrupt and the waiting messages then run; MAWS's hold with Resume is its own. Desktop's own move of running calls at Send now is behind the same server switch as its automatic move.
- **2026-11-12 · First review round, contracts and ordering (adversarial reviewer).** Thirteen findings, each checked against the source it cites; all adopted, none dismissed. (1) U8's two side-column widths go under a new layout key and `regions` keeps version 5: build F reads `regions` strictly and would have reset the stored layout on the way back. (2) UC-14 now forbids a new value where F's reader has a closed set, and U11 stores its new Settings section as one F knows while Settings is still a tab. (3) The owner's read of archived sessions on another machine gets a cut of its own at `manage`; the generic cut would have shown archived records to every member who can list. (4) Where the mod owns a fold MAWS can neither read nor open it, so a jump's target is *revealed* (drawn by MAWS, no site asked), and in the to-human view a run that holds a call waiting for permission stays in its working record: the card at the end is the request's drawing. Rejected: asking the mod for a way to open a stretch, which would widen the mod's change beyond its one gate. (5) A launch advertises 9 only when the mod will draw and 8 when MAWS will, so exactly one side draws in every combination, the failed-probe fallback included. (6) The ordering names a step 0 (U6a, U8a) and splits U4 into its own work and its integrations; the first group no longer claims to need only the base. (7) U3's reader gains the `consent` class that U1's "Approve and retry" draws through. (8) U12's pane request gets a capability of its own. (9) U11's sign-in of a dynamic connector is a new offer with a capability. (10) A side thread on another machine takes Fork's authorisation but its own rule for a running turn. (11) Back and Forward walk only places still open (U6's rule); U13's file now says so. (12) Where a failed task's line cannot be drawn live, its item stays as a Needs you entry. (13) U14's outcomes point at the owning child's branches. Also from that review: UC-4's bound on the folder the second landing admits, and the Files tree keeping all its roots. A second round on the changes (the same reviewer, 2026-11-12) found ten of the thirteen closed and four points to correct, all adopted: the widths key is `panelTools.widths`, because main reads every `panel.<name>` key as one session's panel; `consent` is the person's in the reader's helper and in U2's table of turn verbs; who draws the to-human view is decided again after every plugin reload and never for the rest of a session; and the sign-in of a dynamic connector is a command of its own, because a requirement is looked up per command.
- **2026-11-12 · First review round, design and cut (frontier-model reviewer).** Twelve findings; the facts each turns on were re-read. Adopted: (1) "Peer session worked for" stays for a turn that a peer session's message opened, and only the subagent's report changes to "Continued for": the owner's words cover the report, and with the verb gone a folded peer turn named no cause (U2 had removed the verb everywhere; U3 still tested for it). (2) The register is the one list of open questions; each child's Waiting points at it, and the sentences that said otherwise are corrected. (3) U4's automatic move is built only on the owner's yes. (4) `/fork` has a builder: U4's slash palette, calling U9's act. (5) O-36 (tabs in the side column) moves to U8 and U6 and is put to the owner with this document, because files now open in the side column. (6) The working folder follows the agent only into a worktree of the same repository; another repository is not followed, which also bounds what the files service admits. (7) U3 says that in the to-human view a failed task's line is inside the working record. (8) A session shown without its transcript draws no request, only one line that opens the conversation. (9) An open destination is restored after a relaunch (l7-shell-12 is a keep). (10) The project-wide "Archive sessions" stays this Mac's. (11) l6-artifacts-20's export folder is not a reversal until the owner says which word holds. (12) Facts corrected: the count of folder readers, the engine's cap, three claims about Claude Desktop, the owner-facing line on l5-code-02. Kept against the review, each on the register's list of decisions the owner may see differently: bookkeeping calls as ordinary steps of an opened group; the preview tab in Files (Claude Desktop's shape, and the owner's own rule for main tabs); Commit with nothing staged committing everything. Left for the children's execution documents: the routes a few acceptance lines omit (U1's A8, A10 and A11, U2's A3, O-32).
- **2026-11-12 · Decisions the sections made beyond the cut's lines** that the owner is most likely to see differently are in the register under that heading, were listed for the owner with this document, and are recorded in each child's file; an answer that differs is recorded here, dated, and the child's file is revised.
- **2026-11-12 · The thought rule tests duration, not text (U2, O-27 and #479).** The section drew a thought inside an opened run when it had text, or had none and lasted ten seconds. Check 6 showed that every thought has text in a session MAWS runs, so that rule drew every thought there (a measured run of 54 calls would open to about 96 lines), against the owner's condition on length. The rule is now: a thought of ten seconds or more gets a line, with its text behind it when it has one, on every route, tested on the duration the engine itself records on the wire and in the transcript file (since engine `2.1.287`; MAWS read neither field). A version written an hour earlier tested the gap between rows in the file where MAWS had not seen the block stream, on the belief that the file records no duration; the session files disproved it and that test was removed. Rejected: keeping the test on text and accepting long runs where MAWS runs the session; stopping the request for summaries, which the live thinking label reads. The threshold is the owner's choice: the card "Thoughts" was replaced the same day by "Thoughts 2" (ten seconds and more, recommended; five seconds and more; every thought; none inside a run), and the owner was told that the first card and the drawn example's fourth frame rested on the wrong fact.
- **2026-11-12 · The wire-only engine probes run before dispatch, on a cloud VM.** The ordering had every probe first inside its child, after the owner's word to build. The probes that need only the engine and its wire build no fix and depend on none of the open questions, and their results choose between designs the sections already describe, so they were started while the owner reads, on a leased Linux VM (the same engine version; MAWS drives Linux hosts too) and not on the mini, which was under a CI hold with a degraded link. A probe that needs macOS, MAWS's own build, the changed mod, or sessions on the owner's live desk stays with its child. UC-10's rule that such a probe runs on a Mac other than the MacBook is read as "not on the MacBook". The owner was told the same hour. Rejected: waiting, which would have left four children's first step to start only after approval and after the registry branch lands.
- **2026-11-12 · Probe results, first batch (the working folder, subagent tasks, thinking text, memory recall).** Each finding was read against its fixtures before the spec changed; the probes launched the engine as MAWS's engine host does. (1) l5-code-14's second landing is built as written, a shell's `cd` included: the newest row of the main transcript carries the folder after every move. Two rules were added from what the probe saw: the branch comes from git at the working folder, never from the row; a `relocated` row is not the signal, and the launch folder stops moving on one. (2) #359 keeps the count, which now includes shells and monitors because the engine names their starter; an agent whose run ended while its own task still runs stays in the Running part until the task ends. Rejected: moving it to Done and back, which makes the row jump, and counting only the agents the engine lists, which loses it in between. (3) #351 is deferred: the engine recalls nothing on the owner's configuration. Rejected: building it anyway because both references draw it, which adds a step kind and two readers for a frame that never arrives. (4) No model needs #479's fallback in a session MAWS runs. Read by structure only, it is the prompt of a background session sminos spawned (a seat) on the owner's other Mac: sminos resumes a stopped seat with the delivery as its prompt, and the engine writes an ordinary `user` row of origin `human` with `sessionKind: 'bg'`.
