- **2026-11-12 · The documents of U9 and U13a: what the code said, and the rulings.** The execution documents of U9 (sessions on other machines) and of U13's first landing (the one strip) were written against the code at `85e934368`. Each had one adversarial review and one revision round, and each corrected this spec; the corrections are made where the statements stand (Parent-level acceptance 6, UC-8, UC-12, UC-14, the ordering, and the files of U4, U7, U9 and U13). *U9, nine points reported and ruled by the architect.* (R1) The root's eight acts behind `rl2.session-acts` stand; U9's code block listed six, and U4's file still counted a command the owner's answer had removed. (R2) The project's "Archive sessions" walks this Mac's records only and its sheet says so. That is a change, not today's behaviour: today's code walks every machine that is up, and with `session.archive` offered it would end processes on other machines. Point 10 of the entry "First review round, design and cut" called it a thing that stays; it is read with this correction. (R3) A4's scene is in `e2e/remote.spec.ts`, for a session MAWS runs and for a terminal session, because `e2e/machines.spec.ts` drives a machine linked by a socket, where nothing of the path #563 changes runs. Such a machine gets the mounted pane by the same rule, and steps 4 and 5 of that spec assert the status line. (R4) A11 is amended and is proved by two scenes: a Stop from this Mac that reaches the machine and ends the turn, and a Stop pressed after the status line shows, which gives the offline notice. A Stop that went out on a channel that then stalled is never answered offline and keeps the pending sentence. (R5) UC-7.5's sentence for a control closed by an offline machine holds over the shorter wording of #563. (R6) UC-8 gains U9's insertions in files other children own, and the two transcript files O-11b needs, which no child owned, are U9's. (R7) The live word of a session rides its listing entry. Rejected: a digest said by main's fold, which would need an invented revision, availability, cost and context, and which nothing could take back when the tab closes. A private session is not exempt, since its listing comes over the link like any other; the one case with nothing to carry the word is a session that has no listing entry yet. (R8) No words for taking back a waiting message are added until U4 mounts that act. (R9) As the code stood, the services' handler of `session.update` refused every request made under a reader, and every act from another device runs under its caller's reader, so Unarchive would have failed on the machine. This point was also ruled by the session that keeps master, whose ruling it amends, under four conditions: the handler itself checks that the patch's own keys are exactly `archived` and its value `false`; it keeps refusing every other patch from a reader, an archive included, because Archive crosses as `session.archive`, which also ends the process; the answer, and `session.rename`'s under a reader, is the record as `session.get` answers that reader, read after the transaction; and the amendment is written at two places of P5 O8's plan, with a pointer at a third. The operation ledger stores an answer as the handler gave it and replays it only to the member who sent the operation; one case, a former owner of the machine replaying an answer given while an owner, is with that session. *What U9's review found: four findings, all adopted.* The fork's ceiling: an answer at the endpoint is not bound to the record that is forked, so the check is made inside the services' fork, on the record the fork copies its mode from. Rejected: a second comparison at the endpoint, which could only be a stale copy of that one; and carrying the ceiling in the command's input or the operation's claim, which would change a schema that crosses the link. The stored answer: the ledger keeps R9's answers, so the tests read the first answer, a duplicate and a query. The terminal pane: a terminal session on another machine has no record by design, so a pane is kept by what it has to keep, and only a session MAWS runs whose record was never read in this run shows the words in the pane's middle. Rejected: mounting only a session that has a record, which would have unmounted a terminal session's pane at every outage. The settled word: it is said once per connection generation, and only when no session is still being placed and no subscription is unanswered. Rejected: ending a placement at any answer of the record read, because a refused read is retried and the word would then be said beside a history read already under way, which is the fault of the attempt P5 reverted. *U9's order and verification.* Its first milestone does not start before P5's branch `p5-idle-margin` is on master, and U9 writes against that branch's seam in `src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts`. The session that keeps master runs the xhigh review panel on U9's whole branch, because the endpoint and the rulings U9 amends are P5's. Rejected: a single reviewer, for that reason; and a panel per milestone, because the delicate half is two milestones that the whole-branch panel reads together. *U13a, six points reported and ruled by the architect.* (1) Going back to build F loses no tab and no pin, and that is what the spec now promises; what F does afterwards is by its own rules (U13's file and Parent-level acceptance 6 say what). Rejected: storing a second copy of the arrangement for F, a second stored shape kept for a build that is being left (law 4). (2) The e2e helper `newChatIn` stays unedited; a scene that expects an empty chat to be reused presses the control directly. (3) U13's e2e list takes `e2e/sessions.spec.ts` and drops `e2e/ux2-shell.spec.ts`. (4) Its unit-test list is corrected. (5) UC-8 gains U13a's insertions in files other children own. The Subagents card lists agents that run or have a running agent under them; an agent whose run ended with only tasks running is not on the card, because its tasks are rows of the Background tasks card, and it returns if it is woken. (3) *The narrow strip.* The tabs not in view shrink to their icons, the rightmost first; then the tab in view shortens, its title cut with an ellipsis, to its minimum; only then does the strip scroll. - **2026-11-13 · U5's section brought to its first landing's revised document (U5a).** The document was revised once after its adversarial review (five findings, all accepted), and the six points its author reported (R1 to R6) stand at their defaults. (1) *What a sent comment says.* A message of two to twenty comments opens with the line `<N> comments, in the order made:`. Inside it a comment's line that begins with `<maws_anchor`, after any backslashes, is written with one more leading backslash and read with one fewer. A thread of several comments has a preamble in the plural, a single comment is written byte for byte as today, and the reader takes the framed reading only when the whole structure checks. Why: without the framing line, two comments on one element are byte for byte one older comment whose words hold a tag and a lead. Rejected: the lead-and-tag test first written, which read one old comment as two; a count of lines in each block's lead; an encoding of the comments, which the agent could not read plainly; one preamble for both forms. The sentence for the charter's X10 amendment names the framing. (2) *After a send.* "Sent to <title>" is said only for a confirmed delivery. An unconfirmed hand-off, which the terminal route can give, has its own line that says the comment may not have arrived, stays, and has one action, Show, to the session's pending send, which it finds by a key (one optional field in U4's `composer/submit/send-human-prompt.ts`). Rejected: reading an unconfirmed hand-off as sent; keeping the comments in the pass, which would make the card's Send a second authority for the same words; finding the pending send by its text. (3) Closing a document's tab drops its drafts and never a comment in flight. Rejected: withdrawing every thread of the pass at the tab's close. (4) `side.sendToMain` stays registered and uncalled, because removing a command a machine owns is a breaking change of the wire and would end the link to a machine on build F; its removal is logged as debt. Rejected: retiring it in this landing, as the section said. (5) Named insertions (UC-8): the count rule and one flash under `src/artifacts/` and the reply's pass through `src/engine-host/tool-server/documents.ts`, reviewed by U10, so a reply goes through the documents tool server; one handler and one message in `src/main/browser/comment/`, reviewed by U12. (6) The milestone of the landing that writes the transcript row waits for U3a on master. - **2026-11-13 · U11's default writer after the second probe pass: a file the engine skips marks no default, and "the form the engine writes" means the layout.** The check on engine `2.1.322` (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u11-user-defaults.txt`, on master since pull request #606) found that both keys are taken from the user file under MAWS's launch and in a terminal, so the item stands, and it corrected two sentences of the design merged the same day. (a) *The reader.* The design's reader answered key by key. The engine does not: a `permissions.defaultMode` it does not know makes it skip the whole user file, the model included, with no notice under MAWS's launch (the error is only in `get_settings.errors`) and with a blocking dialog in a terminal; and an unknown `model` is not checked and fails at the first request. So when the engine reports an error for the file, no default is marked, and the menu says the file has an error, in the place the design already gives a file MAWS cannot write. MAWS writes only the four known modes and only a model the engine listed. Given up: the answer per key, which would mark a default model the engine does not use. (b) *The writer.* The design said its output is byte for byte the form the engine writes. The engine's own write keeps every key and writes two-space JSON with a final newline, but it reorders the keys, its known keys first and then the others. So "the form the engine writes" means the layout, and MAWS's writer changes its two keys and leaves the others in the order it found them; it does not reorder as the engine does. The same pass also ran U5's check of a terminal session's comment text (intact, 12 of 12) and U11's check of the favicon route (it holds); those are check results in the two children's files.
- **2026-11-13 · U10's two fixes for writes nobody made, brought to their execution document (U10a): only a change to the content brings a repair, and the place to type under a final block is a gap caret.** The document was revised once, on two rulings of the architect's and its adversarial review (four findings and ten points, all adopted). (1) *The rule.* Only a change of the person's to the content brings a repair of the document. An open, a redraw, a focus, a selection and a caret move write nothing. Why: the owner's complaint is that looking at a document writes to it, and selecting text to copy is looking. Rejected: the section's first rule, which also counted a move of the selection and so failed its own A2; and a rule on the resulting selection, because a selection that touches the last block gives no caret under a chart, and a click under a final chart can resolve to earlier text. (2) *The mechanism.* The guard judges what a plugin answers: a selection-only answer passes, and an answer that changes the document needs a change of the person's in the batch. It reads every transaction at its root. The trailing paragraph waits out a composition, as built behaviour, and nothing is written when a composition ends. The mark `APP_MOVE` is withdrawn, having no reader. Why: the table plugin answers a selection with a selection, which must still pass; a plugin is handed transactions that other plugins appended, which carry nothing of their root but its mark; and under the rule the first change a person typing Korean makes in an agent's document is composed input. Rejected: refusing every answer to a batch without a change of the person's; reading each transaction by itself, which was measured writing the paragraph during composed input in a table; leaving the composition to a fallback after a red scene; a write at the composition's end, which no transaction of the person's would carry. The same document changes how the clean-up of l6-artifacts-07 (a) is switched off, by a listener and not by the flag; the entry "Check results, third batch" above is amended where it stands. (3) *Entry under a final block.* The editor takes ProseMirror's gap cursor (`gapCursor()` from `@tiptap/pm/gapcursor`, as a plain plugin). A click in the room under a final chart, table, rule, math block or diagram, or ArrowDown or ArrowRight from the selected block, shows a caret there and writes nothing; what the person types then makes one paragraph after the block in one row of theirs. With it come one line of bottom padding on the live editor's root, so that every live document page is one line longer at its foot (exports and the preview are unaffected), and `atom.ts` sending the caret to that gap when a field is left and nothing follows. Why: under the rule nothing writes the empty paragraph that used to be the place to type. Rejected: a paragraph made by a click under the last block (a write on a click, no route by keyboard, a hit test of MAWS's own); Tiptap's `Gapcursor` wrapper (a field on every node's spec); a click handler on the page's margin. (4) *Acceptance.* U10's file gains A11: under a final chart, a click shows a caret and writes nothing; what the owner types or composes there keeps the chart and lands after it. Its proof is the gap-caret scene in `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`. The owner looks at a screenshot of the caret, with the extra line named as its cost. Three debt rows are logged (U10's file, Waiting).
- **2026-11-13 · `show_pane` reloads a page the person typed in earlier and has left; protecting such a page was considered and withdrawn (U12, l9-browser-delivery-02).** On an address already open, `show_pane` reloads the tab unless the person is using it (`personUses`: input in that tab within three seconds, or the tab reads "You have the page"). A reload by the agent discards what was typed in the page, as any reload does, and a page typed in earlier and left is reloaded like any other. Why: the item exists so that a person who tested a page and asked for a fix sees the new page without reloading it themselves, and that person has almost always typed in the page. Rejected: keeping a page the person has pressed a key in since it loaded, which the second revision of U12a's execution document built on the architect's conditional ruling that main can tell it cheaply. Its cost: it made A2's first sentence false in the item's own main case; a key that types nothing kept the page too, while input without a key (a menu's paste, a chosen file, ticked boxes) was not protected; and an agent told that the page was kept would reload it through the plugin anyway. With it went a fifth row of the rule's table, a third note, a second optional key beside `page`, and one debt row. The wire's one minor carries one optional key, `page`, in `maws.pane.answer`.
- **2026-11-13 · U4's section brought to its first landing's document (U4a), to the second and third probe passes, and to the owner's four answers on the drawn example.** The execution document of U4's first landing was written against the code, reviewed three times and revised three times, and two further probe passes ran the cases of Send now that the first had left. Its author reported twenty-five points; U4's file, UC-3, UC-8, UC-9 and UC-14 now say what the document and the recordings found. (1) *What Send now delivers.* The request names one message, and the engine delivers with it every message that waits at the next step, as one user message under the last one's uuid; a turn's ordinary end joins them the same way. Each still has a `started` of its own, at which its row leaves the stack, and the transcript draws the one message the engine wrote. Rejected: retiring rows by the answer's `still_queued`, which lists every message that is about to go. (2) *What is moved.* In a session MAWS runs a foreground subagent call does not occur, so A3, the table and the charter's amendment sentence name an MCP call where they named it. A moved call reads "Moved … to the background after …" until its task ends, whatever the tool. (3) *A message at priority `now`* (Send now on a held or an after-the-turn row) goes alone, and moves a running shell command only once the engine has registered it as a task, about three seconds into the call; pressed earlier, the message is delivered when the command ends and its row reads "Next step" meanwhile. Nothing is built for the early case, which is what build F does today: it is a debt row, and sending such a row down the request's path is U4b's, after a probe. (4) *What reads "You stopped".* A stop counts only if it took effect. The end that a message sent now caused is marked by one optional field on X1's `turn.ended`, read from the frames the host already journals, and a message that was withdrawn leaves the set, as the Stop square withdraws it before it interrupts. Rejected: a record of the host's own, and emptying the set at any plain interrupt, which after the move read "You stopped" over a message that runs. (5) *Taking a message back* is stage, withdraw, load: the whole message is first saved as the session's stored draft, what became of it is read from the session's record and never from the withdrawal's answer alone, and whoever finds the stored copy carries the take-back forward. Rejected: withdrawing first and filling after, which had moments with no complete copy of the message. On a machine whose services are of an earlier build the draft store refuses the copy, and Edit and ↑ are refused there from that first press; an attachment past 4 MiB is in the box after the load and not in the stored draft, as today. (6) *Where Send now is not offered.* After an app update the earlier build's engine host can still serve a session; a next-step row's Send now is then drawn disabled with a sentence until that host has handed the session over. (7) *The transcript's files.* The filter reads a mark of the row's own (`waited` on the turn, `unread` on the row), set in the reducer; UC-3 names these insertions, and no milestone starts before master holds U3a. (8) *The command's grade* is `converse`, and the first landing declares the command unavailable over the machine link in U9's file, where U4b replaces the row with the offer. (9) *The owner's four answers* (2026-11-13, each the recommended one): the queue mark is the arrow under a bar; Stop stands on the strong fill; the `/btw` card lies over the pane's corner and, from U4b, at the column's foot; and the `/` list has MAWS's panels first. The last changes the order the section had, the engine's: engine 2.1.322 lists its eighteen bundled skills first, and MAWS's panels fell behind the cut at 40 rows. Eight things are logged as debt (U4's file, Waiting).
- **2026-11-13 · U2's section brought to its first landing's document (U2a), and U2 cut in two.** The execution document of U2's first landing was written against the code and the probe recordings, reviewed adversarially, revised twice, read once more within the changed parts and revised a last time; its author reported twelve points, then fourteen more at the revisions, and each is ruled. U2's file, UC-3, UC-8 and UC-13 now say what stands. (1) *Two landings.* U2a (ticket #587) is the runs, the thoughts and the turn's line: acceptance A1 to A11 and A17, and A18 where MAWS draws the plain transcript. U2b (ticket #608), after it, is the to-human view with UC-13's raise and the mirror: A12 to A16. Why: about seventy files together; two ways of proving (recorded sessions drawn by three routes, against a mod's answers on a live surface); and the raise to 9 with the removal of MAWS's own report is one change, wholly in U2b. Between the two every launch advertises 8 and the report is today's. (2) *The file reader reads a refused call's kind,* from the transcript row's `toolDenialKind`, through the live reader's function. The review found that a denied call ended a run live and was a failed member from the file, so A1 was false for it. The spec had given the mapping to U1c; it is built by U2a, which needs it first. (3) *A stopped call is a member of its run.* A call that reads denied or auto-denied stands alone, and so does `automode-unavailable`; `interrupted`, `cancelled` and a `user-rejected` call at a Stop are stopped members, counted `(N stopped)`. A `user-rejected` call reads stopped from its result on, and denied only when work of the model follows its result in the same turn (a block of another API message than the call's) or its turn closed otherwise than stopped. A permission card's Deny is a `permission-rule` denial and the turn goes on; "Deny and stop the turn" leaves what a Stop leaves and reads stopped. A stopped call's row reads one form on every family and route, `Stopped: ` and the call's subject, with no command beside a description and no duration. `wasInterrupted` gains one condition, so that the turn's line does not contradict the call on the file route. (4) *A waiting prompt's slot is not a boundary of a run,* and *a thought after a run's last call is that run's*: a run keeps one row and one id through a request, and a thought of under ten seconds between a run's last call and the turn's answer no longer has a row of its own, which is the owner's ten-second rule applied to the last thought of a run. (5) *Attention does not change on the file route:* one condition in U1's attention service, U2a's insertion. (6) Smaller: a running turn's line carries the turn's line counts beside `Working for …`, as the frame the owner looked at draws them; a call in the background never takes a run's line; a waiting permission that names no call of the turn leaves the run reading its caption; a caption part keeps one outcome per key, as Claude Desktop's builder does; an artifact's title is taken from the results in the loaded transcript, not from the library; A18 is read against the three measures that exist, the P5 phase having recorded no bound on mounted rows; the desk web app draws the new rows from its next deploy, and two of its e2e scenes' expectations are edited; the mod's main is 0.7.3. Replaced at the revisions: a `user-rejected` call read as denied and alone until its turn closed, which unmounted a run with calls in flight for the frames between the first rejected result and the turn's end; the diagnostic label kept on a stopped member, under which the file and the interrupted process's journal drew one call two ways; a stop-and-report if a refusal read from a file raised attention; and no line counts on a running turn's line. Rejected: every refused call standing alone, as today, under which `(N stopped)` would show only for a call that never got a result; reading a result's text to guess a kind; a second flush rule in the group machine for a run with an unfinished call, where carrying the slot past the open run is enough; `seq` alone as the mark of a wire event, since a rebuild from the transcript restamps it; and a library read for an artifact's title, which a pure model cannot await and which differs by machine. Three things are logged as debt by U2a (U2's file, Waiting).
- **2026-11-13 · U7's section brought to its execution document, in two landings (U7a, U7b).** The execution document of U7 was written against the code, reviewed adversarially (ten findings) and revised, and then brought once more to what a drawn example of its frames showed. (3) *When a hidden session returns.* The section's rule ("a record's turn start; a listing's `busy` state issued after it") is replaced. A session MAWS runs here returns when a turn of its began after the hide. For any other session nothing says when a turn began, so the window must have seen it not working since the hide before it counts it as working again, and that fact is stored with the entry. So a session hidden at rest returns at its next turn, and one hidden in the middle of a turn stays hidden through that turn. One limit remains: a session MAWS does not run here, hidden while it worked, that ends that turn and begins another while the window is closed stays hidden through that second turn, and a turn that both begins and ends while the window is closed is not seen. Why: a digest carries a turn's start only for a session MAWS runs, and a listing is signed again for a rename or a change of owners. Rejected: an observed change into `busy` kept in memory only, which a relaunch forgets; a rule on `issued` with exceptions for renames. (4) *The hidden store.* An entry of another machine's session is never dropped because the session is absent, since the rows that name a machine do not say that its listing is whole; the key is bounded at 500 entries instead, other machines' oldest first. Rejected: carrying the rows' completeness into the store, since absence on another machine is not evidence even then. (5) *Hide in the menu, and the Hidden row.* Hide stands directly after Pin, and after the opening items where a menu has no Pin, so that Archive and Delete stay last; the drawing showed it as the eleventh item, directly under "Delete conversation…". The Hidden row carries a chevron and its rows are indented one step, so that they read as its contents. Whether a row also carries a Hide button is with the owner, with the button recommended; the menu item is built in both answers. Amended the same day: the owner chose the button (the entry below on two more drawn examples). (6) *S-11's reach.* The cut is made on the record's title. A row that is only listed, and the desk's web view, are named by the listing's title, which the index does not write, so the six words reach a listed row only through U9's listing rule, for a session MAWS runs on that machine. Rejected: a second cut in the window, which cannot tell a prompt from a title the person chose. (7) *A page takes everything right of the sidebar,* the side column's place included: the side column is not drawn, nothing of it is written, and Back returns the tabs and the side column as they were. Replaced: a page beside a kept side column, which the drawing showed would leave the ring in mid-window with a blank strip beside it. What a page hides stays mounted, and behind a page nothing counts as on screen, while behind the dialog every session does. (8) *Not the person's act.* An agent's pane, the Browser following an agent, MAWS's ask sheet and a mod's pane close neither the page nor the dialog, and who asked travels as data with the request wherever the open is deferred. Rejected: a synchronous wrap alone, which an open queued behind the read of the layout escapes. (9) *The stored page after a stay on build F.* A retired Settings tab in front suppresses the stored page, and the test of the stored tabs is one of shape, not of who wrote them: after a stay on F that left a one-set layout with no retired tab in front, the page opens again and the person leaves it with Back. This is accepted and pinned by a test. Rejected: a witness or a marker stored to tell that case apart. (10) *Smaller.* The ring stands at the title bar's own right end; U7b removes Customize's and Projects' own headings, so a page's name stands once; the dialog's box is 960 px wide, at most 800 px tall and at most 85 percent of the window's height; in Settings › Usage the panel's title reads "Claude accounts"; View › Usage shows Settings › Usage while the dialog is open; an act on the side column leaves a page first. - **2026-11-13 · A model pick is the default for new sessions, and the default permission mode is set in Settings (U11, l8-settings-11).** Asked with the drawn Customize page where "Make … your default" should live, the owner chose none of the options and answered in their own words (2026-11-13; the register holds them verbatim): the model picker shows models only, with no "Default" entry; Claude Code making the last model the default is kept; for the permission mode Claude Code's default stays, and a person who wants another default sets it in Settings; the composer stays simple. What the code says: in a terminal, Enter in the engine's `/model` picker saves the pick to the Claude Code user settings ("Your pick becomes the default for new sessions"); the control protocol's `set_model` and `set_permission_mode` never write settings; so in MAWS today a pick lasts for the session only, a new session passes no model, and a session starts in the user's `permissions.defaultMode`, else in auto (charter D-18). The architect ruled on 2026-11-13: (a) the model menu lists the engine's models and has no "Default" row; (b) a pick is sent to the session as today and is also written to the Claude Code user settings (`model`) on the machine the session runs on, as the terminal's picker does, so it starts new sessions in MAWS and in the terminal alike, and the menu's line "For this session only" changes accordingly; (c) neither composer menu has a "make default" item; (d) the default permission mode is one control in Settings, which writes `permissions.defaultMode` (the owner's earlier answer, "Write Claude Code settings"); today's rule, that setting and else auto, stands, and D-18 is unchanged. Why: the owner's words, and one default for MAWS and the terminal, which the earlier answer chose. Rejected: MAWS keeping its own last pick and passing it at launch, which is Claude Desktop's way, because a model passed at launch would override a default a person fixed in their settings, and MAWS's default would then differ from the terminal's. The landing that builds it carries a dated sentence in the charter's Decision Log: MAWS writes a key of Claude Code's user settings, `model` on a pick and `permissions.defaultMode` from the Settings control, one key at a time. U11's item is rewritten to the ruling: the first design's mark "Your default", its "Make … your default" actions and its sentence for another machine are gone, and its writer and the writer's checks stand. The model menu is `widgets/ModelSelector.tsx`, U11's own file by UC-8's table, which U4a edits first; U11 no longer inserts in U4's mode menu. One row joins the table of rulings reversed, the "Default" entry of the model list. - **2026-11-13 · Source control becomes a page to read; a commit opens in Review; MAWS runs no git by hand (U5, l5-code-06).** The section that had moved Stage and Discard into Review was designed again after the owner's answer on the ask-the-agent buttons ("Source control is enough for read only"), drawn as an example of eleven frames, put to the owner with three questions, reviewed adversarially (seven findings and four points, all adopted) and merged into U5's file. The owner's answers, verbatim (2026-11-13): what may still be done by hand to a session's checkout from MAWS, "Nothing (Recommended)"; how much the page says about the branch's commits, "List, and rows open", which was the second option and not the recommended one; where a pull request is merged or closed, "On GitHub (Recommended)". The design: Source control is one page of four parts in a fixed order (where the work is; what is uncommitted; the branch's commits, under "Not pushed" and "Pushed to …"; the pull request with its checks by name), and it changes nothing in the repository. A click on a commit's row opens that commit in Review as a scope of its own, against its first parent. The commit form, the Push and Open PR sheets, and Stage, Unstage and Discard leave the window, and Review's file header gets no menu and no "Staged" badge. Two new reads of `git.*` at grade `view` serve it, the branch read (shared with l5-code-03's base) and the commit read, under one capability inserted through U9's seams. A comment on a commit's diff names the commit: X10's `file-line` anchor gains `commit`. A base that moves alone (a fetch, with `HEAD` and the upstream unchanged) is followed when the page or "This branch" comes into view and within two minutes while either is in view. Why: the owner's sentence and answers; a change made by hand leaves the agent with a picture of the files that is no longer true, where a line comment and a receipt's Undo do not; and the list of commits is what makes the page worth opening. Rejected: "Discard changes…" kept in Review's menu, the forms folded behind one closed line, and the forms as today (the owner's first answer); a list whose rows open nothing, and counts only (the second); "Merge…" and "Close…" in MAWS behind a sheet (the third; Claude Desktop has the Merge); a report from the watcher when the base ref moves, which needs a ref the watcher is told by a read, a git run of its own per ref event and a new event through the machine link's feed; new fields on `git.log` and `git.diff` in place of two new commands, because an older daemon's tolerant reader would drop them and answer something else; and removing now the ten `scm.*` commands the window stops calling, which takes a wire major, so they stay served and a tracker row holds their removal. The item reverses the charter's scope line for the Source control panel and the acting half of E6's design (Rulings reversed). UC-7, UC-8's table, U8's and U9's files and the register follow; whether the item rides in U5b's pull request or follows the stack as a third is decided by the author of U5b's execution document.

Why: the owner's words name it ("Some user might want to make Bypass permission as default, and let that happen in settings"). Rejected: only the modes the engine's own panel offers. Until its fourth milestone replaces the window's helper, the six acts its first milestone takes out of the declared set stay refused for a session on another machine from a list of the helper's own: a seam of the build, not a rule of the design. A pane's answer for a session on another machine is carried through a host handover by the completion route the drain's other replies take, to the host that raised the request while its link is open, and as an ordinary op to the next host served otherwise; why: an answer that can be lost is a defect of the landing that offers it, and the route already exists. (7) *U1a.* An asynchronous question, which has a kind of its own, does not raise the floating window's pill, and the sidebar's hover card offers Quick Reply beside it; both are accepted as built, with neither file edited, and the owner looks at them on the built window. - **2026-11-13 · U13a's build: one definition of an empty chat, what a refused discard puts back, and a tab's place drawn readable or not at all (U13, O-01).** (1) A chat is empty only when it has no content anywhere content can wait: no message, no draft, no draft write in flight, and no prefill and no attachment queued for its composer. Every caller reads the one function: the New chat reuse, the close of the other empty chats and the relaunch discard. Why: U13a's branch review found that a discard could remove text the person had just saved. (2) A refused discard puts back the tab and its pin; an unpinned chat returns at the end of the strip, which is accepted and recorded as a tracker row. Why: a refusal is rare once a draft write in flight counts as content. Rejected: restoring the tab's position. (3) A tab's place in a strip that mixes places is drawn readable or not at all: it gives way before the title, and below the width that holds its first four characters and the ellipsis it is not drawn. Why: the one-strip scene's screenshots showed places squeezed to one letter and an ellipsis. U13a's residue on the board is #620: a tool pinned to the session-surfaces icon row cannot be pinned or unpinned while the tab in front is not a session's. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-13 · When a hidden session returns, as U7a builds it (U7, O-16).** The rulings on U7a's third milestone and on its branch review. A row under Needs you carries no Hide, since such a session stays listed there whatever is hidden and the act would read as doing nothing. A hide is never armed from a terminal session's bridge that has given no snapshot yet, and while that session's state stream reconnects the hide keeps what the last snapshot said; main does this (`src/services/spine-root.ts`, an accepted insertion of U7a in a file of the session that keeps master; no wire minor), because the window cannot tell a bridge's first fold from a real idle. One limit is recorded: a Hide made while a terminal session's state stream reconnects comes back at the end of that interval if a turn began inside it. A lead whose end is read from the transcript ends at its quiet deadline; the Hidden rows keep the order held while the person works in the sidebar; and a returning row that holds the keyboard keeps it, and stays drawn while it does, past the five and the eight. Rejected: clearing a bridged session's state while its view is not fresh, which would turn every bridged row to `foreign`, with no waiting count, through every reconnect, to close a case that needs a reconnect, a turn begun inside it and a Hide inside it at once.
- **2026-11-13 · U3a's build: the first carrier stands, a scheduled fire is not held, and an answer stays in view until U1b (U3, UC-2).** (1) Under one message id the first carrier stands: a later non-empty carrier is another form of the same arrival and changes nothing; an empty list withdraws it, and the next carrier then places it anew; a list of unknown entries alone does neither. U3's file and its X1 amendment sentence say so. Why: the live caller never places again without a withdrawal first, so "the later standing" served no route, and it moved a line into the other form's turn when a session is read in both forms. Rejected: "the later standing", as written; a rule that depends on which form a carrier came from, which the event does not say. (2) A terminal session's scheduled fire places its line and opens the engine's turn at its own row, and the meta row after it continues that turn. Why: a held fire was lost at every input boundary after the first. Rejected: a flush at every boundary, one more lifecycle per caller. (3) Until U1b fills the question's row, an answer notice stays among the rows a folded turn keeps in the to-human report; it is handed over to U1b with a tracker row. Why: the landing must not take away what today's build shows. Rejected: accepting the gap between the two landings. (4) A command the sminos bridge ran draws as the person's command bubble, since the engine writes the same row; it is residue for U3b's O-18 and on the list of the owner's looks. (5) On the file route a terminal's command output stands directly under its command's bubble; the routes' test states the live command name against the file's (`/cost` against `/usage`); notices that only the file holds each draw in their own turn when a session is read in both forms; and a turn that a fire, a task or a hand-back opened carries its origin word, which releases the queue by the existing rule. (6) U9's A4 says a remote transcript can be searched during an outage; the pane has no find today, and the find bar is U3b's, so U3b proves that part of A4.
- **2026-11-13 · One phase rule for every row of a machine (U9, O-39).** Main greyed a machine's Needs you items at the link's loss, which also took them out of the count and the dock badge, so every short loss of the link removed a pending approval from the badge, against U9's sentence "while it connects or reconnects nothing is greyed". Ruled: one phase function, shared by main and the window (it moves under `src/shared/`); an item is greyed, and leaves the count and the badge, exactly when its machine reads offline or removed; while the machine reconnects the item is counted and reads "…, reconnecting". The location card's "offline since" follows the same phase. Also from U9's build: a listing keeps the title it reported when the record's only title is the one the index filled from the first prompt; every failure to load a conversation reads "Couldn’t load this conversation", with its reason in the tooltip; and Enter sends nothing while the machine is offline. - **2026-11-13 · U8 after U8b's build and U8c's document (UC-4, UC-14).** (1) A background task's type is read through one function in both of the engine's vocabularies, a listing's task type and the labels of the frames after a Stop, for the cards, their counts and the panels. (2) An agent or task of a session on another machine is drawn ended when that machine says the session is gone, its presence going to no record while the machine's lease holds; an offline machine supplies nothing, and a live snapshot or a returning record clears it. (3) l5-code-14 is two landings: U8c-1 (#621) repairs three faults of today's build at the moment the engine moves a transcript (a terminal session's Background tasks stop changing, an open transcript of one of its agents stops reading, the session is told removed for an instant), and U8c-2 (#593) makes the card and the code surfaces follow the agent. U8c-2's storage is additive: the record's `directory`, `worktree` and `branch` keep the earlier build's meaning, and the launch folder and the agent's folder are two optional facts read through one selector, so UC-4 and U8's file no longer say that the record's launch folder stops moving. Rejected: keeping `directory` at the launch folder, which broke build F's readers of a transcript's path on the way back. - **2026-11-13 · U12a's branch review: what counts as evidence for the computer-use line, and the person's act wins over the agent's tab (U12, l9-browser-delivery-07 and -01).** The review panel's seven findings were accepted, as one fix wave. (1) The line that stays after a Stop has no timer and leaves on evidence or at Dismiss, and where the evidence can be read two ways the line stays. A later call's result is evidence only when it shows that call's cell ran (an error result, and the plugin's answer to an empty source, are not); an interruption marker withdraws only the calls it cut; evidence is read from the transcript at each look and is not kept from an earlier one; and the process is gone only when a datum says it exited. Why: a line that stays until Dismiss is the lesser fault, and a line cleared while a step may still run the greater one. (2) The agent's Browser tab comes forward "unless I use mine", and also not while a Browser panel in any window shows any tab of that session's browser; an opening that waits for main's answer is dropped when the person adds or removes the Browser tool, or changes the shown session, in the meantime; and the tool opens only when the window's own list holds the tab the answer names. Rejected: a stamp of the person's input on the chrome's selection.

- **2026-11-13 · U6c's adversarial review: what "names" means, what is remembered, one queue, and the stated bound (U6, O-37).** The review of U6c's execution document returned four findings, all accepted. (1) A path is named exactly when the transcript writes it, whole, in a form the window makes a file click from (a link's destination, a read's or an edit's file chip, an anchored comment's path), in the agent's text, the person's message, a tool's input, an MCP call's result or an edit's result; the daemon applies the click's own decoding and compares the whole result. (2) A remembered hit is proven again from its row before it is used, and a remembered miss carries a fingerprint of the bytes before it. (3) Every search goes through one queue per daemon: one search at a time, at most eight waiting. (4) U6's file states what the check stops and what it does not, in place of "so the window cannot ask for other files", which said more than the bound gives and was corrected to the owner. Also ruled: MAWS's instruction sentence that a link opens a file inside the session's folders stays as it is in this wave, because it holds on every machine; U6b's design revisits it. Why: the rule exists so that a click on a file link opens the file, so nothing a person can click is refused by it and nothing wider is admitted. Rejected: a test for a boundary on both sides of the path, the document's first rule, which admitted other files (a link to `/vault/.env backup` named `/vault/.env`, and `host:/vault/key` named the local `/vault/key`).
- **2026-11-13 · A plan answer's mode is guarded where the services forward it (U1, l4-approvals-11).** An answer that names the mode after the plan is refused at the services' forwarding boundary, for every caller, from what the daemon already holds about the engine-host link it is about to send on: refused when the host served is of another wire, and refused, not held for a successor, when no host is serving. The card offers the choice only where the engine host is this build's, and that stays the copy a person reads. An answer that names no mode is unchanged. Why: a guard only in the window's port left every other caller able to send a mode to a host that drops it, and the plan was then implemented in the mode the session had before, which can be wider. Rejected: a status request before the send, which U1a's delta review found could check one host and send to another, inside a second request budget.
- **2026-11-13 · The computer-use line keeps every withdrawn call it showed (U12, l9-browser-delivery-07).** As built in U12a's fix wave, at the controller's ruling that the architect accepted: the line keeps each withdrawn call it showed with the calls after it as last read, refreshed at each look; it names the latest one that no later call's evidence resolves and leaves when none is left; only the process gone, the server gone and Dismiss settle every call at once. Why: the earlier calls were first also settled for good when a later shown call was withdrawn, which the review's ruling did not allow. Rejected: rebuilding the line from the current reading alone, under which a later turn with no computer-use call would bring the line back for a cell that is over.
- **2026-11-13 · An agent's waiting request is said once on its row (U8, #359).** Ruled in the owner's place on U8b's seventh milestone: there is no "waiting" badge in the title; the row's one line of state reads "Approval needed" in the warning colour, in the Subagents panel and on its card alike. A folded parent does not say that an agent nested under it waits; accepted, with a tracker row. The owner was told on 2026-11-13. Why: a row has one line of state, and the owner's standing position is that a fact is not drawn twice. Rejected: the badge beside the title, which stated the same fact twice.
- **2026-11-13 · U8c-2's third round, and U11a's branch review (U8, l5-code-14; U11, l8-settings-03 and -06).** (1) U8c-2: a fact is written only for a folder that passes the admission's test at the write; a linked work tree counts only when its git directory names its `.git` file back; the admission's answer is not cached; `settings.reveal` and `session.revealPath` are not widened. - **2026-11-13 · A landing's final Mac runs (UC-10).** A landing's final Mac run set is every scene group of its first full set, on the final code; a group's earlier run stands only when the diff from the head it ran on to the final head touches no file that group reads, and the pull request says so with the diff's stat. Ruled by the session that keeps master, after a pull request reached it with one group run only before the fix wave. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected. The same day that session made the test concrete for a run made before a merge of master (three tests on the merge's incoming change and the branch's own, stated in UC-10), and the wave's next landings were ordered as a chain, each branch holding the one before it, so that each pays its whole Mac set once. - **2026-11-13 · An older engine host's task end keeps one line, and yields to the same task's own end (U3, O-28, O-34).** Ruled on the review of U3's first landing, which is on master (pull request #643). A background task's end told by an older engine host, which sends no arrival, keeps one line in the transcript and raises nothing in attention. The wave's build marks every task notification it writes, and the line is drawn only for one without the mark. Amended the same day: the line is not drawn while the window holds the same task's own end as an arrival, the same task id and a stated outcome, in either order; a monitor's event arrivals do not count; a task with no start in its turn counts as a background task. Why: sessions stay with the older host after an update until they restart, and removing the error card (O-34) had removed the only place such a session showed a failed background task; and at the handover of a launch adopted at update time, an older host's notification followed by this build's arrival for the same completion drew two lines. Rejected: telling an older host by inference over the whole session, which failed both ways (a window loaded from the middle of a history read as an older host's, and an older host's span lost its lines once one arrival of this build followed); the serving host's identity, which is wrong for a span an older host wrote. Accepted, with tracker rows: an end carrier with no message id does not suppress an older host's line, and an older host's foreground end whose start was lost draws one line.
- **2026-11-13 · The footer's "Archived sessions…" sheet reaches every machine (U9, S-02).** Ruled on U9's fourth milestone: the sheet lists this Mac's own row first, then one section per other machine under its name, asked when the sheet opens and each with its own state; the machine row's own menu entry stays. Why: archiving another machine's last listed session of no project removed that machine's row, whose menu was the only way to its archived sessions, so Archive had no undo within reach. - **2026-11-13 · ⇧⌘J and the Overview behind a page, as U7b builds them (U7, O-40).** Ruled on U7b's fifth milestone. ⇧⌘J does not take a session behind a page for the current one: when that session waits it is opened first, which leaves the page. Opening the Overview over a page writes nothing of the side column, so Back shows the column as it was; the Overview is never over a main region that a maximized or floating column covers, so the column is docked when the page is left with the Overview still open. Why: both are the rule already ruled for acts behind a page (nothing behind a page counts as on screen, and a page writes nothing of the side column), applied to one more reader. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-13 · A terminal beside the chat opens wide (U8, O-09).** The owner's answer "Wide, like Review (Recommended)": a terminal in the side column is in the wide class with Review, Files and the browser, so it opens at 60 % of the room right of the sidebar and its dragged width is remembered with theirs; Subagents and Background tasks keep opening at 360 px. Rejected: keeping 360 px, with 46 columns until the terminal is dragged.
- **2026-11-13 · The Environment card after a move: the owner's three answers (U8, l5-code-14).** On a drawn example of the card after an agent moved into a worktree, the owner chose the recommended option of each of three questions. "Cut at the start": a folder path that does not fit its row loses its start and keeps its tail, with the whole path in the tooltip, for every session's card, moved or not. "Under the folder row": the muted line "moved from <the launch folder's name>" sits directly under the folder row, above the branch row. "In the folder row": the row shows the agent's own folder, the working folder or the sub-folder of it the agent is in, and changes in place, so the card keeps its height; there is no line for a sub-folder, and a sub-folder of the launch checkout is not a move. - **2026-11-13 · A diff tab after a move starts again from its own ref (U8, l5-code-14).** Ruled on the review of U8c-2's fourth milestone, as built: after the working folder changes, a diff open in a tab starts again from its own ref (its scope, its turn and its file) in the new folder, from the top; where that file has no change there, the tab shows the new folder's list with its first file selected, with nothing of the old folder and no error. - **2026-11-13 · U6c's build: the window's URL step, the results that wait, `maws-path:`, and a session with no folder left (U6, O-37).** Ruled on U6c's first two milestones. (1) A test that holds the window and the rule together found that the window parses a path link's destination as a URL before the click reads it, a step the rule did not have. A destination is named only when the same reading of the URL-serialised destination gives the same path, and nothing is named when the parse fails; the two paths are compared, not the two strings. Why: every named path is then the plain reading of what the transcript wrote and a parser can only take a name away, so a difference between two URL parsers can refuse an open and cannot admit one. Rejected: mirroring the window's serialisation, which lets the parser's reading of a host, a port and a user field decide what is named; and refusing every destination that begins with `//` or holds `?` or `#`, which refuses `/work/notes#1.md`, where nothing is wrong. (2) At most 256 tool results wait for their call's record, the newest staying; an older one is not waited for, which fails towards the refusal. Rejected: no cap. (3) A written `maws-path:` destination, which the window draws as a path link, stays refused and joins the list for U6b's design. Rejected: admitting it in U6c, a form no agent is told to write. (4) A session whose folders no longer resolve still opens a file its transcript names, as it already opens a delivered file; the owner's answer "Files the transcript names" covers it.
- **2026-11-13 · U6c's branch review: the rule names only what the window draws, and its work is bounded (U6, O-37).** The review panel found six faults, fixed in one wave, and the fixes made the rule read Markdown as the window does, by hand. Ruled: (1) one seeded differential test asserts one direction, that every path the rule names is a link the window draws, and a counterexample is fixed by refusing; the other direction, a link the window draws and the rule refuses, is a tracker row. With it the admission of a link inside a code fence, ruled the same day on the document's review, is reversed: a link in code, in `$$` math, inside another link's or an image's text, in an autolink, after a literal web address or in a footnote's definition names nothing. Why: a path with no link cannot be clicked, so admitting it gave the person nothing and widened what the daemon opens. Rejected: more rounds of review by example, which did not converge. (2) The scan's work is bounded by construction: one count of work per text, a pause at fixed amounts of it, a cap on every helper, and no pattern with a quantifier over unbounded input; a text that costs more than its bound names nothing further. Rejected: one fix per slow input, which leaves the next. (3) Not built: freeing the one search when a call on the file system never returns; a file outside the folders then reads as not openable until the daemon restarts. Why: each abandoned call holds one of the daemon's few file system threads, and one stuck search is the smaller failure. (4) Kept, though they reach files inside the folders: a change event that changed no byte no longer shows "Updated" on an image (a PDF still shows it after every change event), and a file tab whose last read failed is read again when it is shown again.
- **2026-11-13 · Readings of the landing test, each link's `default`, and three rules for scenes (UC-10).** Ruled by the session that keeps master. (1) Three readings narrow which runs a merge of master sends again: a change confined to e2e spec files counts for the groups that list them; a change confined to one harness page counts for the groups that open that page; a change confined to unit test files under `src/` counts for class 1a and `unit:darwin`. Each has its conditions in UC-10 and is shown in the pull request. (2) Every link of the chain runs `e2e:default` on its merged head, whatever the tests say. (3) R1: a landing that adds or edits a spec runs each group that lists it once on the project's own Mac runner as well as where the group usually runs. R2: a scene settles on a state the app shows. R3: a failure that matches an existing tracker row, on a scene the branch does not touch, is named and run again once, and a second red or a new signature stops the link. Why, for R1 and R2: #639 showed two scenes green in their groups and red in `e2e:default`, one of them failing only on the project's own runner, and both settled on time or on a read made too early. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-13 · Where U7a and U1a meet in a terminal session's listener (UC-8, `spine-root.ts`).** Ruled by the wave's architect at the chain merge, on the one hunk the merge could not settle by keeping both sides: the digest's phase and tool come from a current snapshot only (U7a), and its waiting count is what the attention list holds, at all times (U1a, UC-1). Inside a reconnect the digest keeps the last snapshot's phase and tool, and the count for the list's five-second grace, then 0, as the list reads; a tracker row records that a session may then still wait in its terminal. - **2026-11-14 · A moved call's line is decided by a fact on the call, not by its label's words (U4, l3-input-01).** U4a's first Mac runs showed a moved shell call reading as an ordinary one: its row drew the command's description and never the "Moved … to the background" words. Ruled: the call's cell carries the move as a fact of its own and the row reads that fact, with one condition in U2's row component (a named insertion, UC-8). Why: a reworded or translated label would silently bring the description back. Rejected: reading the label's opening words. Two tracker rows came with the same fix wave: an open `/` or `@` list does not follow a change of the panels until the next keystroke, and the transcript view's import cycle, which a test that loads the view must wait out.
- **2026-11-14 · U7b's first Mac runs: an empty search field passes Esc on; a sheet over Settings; Settings' body under always-visible scroll bars (U7, O-40).** (1) An empty search field passes Esc to the dialog or the page that holds it, so ⌘, and then one Esc closes Settings; with text the first Esc clears the field; an Esc during an input method's composition stays the field's; every other text field keeps its rule. Why: Settings opens with the keyboard in its empty search field, and two presses were needed to close it. Rejected: no autofocus in Settings, which takes the caret away from search. (2) Settings' Esc yields while a sheet drawn after it is open, so one Esc no longer closes both; an Esc within the sheet's opening frames is dropped, accepted with a tracker row. (3) Where scroll bars are always visible, the bar's width comes out of the dialog's right inset, so the body keeps its 760 px. Why: at 745 px a note wrapped, every time Settings opened, for a person with a mouse and no trackpad. Rejected: a dialog 15 px wider; a fixed inset with a capped column, which changes narrow windows under overlay bars; accepting 745 px.
- **2026-11-14 · What became of U6c's list for U6b's design (U6, O-37).** Ruled on U6b's cut: a `file://` link the transcript writes names its path, since the transcript writes the path whole; a relative link never names a file outside the folders, because the rule has no folder to resolve it against; a link in a tool result drawn as "generic", in a side question's answer and in a mod's own text stays refused; a written `maws-path:` destination stays refused. Rejected: building the refusal of the agent's open until the answer, which would have changed what a person sees; and resolving a relative link in the daemon against a folder that moves with the agent.
- **2026-11-14 · An artifact mention is a link, and the agent opens no artifact (U6, O-44; reverses X9).** The owner typed "MAWS preview shouldn't be limited to PDF, and agent's artifact mention opens in the side panel." and then answered three cards: the link's look, "As Claude Desktop (Recommended)"; a mention of an artifact already open as a main tab, "Show that tab (Recommended)"; what the agent may still open by itself, "Keep all six (Recommended)". Ruled on U6d's document the same day: the link is a `span` with the role of a button, not a `<button>`, so that a long title wraps as prose does; one tooltip that is true in every case, "Opens the artifact · ⌘-click opens it in a tab"; "Show that tab" covers a mention only; with the artifact open in both places the main tab comes forward and the side column is left as it is, a maximized or floating column docking first. - **2026-11-14 · Word, PowerPoint and Excel files are previewed in a sandboxed frame (U6, l6-artifacts-01).** On the owner's typed words and two answers on drawn examples, "Yes, as drawn (Recommended)" for the look and "Compute and say so (Recommended)" for Excel's totals. Each format is drawn read-only by a public library in a sandboxed frame of the window, in a process of its own, with no way out to the network or the app; the host was ruled by the wave's architect after a containment spike (`spikes/viewer-frame/FINDINGS.md`) and rests on five settings in the main process, one of them a proxy that reaches nothing on the window's session. The order a person meets: the artifact link; U6b's part; Word, with the host and its guard, as a delicate change; PowerPoint; Excel after a qualification of its calculator, which can also end in no Excel preview. Ruled after the design's revision: the guard test on the window's session has five failures, and the permission refusal for the viewer's scheme and an opaque origin is required. Four proofs are owed from a live run on a Mac before the Word landing merges. Why: a file an agent wrote on the owner's machine should not leave it to be looked at, and MAWS has no conversion service. Rejected: a separate web view with a session of its own, which added no containment and costs a placement and cover protocol; conversion by an installed LibreOffice, which cannot be assumed; macOS Quick Look; a table that never computes for Excel; trusting the Excel engine without the three rules.
- **2026-11-14 · U6b is cut into five landings (U6).** U6b-1, a file opens beside the chat; U6b-2, a PDF, an HTML file and media by a path; U6b-3, Files shows what changed, and Go to file; U6b-4, every form of link, a refusal that says why, and "Open in"; U6b-5, the rest of the keyboard. - **2026-11-14 · Connector switches are scopes, carried by the engine's switch per project (U11, #476; l8-settings-04, l8-settings-03).** The owner's answers of that day, verbatim. Asked where a per-chat connector switch should sit, the owner chose none of the options and typed: "two switches mean that a connector can be installed like plugin -- user scope and project scope." Asked which behaviour a connector with many tools needs: "Off by default, on per project (Recommended)". Told that the mechanism that card described, an allow list by name, cannot name a connector a plugin brings, and asked which mechanism should carry the choice: "Engine's project switch (Recommended)". What the two switches mean together: "Last press wins (Recommended)". What the connectors already there start as on the first day: "On for all projects (Recommended)". So a connector is on for all projects, on for one project, or off, and a new one starts off; the one on/off is the engine's own list per project, which a terminal session reads too; "All projects" is an instruction MAWS carries out place by place through the chats it starts; there is no switch for one chat, and the dialog's switch stays the project's. Why: the engine's project switch works for every kind of connector, both ways, in a running session, keeps the sign-in, and is the same switch in a chat, in a terminal session and in the older build. Rejected: a deny at launch for the chats MAWS starts alone; an allow list by address; a third position by which a project follows "All projects"; connectors off, or asked about once, on the first day. The architect's condition on the first-day answer, not the owner's words: account connectors are not governed until a check on a machine signed in to an account.
- **2026-11-14 · The review's rulings on the connector design (U11, #476).** Ruled on the adversarial review of the design and on the second probe pass. `session.mcpToggle` keeps exactly today's effect, one session and what the engine persists for the place; other ready chats are told only by the machine's own acts, which no other machine can call. MAWS's record holds explicit intent only (what a person pressed, and where it has been carried out): nothing a process reported about itself, and a flip made outside MAWS is left alone until the next press on that connector in MAWS. A plugin whose connectors cannot be read at the first run is resolved once, at the first session that lists them. One resolver gives a folder's place, fixed for a linked worktree and unidentified, with the switches disabled and the reason said, where it cannot be proven. The first message of a launch is held until each toggle is confirmed, for at most 5 seconds, and the hold, a failure and a timeout are each said in the chat. MAWS never edits the engine's configuration file. Why: the first design inferred a person's choice from what a session had been seen to hold, which invented choices and could overwrite a waiting press; and an act graded for one session must not change another. Rejected: the inference; a fan-out behind the single-session act.
- **2026-11-14 · A custom connector is added in Customize and is Claude Code's own (U11, l8-settings-05; built as U11g).** The owner's answers of that day, verbatim. Where "Add custom connector…" writes: "Claude Code's config (Recommended)". Whether the form's value fields show what is typed: "Plain text (Recommended)". How the servers already in Settings › MCP servers move: "On a press (Recommended)". So the form is one sheet in Customize for a remote server with request headers or a local command with arguments and environment, written by one run of the engine's own `claude mcp add-json` at the scope chosen; adding in Settings is retired, and an entry of the old list keeps being passed at launch until a person moves it with a press that shows what would change first. Ruled on U11g's review: the secrecy rule is about value slots, a line of the engine's CLI is forwarded whole or not at all, and the name is checked again where the act runs. With U11g the Connectors dialog's head reads "A switch here is for this chat, and every session started in <place> afterwards." in place of "Off here is off for every session in <place>.", which claimed sessions that are already open. Why: one place adds a connector and every session has it; an old entry wins over a configured connector of the same name, so moving it unasked could change which server a chat reaches. Rejected: keeping MAWS's own list as the place to add; masked value fields with a reveal control; moving the old entries at the first start; replacing a value inside a line of the CLI's output; a fallback to `claude mcp add`.
- **2026-11-14 · A plugin's connectors are a landing of their own, with a restated sign-in guarantee (U11, X-184; U11h).** X-184 was cut out of the Customize landing after the review of that landing's document and a probe of plugin connectors: the engine names a plugin's connector by the plugin's name and the server's name alone, so more than one plugin can build one name, and the engine keeps one and says nothing. Ruled on the review of U11h's document: a press on a plugin's row never opens the browser for, and never reports a sign-in on, a server whose address when the engine answered is outside the address the row declares, nor while the session's report of its plugins shows no owner, another owner or more than one possible owner of the name; the check runs at the press, immediately before the request and before the browser is opened. What an unmodified engine does not let MAWS close, the engine reconciling its plugins by itself between two checks, is named as a residue and not hidden. Rejected: the first version's sentence that a press can never reach another plugin's server, which the design could not enforce; asking the engine to reload its plugins at the press, since no record shows that a reload leaves running connectors and their sign-ins alone.

- **2026-11-15 · Fork from here around a typed command and around a compaction (U3, l2-transcript-07).** R17, on a probe of the engine's rewind (thirteen forks, on the probes' branch of U3b): a typed command's bubble is a prompt of the owner's for placing an answer's action row, and Fork under the answer before it is cut at that bubble, so the fork ends with that answer. A command's output is never such a prompt and never the message forked from, whichever of the two is drawn first. No Fork is drawn under an answer whose next prompt lies above the transcript's last compaction boundary; the answer directly above a typed `/compact` keeps Fork; and, ruled on the third milestone, the answer before the prompt that set off an automatic compaction draws no Fork. Why: the engine accepts the id of a typed command's own row and refuses a command's output and anything from before a compaction; after an automatic compaction the prompt that set it off stands above the boundary, and a fork cut at the prompt after the compacted turn would hold a turn the person did not ask for. Rejected: cutting at the next typed prompt, which keeps commands run after the point forked from; reading the id of the engine's caveat row from the file, a second source for one id.
- **2026-11-15 · A compaction in progress is a row at the transcript's end, and what ends it (U3, #331).** Ruled without a card at U3b's execution document: "Compacting session…" is one row at the end of the transcript, with the elapsed time, and no turn line's words change. Why: the section put the words on the "Working for" line "at the transcript's end", and that line is the turn line at the head of its turn. Ruled on the fourth milestone, for a terminal session's compaction asked from MAWS: besides the bridge's `end`, the row ends at a compaction boundary that arrives in a live batch, is the newest in drawn order and was not already held, told by the boundary row's identity and never by a clock; and it is cleared wherever the session's watch ends, resets or is released, or the bridge is neither live nor starting. Why: a time comparison sets the transcript machine's clock against this window's, and a row that outlived its source would have nothing able to end it. Rejected: "later than the start" by time; a first-batch baseline for which boundaries are known.
- **2026-11-15 · Edit above a compaction: only while it works (U3, O-02; ticket 698).** Two probes on the probes' branch of U3b showed that a rewind in place to a message from before a compaction is accepted by the engine process that compacted and refused after that process restarts. The session that keeps master ruled it a fault a person sees and folded it into U3's landing of find and the keys. The owner, asked on the card "Edit, compact", answered "Only while it works (Recommended)": Edit above a compaction is offered only until that session's engine restarts. The session that keeps master added the condition that the window must know the engine process is the one that compacted, and draws no Edit there otherwise. Rejected by the answer: "Never above a compaction", the default until the card was answered; "Always, with a reason".

- **2026-11-15 · A subagent's request stays through Stop, and what "delivered as a message" means while a question waits (U1, l4-approvals-08).** Ruled on U1b's recorded runs. (1) A request's card leaves when the engine settles or cancels that request, or when the subagent that raised it ends, exactly as the engine reports it. Stop withdraws the main agent's request; a subagent's request is not withdrawn by Stop, its card stays and still answers, and the window never withdraws a request the engine still holds. Why: the engine keeps a subagent's request live and answerable after Stop, so withdrawing its card would hide a request whose answer still runs the tool. (2) A message sent while a blocking question waits is taken as a message and waits as any message sent during a turn waits, in the stack above the composer by U4's rules; it reaches the agent with the answer or when the turn ends, and the question's form does not leave, lose its draft or change. Why: the engine queues the message and replays it after the answer's result. No new word and no new surface. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The floating card with a request at its end (U1, l4-approvals-08).** Ruled on two scenes of U1b's built card that failed, both faults of the product. (1) While the card holds a request at its transcript's end, the transcript's region inside the card is at least 200 px tall and the card's body scrolls. Why: at the card's smallest size none of the request's buttons could be reached. (2) The line "Waiting for you · N" above the compact composer opens the card with the keyboard on the request, not in the card's composer. Why: the card's composer took the keyboard as it mounted, after the request's row had been given it. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · Approve and retry as U1c builds it: sent as Enter sends, and approved only once read (U1, l4-approvals-12).** Rulings on U1c's document, its probes and its milestone reviews. (1) During a turn the approval goes at the agent's next step, as Enter sends; on a session MAWS runs on another machine the press sends as Enter does there, a send with no turn running and a steer at the next step with one, and the waiting approval keeps that link's rules for a steer. Why: in recorded runs a grant written at the next step was honoured 27 times of 27, joined with another waiting message or not. Rejected: every approval sent for after the turn, the document's first choice while no recording showed a grant honoured at the next step. (2) A press is answered only by an approval line that stands after the blocked row that was pressed. Why: a count of every approval of the command was raised by an older page that held an earlier approval, and by a folded turn. (3) An approval appended into a terminal session's running turn waits from its delivery to the next result of a tool call of the main agent, or to the next turn when the turn ends or is stopped first; a result an Esc rejected is not that boundary. Why: the row read approved one tool call before the engine had read the approval. Only approvals wait so in U1c; the same rule for every message sent into a terminal session's running turn is #706. (4) U1c offers the button on a plain shell command only, although recorded runs honoured a grant that names another tool's call 6 times of 6: how the whole input of such a call is shown beside its button is a design of its own, a later landing (#693). Where no other alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The owner's answers on Approve and retry (U1, l4-approvals-12).** Three cards, each answered with the recommended option. - **2026-11-15 · The words of a failed act on a waiting message of another machine's session (U4, l3-input-04; UC-7).** Asked which words begin the line when Edit, Delete, ↑ or Send now on a waiting message cannot be done on another machine, the owner chose "Taken back / Sent now (Recommended)": "Not taken back: <reason>" for Delete, Edit or ↑, and "Not sent now: <reason>" for Send now. It is the default U4b had built.
