- **2026-11-15 · Who draws the to-human view: a declaration, a recorded advertisement, three values (U2, O-13; UC-13).** Ruled on the adversarial review of U2b's document, under one law: at every moment one side draws the view, never both and never neither, and no state that is merely unknown is read as "MAWS draws". (1) Whether an installed mod draws at 9 is read from a declaration the mod ships with its implementation, never from a version; no constant in MAWS names a release, and no list of failed releases is kept. (2) A launch advertises 9 only once that advertisement is durably recorded for it, and 8 when it cannot be; an advertisement that cannot be known is published as the mod's while the mod may be loaded. (3) A launch carries 9, 8 or nothing; with the lane off and the plugin on, the mod draws (the owner's card "Lane off": "Let the mod draw (Recommended)"). (4) U2b is two pull requests, the mirror on every route first and the cutover second; the first advertises no 9 and so leaves master releasable by itself. Why: a release number cannot gate a MAWS that has already shipped, and the review showed the window between the host's acceptance of a launch and anything written after it. Rejected: a constant for the first drawing release; reading an unreadable record as "MAWS draws"; advertising 8 everywhere to settle the lane-off case.
- **2026-11-15 · The fold where the mod draws and where MAWS mirrors it (U2, O-30).** Ruled on the same review and in U2b's build. (1) Where the mod draws, every row the fold can hide has an ask of its own, alive while the row is mounted; the first design, in which a row followed the remembered answer for the first row of its stretch, is withdrawn, and with it the remedy of check 5. (2) A reveal ends when its row leaves the mounted range after the jump has entered it, or at the next jump. (3) An asynchronous answer is stated once outside the record: its notice names the call that asked and folds where that call's row carries the receipt. As first built the notice named no call, so the fold never happened in the app although its tests passed on notices made by hand; found after the branch's merge and fixed where the notice is built. (4) "Toggle report and transcript" stays and says why it does nothing where the mod draws; "Mark all read" stays as a command of the palette. (5) In the mirror an open key names a row, not a place. Rejected: a tracker row that would narrow "the full transcript folds nothing"; a stretch key that cannot move, since no row of a stretch survives both of its ends changing.
- **2026-11-15 · The owner's answers on U2b's drawn example (U2, O-13 and O-30).** Seven cards, recorded verbatim in the register. "Fold line" was answered in the owner's own words: the record line should carry the mod's summary of the stretch, as it already does in a terminal, with a way to turn the summaries off. Ruled from it: the summaries are the mod's and reach MAWS with U2b's second pull request and the mod's pull request, with a setting in MAWS; the first pull request draws `› working record`. The six others are each the recommended answer ("Unnamed rows", "Lone thought", "Mod layout", "Switch frame", "Other machine", "Lane off"). Decided in the owner's place and reported: going back to an earlier build while the mod draws a session shows both drawings until that session restarts (accepted, a tracker row); the desk web app keeps its Report · Transcript bar (board ticket 682). Rejected: holding the 9 until no earlier build is kept; replacing the desk's bar now.
- **2026-11-15 · U4b's review: an uncertain take-back, an earlier engine host, the side card's move (U4, l3-input-04, l3-input-01, O-20).** (1) A record that could not be asked is asked again and nothing is loaded meanwhile; only a record that answered and cannot say loads the message under its warning. This changes U4a's code on master for every route. Why: a failed read is a question not yet asked, and over the machine link it is the common failure; loading on it made a second, sendable copy. (2) A Send now that a machine's endpoint accepted and its earlier engine host refused reads "Not sent now: the engine host on <machine> is finishing an update; this works once it has handed the session over", and no new interface carries a per-session host capability. Rejected: the busy sentence, which invites a retry that cannot work; and "needs a newer MAWS on <machine>", first ruled, which would send a person to update a machine that is up to date. (3) A question being started in the `/btw` card and its refusal live with the card's question, so a move between the corner and the column's foot starts no second question. Also ruled: neither Send now nor the cancel starts a session that is not running on another machine; "; " stands between the questions of one answer row; Edit and ↑ are closed from the first draw on a machine too old to keep a stage. The owner's card "Failed act" was answered "Taken back / Sent now (Recommended)".
- **2026-11-15 · Send now on an after-the-turn row goes down the request's path, and when a send-now cuts the turn (U4, l3-input-01).** A probe of 22 runs on engine 2.1.322 answered the condition U4b's document had set. Ruled: while a turn runs, Send now on an after-the-turn row moves the message to the next step and then sends the request for the uuid the move answered, in that order; a request written first answers `nothing_waiting` and moves nothing. Every next-step message already waiting goes with it, before it. The probe also showed what no earlier recording had: behind a shell command the turn is cut, and the messages are one, only while the call is not yet a task of the engine's (about its first three seconds); once it is a task the messages are read in the same turn, each its own. U4's table cell "that message alone goes now" and the sentences that said a Send now always cuts the turn are corrected. Not proven: a gap longer than one tick between the two writes; the request first at the late timing; a call other than a shell command. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · A waiting answer joined with a message is read apart by its text (U4, X-049; board ticket 704).** The engine writes several waiting messages as one, so an answer's envelope joined with a person's prompt was recalled raw by ↑ and drawn raw in the bubble. Ruled: one shared reader returns the envelopes found and the rest of the text; the bubble, Copy, the Edit prefill and recall read the rest, and the answers of every envelope are recorded for their questions. The rule first ruled, the shortest slice that parses, lost a person's words between a malformed envelope and a valid one, and was revised the same day: a slice ends before the next line that begins with a tag. Four reader rules followed from the review of the build: a pasted segment's body is data; attachments are never hidden with an all-envelope row; a member's boundary is the writer's whole opening line, so that a question holding a tag-like line no longer cuts its own envelope short; recall does not re-read derived text, and never skips a row with attachments. Rejected: keeping the envelope out of the join, since the join happens inside the engine and each workaround changes delivery; splitting by the members, which the transcript does not keep. The other readers of raw message text are ticket 704.
- **2026-11-15 · `/fork`'s panel when its refusal lifts (U4, O-48).** Ruled in the owner's place on U4b's branch review: while the refusal stands nothing is started; when it lifts with the panel still open, the panel closes and forks once, as an ordinary open does; a panel dismissed meanwhile forks nothing. Why: as built, the sentence went when the turn ended and an empty dialog with only a dismiss stayed. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The owner's six answers on the stacked Review (U5, l5-code-05, l5-code-01, l5-code-06).** Put with a drawn example of nine frames at 720, 360 and 280 px and answered the same day, each with the recommended option, which was also the built default: "Wrap, drop scope word (Recommended)" (the totals drop the scope's name, and the toolbar wraps by measure, not at a width); "Words wide, glyphs narrow (Recommended)" (a file's header); "The card's usual width (Recommended)" (the card under a line is the same 300 px card as on a page or a document; in Split its row spans both sides and the card stands under the side that holds the line) and "One line (Recommended)" (an unsent comment under a line); "No, the line says it (Recommended)" (the chip of a comment on a removed line); the option labelled "Show fewer" (Recommended) (Source control's list of commits). Why: the drawing showed that the toolbar does not fit on one line at 720 px either once comments wait, so the toolbar's card went out with a fourth option, the one the owner chose. Rejected, by the owner's choice: every word kept; one line shortened hard; the counter in the View menu; glyphs at every width; a header of two lines; a card as wide as the row; the whole comment in the row; "· before" on the chip; a list that stays whole.
- **2026-11-15 · A comment Review cannot place is listed under the toolbar, under one of three sentences (U5, l5-code-01).** Ruled in the owner's place on the reviews of U5b's fifth milestone: while Review holds waiting comments it cannot draw under their lines, a row under the toolbar lists them, oldest first, each opened, sent or removed there; one sentence per kind stands above its own comments, in the order "The line this comment was on is no longer in this diff.", "The file this comment is on is not in this diff.", "This file's diff is not shown here."; the rule that decides is one closed table with a row for every state a file's section can be in, and a test that fails for a state with no row; a card's reveal unfolds its file. Why: before this landing every waiting comment could be opened, and the landing must not make a person's words unreachable. Rejected: leaving such a comment's card undrawn, as the milestone first built it.
- **2026-11-15 · Review's place is a live place that is always kept and one reveal that input cancels (U5, X-072 and l5-code-01).** Ruled on the adversarial review of U5b's document, and again on the review of its branch: the reading place (file, row and offset) is kept through every change of width or zoom, and at most one reveal is outstanding, which the person's scroll, key or press cancels. The same holds for a comment card that had to wait for its file's read: the person's input during the wait cancels its reveal and the scroll that would show the whole card, and a hunk jump begun during the wait replaces them. Why: the review found a card revealed as if freshly opened although the person had scrolled away while it waited, and a hunk jump overridden by an older card's waiting scroll. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · U8d's rulings before its build and on its probes (U8, O-05 and #359).** U8d gives a terminal session that this Mac drives the source of its agents' states. (1) The panel's sentence "Status is known only while the session runs here" is not drawn where the session reports at least one agent whose status is known: one condition for a terminal session of this Mac and of another machine, so the sentence also leaves another machine's panel. (2) By the architect's ruling the derivation reads every task id of a notification block, ahead of the plugin's rule it is ported from, because at a resume the engine ends several orphaned agents or shells with one block. By the probes' findings it also takes a parent's end as no evidence for a nested agent launched in the background, which can outlive its parent, and reads a nested agent's end from the main transcript where its parent's file holds none. Until the plugin's reader follows (doperpowers issue 504), this Mac and another machine can differ in these three cases. (3) A bridge that goes while its terminal lives is read as the process gone, for agents as for jobs, so its agents read "ended with the session" until the bridge answers again (a ticket at landing). (4) A running row's line reads "Working", and an agent the engine wakes after it ended is not shown running again (a tracker row). (5) The list of this spec's sentences that the landing makes false was accepted, and they are amended in U8's file, UC-9's summary and the root. Rejected, for (2): staying in step with the plugin's rule until it changes, which would have this Mac's card list agents that no longer run. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · An agent that ended with no record of how reads "ended" (U8, #359; U8d).** The owner's answer on the card "Ended word" was the recommended option, "ended". In a terminal session, an agent that is known to have ended while the session still runs, with no record of how (the terminal's bridge says it no longer runs and the transcript holds no result for it, or the agent that started it has ended), reads "ended" in the Subagents panel, on this Mac and for a terminal session on another machine alike; "ended with the session" is kept for an agent whose terminal has gone. Ruled on its build the same day: the drill-in's reason line and its tooltip read "This agent ended" in the same two cases and "This agent ended with the session" otherwise, by the one rule that chooses the row's word; no time is drawn beside "ended", because such an agent has no recorded end time and a time taken from its launch would be false (the card's preview showed a time, and the difference is reported to the owner); the desk's web app, which draws the same rows, follows the word. Why: whether the word is true does not depend on how often the case arises; the probes showed it arises between the bridge's stop event and the read of the transcript's row, or where no row ever comes. Rejected: "ended with the session" for these cases, the word built until the answer, which can be read on an agent the person stopped in a terminal that is still open; "stopped", which would also name an agent that crashed or went with its parent.
- **2026-11-15 · What ends a gone terminal's kept statuses is another holder of the session, and each report names its origin (U8, O-05 and #359; U8d).** Ruled on U8d's branch review and on the review of its fix. When a terminal's process goes, the last statuses of its agents stay in the panel while the page lives. They stand while the session's record names the holder they came from (that terminal or background job) or no holder, and they are discarded when another holder takes the session: a launch by this app, another terminal or job, or a binding of the session to another machine's feed. Each report carries its origin, the holder whose bridge its facts were read from, and the window compares it with the record's holder: a report of another origin is ignored, and a report with no origin (an older build's driver; another machine's sminos today) is drawn while its watch lives and never kept past it. No registered wire shape changes, so no wire minor and no two-build run is owed (accepted by the architect, with one unit case that the path from another machine carries the origin through unchanged). Why: the question is which process's account the window is drawing, and the record's holder says it. Rejected: fencing by this app's launch time against the time the page received the statuses, which compares two clocks of two different events and let a gone terminal's last report go on being drawn over the new launch's own agents; a generation number stamped on the driver, which would add a field to a registered event and with it a wire minor; reading the origin from the session's record at each report, which relabelled an old watch with a new terminal's name. The residual cases are accepted as tracker rows.
- **2026-11-15 · A file from outside the folders is editable, and Open File… (U6e; U6, O-37).** Asked whether a link inside a shown Markdown file may open a file outside the folders, the owner typed that it should be editable too (the words are in the list of the owner's answers), and then chose "Every outside file (Recommended)" and "Yes, add it (Recommended)"; this reverses the read-only half of the answer of 2026-11-13. On the cards before it: "Keep it as it is (Recommended)" for a file the agent opens by itself and "Yes, the same rule (Recommended)" for a link in a subagent's transcript; U6b-4 builds that link and the Markdown file's. Ruled around the answers: an outside file becomes writable only as the person's own act, read as follows: the act is the save, the daemon writes only on evidence it reads itself, and the save comes from the app window's own page with the check of the content shown; for a save the transcript names a path only in a person's own message, the agent's own text or a tool call's input, never in what a tool returned or in a message someone else wrote; MAWS's own folders are never written, inside the session's folders or outside; the save is a command of its own; an open the agent asked for is never admitted by the person's choice of a file. Why: text the agent only read, injected text included, could otherwise admit a path by containing it, and the profile folder holds the record of the person's choices. Rejected: a key on the ordinary save, which a daemon of an older build would drop and then write by the folders alone; making the open the act, since the owner made a file the agent opened editable too; and a choice that holds for the whole window, by which one session's agent could be shown a file chosen in another.
- **2026-11-15 · The bar of a file from outside the folders, on a drawn example (U6e; U6, O-37).** The owner answered four cards: "No label if savable (Recommended)", "Dark text (Recommended)", "Label opens a panel (Recommended)" and "Right-click menu only (Recommended)". A file that can be saved carries no label and shows its full path, with "Save writes to <full path>" as the path's tooltip; a file that cannot be saved reads "Read-only", a button whose panel gives the reason. The first answer is the owner's ruling over the architect's condition that an outside file's tab show that it is outside: the card stated the cost, that nothing on screen says so except the path. Ruled with the drawing: a save's failure sentence takes a line of its own and is never cut, in every file tab; nothing a person needs is reachable by hover alone; the panel is never wider than its column. Rejected: the words "Outside this session's folders" on every outside file, the document's first default.
- **2026-11-15 · A person's transfer of a file tab takes the agent's mark back (U6e; U6, O-37).** Ruled at U6e's merge with the landing that opens a file beside the chat: every transfer of a file tab by the person (to the main area, to the side column, between the side column's groups, Reopen closed tab) takes back the mark of a tab the agent opened, as a click does, at the act. Why: a transfer is the person's act on the file, as a click is. Rejected: taking the mark back when the tab is mounted, which a restore or a re-mount with no act of the person would also do. A main file tab's move between panes takes nothing back in this landing, and is logged as debt.
- **2026-11-15 · A file from outside the folders on another machine (U6f; U6, O-37).** The owner answered "Open and edit there too (Recommended)" and "Pick a file on that machine (Recommended)", and on the chooser's four cards "One folder at a time (Recommended)", "That machine's home (Recommended)", "Shown, after the others (Recommended)" and "Owners only for now (Recommended)". This reverses "a session on another machine keeps the refusal" of 2026-11-13 for a person who may open a terminal on that machine. The session that keeps master set eight conditions, which bind: the acts take exactly the terminal's grade, checked by the same decision and not by a copy of its outcome; that machine's daemon alone reads the evidence, and the choice is recorded there; P5's Decision Log 237 stays for every caller below that grade; the chooser may list wherever the daemon's user can read; MAWS's own folders and the comparison of the real path apply there; each save is logged on that machine with the member and the time, and the path stays out of the hub; a machine on an older build leaves the items unavailable; and the build takes the review panel, with an authorization lens first. That session amends Decision Log 237 at the landing. Ruled on the document's review: this build's window offers the acts to the machine's owners alone, which is the window's offer and no rule of the machine; no read and no save enters a folder macOS protects on that machine unasked; a save that waits for the machine is sent inside its send window or never. Why the terminal's grade: whoever may open a terminal there can already read and write any file the daemon's user can, so no reach is added. Rejected: offering the acts to every holder of the terminal's right, for whom every page of a read, every folder and every save would raise a confirmation, some with no press.
- **2026-11-15 · U6b-2, the file frames: the rulings on its review and on its drawn example (U6, O-37).** On the review: the check that a request comes from the page its token was issued to must be shown to see every request that reaches the bytes, by a spike that instruments both ends, and one unmatched request stops the landing; the copy of another machine's file is one version within the hashed read's cap and compared by size alone above it, said so, with a tracker row; its bytes never exist under a name; one failure path for a frame, and a failed frame is read again only on the person's act. On the drawn example: the frame's URL ends in the file's base name, and main answers the token's file for that exact path alone (the form that ignored the path stood for one commit and was withdrawn); a row never offers an act that can only fail, and says why its file is not drawn; a clip that cannot be played shows a row; the failure body reads "This file stopped loading: it changed, or it can no longer be read." with "Open again"; an HTML page with no declared charset is served as UTF-8 when its first 64 KiB are valid UTF-8; the PDF toolbar is hidden beside the chat and kept in the main area. The owner answered its four cards the same day, each with the recommended option: "The page alone (Recommended)", "Say so (Recommended)", "True sentences now (Recommended)", "Hide beside chat (Recommended)"; only "Say so" changes a built default, adding the line "Shown without the files beside it" under the file's bar of a page that names files beside it. Loading the files beside an HTML page is board ticket 707, deferred. Rejected: taking the request filter's reach as given; a part file renamed into place, and a sweep at start; a silent refusal of a first read, and a timer or a retry; the toolbar kept everywhere as the default.
- **2026-11-15 · U10b-4: the sentence for instructions that cannot be read, and the cards' least width (U10, l8-settings-13 and l6-artifacts-08).** The sheet reads "Couldn’t read this Space’s instructions: <message>", the pattern of its neighbours, holds its field and offers no Save. The cards of "Make something new" are no narrower than 180 px. Why: 200 px contradicted the drawn page it was ruled on, where four cards and More share one line of an 896 px column; the first Mac run showed a second line. Rejected: 200 px.
- **2026-11-15 · U10b-2: the reads of an editor whose artifact an agent deleted are coalesced (U10, l6-artifacts-07).** Such an editor waits, taking no edit, and leaves the wait only on a current read that answers that the artifact is there again. Once it waits, no read is started while one is out, and never one with the timer armed; a trigger during a read marks one more ask; on settle a current live answer ends the wait, else a marked ask issues one read, else a current gone answer fails the copy, else the capped backoff is armed. This replaces the wording "at most one standing read in flight while parked". Why: the branch reviews found two ways around the first form. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · U5's third read, and what holds a session open (UC-7, UC-8, UC-14; ticket 686).** (1) Ruled by the wave's architect on U5b's execution document: "This branch" and the count of a new file's lines need a read of the working tree against a commit with new files counted, so U5's two reads over the machine link become three, under the same checkout rule and the same one capability. Rejected: a field on the existing diff read, which an older daemon would drop and answer something else. (2) The side thread of a session on another machine leaves U5b for a landing of its own (ticket 686): no path today subscribes a session shown only in the Side pane. For it the session that keeps master ruled that the Side pane may hold such a session open: "only a tab holds a session open" becomes "a surface that shows a session holds it open" when that landing is on master, within four bounds (one thread; the same function as a tab; released on close, on a switch and on the window's close, with tests; held once when shown in both). Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · A command's output, a code block, a table, an answer's row and the compaction's line on U3b's drawn example (U3, O-18, O-07, O-08, l2-transcript-07, #331).** The rest of the rulings on the drawn example of U3's second landing. R2, R12, R17 and R19 to R23 are in this day's other entries on U3; R3, R13 and R14 are stated in this child's file with R19 to R21, which rest on them; R6 and R16 restate what this child's file already says. R1: inside a command's output the markdown is drawn at the line's own scale (a heading the line's size in bold, blocks 4 px apart, a table's text the line's size); one drawing, both states. Why: closed, two lines of its type must hold two lines of text. R4: line ends are kept for an output that is not markdown structure. R5: a terminal's grid scrolls sideways inside the row and does not wrap. R7: a code block's frame is the outer box, with one ground, one border and the table's radius, and 32 px of room is kept at the end of the first two source lines. R8: its copy control stays at the frame's top right with its ground behind it and may lie over the end of a later line while the block is scrolled, as in Claude Desktop. R9: both copy controls have a hit target of at least 24 by 24 px. R10: the table's control takes the code control's ground and the table's inner scroller has none: one frame, one ground. R11: cells of a wide table keep wrapping. Rejected: forbidding wraps, which would make a cell that holds a sentence unboundedly wide. R15: an answer's action row is 20 px with 8 px kept under it, so an answer that has it is 16 px taller than one that has not. R18: the compaction's line starts at the rows' left edge, not indented to the turn line's words. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The adversarial review of U3b's execution document: find counts only what it can mark, and Copy takes the whole answer (U3, l2-transcript-08, l2-transcript-07, O-18).** Six findings, each verified in the code and ruled. (1) A counted match is a markable match: find's words for a row are the words that row draws, by one reading the row and find share; a command's output is one drawing in both states; a kind of row that cannot meet this is not searched. (2) Going to a match handles every clipping ancestor between the match and the transcript, innermost first, and a line's words are searched once, never left behind an ellipsis; with it, moving to another match closes what find opened for the last one and closing the bar closes nothing. (3) Only controls named one by one are left out of the search, and marks are drawn only inside a searched part. (4) What Copy takes is read from the whole turn and never depends on what is folded, closed or drawn. (5) The promise that every slice of find's first pass is at most 8 ms is withdrawn for a resumable pass, budgets measured on a Mac and a named fallback off the main thread. (6) The new entry a command's output travels with owes a run against each older build although the wire's manifest did not change: the wave's rule for a changed message. Why: the review showed counts for text the row does not draw (a call's label, a command's opened output), a revealed row that still hid its match in a table, a tall block or a cut line, a skip rule that skipped a call's line, and a Copy that lost answer blocks a closed stretch removes from the drawn list; one turn's derivation and one part's drawing cannot be split, so no slice bound can be promised, only measured. Rejected: a second reader of the markdown source with a fallback that kept a counted match with no mark.
- **2026-11-15 · U1b's review: when a prompt takes a key, and one way to a waiting request (U1, l4-approvals-08 and -02, l2-transcript-10; UC-6).** Rulings on the adversarial review of U1b's document, on its fourth and fifth milestones and on its drawn example. (1) A prompt's chords act only with the keyboard on the prompt's own row, in that session's composer, or on nothing with that pane active; a subagent's transcript in the pane keeps its prompt's keys to its own row. Rejected: a binding within the whole pane, which let a press made on another request's row answer the prompt; and a listener of the card's own beside the keymap, which a rebinding would not follow. (2) The 400 ms is per prompt and starts again when the card comes back into view, when a menu, dialog or popover closes and when the card becomes answerable again; the two chords that allow also need the row of buttons wholly visible, and for 400 ms; Esc needs a part of the card in view. The guard starts again 400 ms after a cover it observed (a change of the buttons' intersection, an element added to or taken from the body, an overlay's change of state, a press while covered); a cover that no observer saw counts only if it is still there at the press. This rests on every overlay that takes keys being portalled to the body, which a source guard pins. Rejected: a check of the buttons at the press alone, under which a card partly in view for seconds could show its buttons and take ⌘↩ at once; and hit tests on every change outside a transcript, which would bring back the layout reads an earlier ruling on the same milestone removed. (3) Every way to a waiting request shows that one request, opens it at the end when it is a prompt there, and puts the keyboard on its transcript row; a request answered meanwhile is said to be no longer waiting, and the keyboard goes to nothing that answers. Rejected: focus on the card's primary button or the form's first option, as U1's file said, because a key pressed on a button or an option is that control's own and the request's keys would be dead after a jump; and a jump to the end's open card, which with two prompts waiting left the keyboard on the one that was open, not the one chosen. (4) The pill "Needs your input" keeps the round button's right edge and vertical place and grows to the left.
- **2026-11-15 · What a row reads once its request is answered, as U1b builds it (U1, S-07, l4-approvals-08, O-21).** (1) A row never draws an empty form. A question's row whose request was answered elsewhere draws no form and no control and reads "Already answered elsewhere" until it carries its own answers or the session leaves; the two seconds close only the card. Why: "Not answered" is false in that gap, and an empty form shows nothing true; the sentence exists already. (2) A plan's row shows a known answer's words in the place of its Pending badge until its own outcome arrives. Why: a known answer must never read as unanswered again because a timer ran out. (3) A receipt belongs to the session it was addressed to, so a call id that exists on two machines shows each machine's answer on its own row only. (4) The three-second note for a request the terminal answered first stands where the request stood at the transcript's end, also when it was the only one there, with no control. (5) After a reload a terminal session on this Mac reads its settlement again; for another machine's terminal session a reloaded window reads "Not answered" until the delivery, and nothing more is claimed for that route. (6) The answer's notice stays a row of the turn in which the answer reached the agent and folds with it in the transcript view; when it is that turn's only work it stays in view and the turn gets no line. Rejected: a line "Worked for 1s" over nothing but the notice, which reports work that did not happen. Where no other alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · Approve and retry: where the button stands, and what the rows around it say (U1, l4-approvals-12).** Rulings on U1c's adversarial review and its milestone reviews, under one principle: the button is the one control that consents to a call a safety check refused, so it is drawn only beside the whole thing it consents to. (1) The button stands under a line that wraps to show the command whole, for a command of at most 200 characters on one line with no backtick; otherwise under a block that shows the command whole. Rejected: the button beside a label that does not show what the approval names; and a wrapped line for a longer command, which would make one row many lines high. (2) Of the engine's notices of a refusal only a classifier block's is dropped, and only once the call's own row carries the reason; every other refusal's notice stays. (3) No button on a subagent's rows, the reason corrected: the approval never reaches the subagent, and the main agent runs the command itself, which is not a retry of the subagent's step. What a subagent's blocked row reads differs between the live route and a file, and is left so, with a tracker row. (4) An approval that was dropped before it was sent reads "You approved `cmd`" with the existing "Not sent" mark. Why: an approval that never reached the engine must not read as one. (5) The opened detail of a blocked call shows the command and none of the engine's refusal text; a real reason stands on the row's line only. Where no other alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The to-human view's second pull request against earlier builds, and one reading of a text's marks (U2, O-13 and O-30; UC-13).** The session that keeps master allowed the second pull request's wire and stored forms, with conditions: the new command takes its component from the engine host's list alone and refuses an unknown one by name; `drawnBy` is one optional key in one minor, and the two-build runs must show both earlier builds reading the state that carries it; the advertisement is a nullable column, and those runs include going back and coming forward again, where a row the earlier build wrote or rewrote reads as 8, never as a stale 9. Directed in the first pull request's build: which words of a text are a message and which are the record's comes from one function for every drawing of that text and for the mirror, and a closed stretch's head shows exactly its messages. Where that reading differs from the mod's (a mark left open in a completed text, a stray closing tag, crossing marks) MAWS keeps the shared grammar's answer. Why: reading such a mark as a message would change stored marks and their ids, stored previews and what the channel sends, which an earlier build reads. Rejected: following the mod's reading in the first pull request; it is the second's to decide.
- **2026-11-15 · U4b: what the qualifications of its adversarial review settle (U4, O-20, l3-input-04, X-049).** (1) For the length of an edge drag that hides the card column, the `/btw` card at its foot is hidden with it and is not drawn at the corner. (2) The desk web app is in every milestone's proof and draws nothing new for a waiting answer. (3) What the take-back's sequence states is one window's steps, and no claim of exactly-once across two windows or devices. (4) A Send now that an earlier engine host refuses on another machine is worded and not drawn disabled, since nothing carries over the machine link which host serves a session. Rejected: reading the drag in the rule that places the card, which would move the card to the corner and back within one gesture; a new interface for a host's capability per session.
- **2026-11-15 · Rulings on the drawn example of the stacked Review, and the chip of a comment that names a commit (U5, l5-code-05, l5-code-01, l5-code-06).** From the drawing: a rename reads "old → new" only where both paths fit, else the new path with the badge "Renamed"; under a line the card leaves out its own list of the pass, which the rows under the lines already are; the controls the landing adds are at least 24 px in their pressed area; a commit's footer shortens its subject first; Split is not offered where the stack is narrower than 480 px, a stack set to Split drawing Unified there with the stored choice kept; the quoted line of a comment is written as an inline code span on a line of its own, which the transcript draws as one compact line of code; a deleted file's row keeps its column in line and the unsent-comments chip is drawn as a chip; and the words the frames had to invent are fixed once (a pure rename and a binary file show no counts, a new file "+N" alone; the hunk counter is not drawn while no file is loaded). Why: the drawing, made at 720, 360 and 280 px, showed each (a rename that did not fit a narrow header, every waiting comment drawn twice, small targets, a long subject pushing the footer's last words out, a few characters of code a side, a fenced quote drawn as a code block about 110 px tall for one line). Separately, on a point the document's author reported: the transcript's chip of a comment that names a commit opens Review on that commit at that file. Rejected: that chip as a label, which would be a dead control where every other anchor's chip acts; and for the quote, a fenced block, which the bubble draws as a code block with a header row and a copy button. For the drawing's other rulings no alternative is named, and none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · What counts as a file beside an HTML page (U6, O-37; U6b-2).** The line "Shown without the files beside it" is drawn for a page that names a stylesheet, a script, a picture, a media source or a frame by a relative URL or a `file:` URL in the head main already read. An empty value and a scheme-relative value do not count: the first names the page itself, the second another host. Rejected: the rule's letter (a value with no scheme), which would say the line of a page that has no file beside it.
- **2026-11-15 · Eight answers on the drawn examples of the artifacts' second landing (U10; l6-artifacts-07, O-43, l6-artifacts-08, l8-settings-13).** Each is the first, recommended option, and each is what is built. An export is spaced as the editor is, for every document, existing ones included ("Yes, match the editor (Recommended)"): found while the export was drawn, where the exported page lacked the reset the editor has and a memo that fits one page in the editor printed on two. Over a side surface the chat stays as the person left it when another session's tab comes in front ("As you left it (Recommended)"), where the section said it shows the card: that sentence rested on a premise the code contradicts. A Space that has instructions is marked by the word ("The word "Instructions" (Recommended)"), as the section already had it. Rejected, as the cards put them: today's wider gaps in a PDF; a tighter Compact, and spacing alone; a size checked only when it was chosen for the document; nothing behind the things above the box, and a surface at all times; a chat bubble and a chevron; a hidden chat coming back as the bar, and the card at every change of session; a card with the icon, the name and "Blank" only; a small icon for the mark.
- **2026-11-15 · Customize's page as U11c built it (U11, O-14).** Ruled during the build: between the wide head and the stacked one, once the tools take a line of their own the search field fills it with the actions at its right end, and where they no longer fit the search is the full line and the actions go to the next line from the left, with no right-then-left flip; the skills' first-run line names "Customize › Discover, with Show set to Skills" in place of the retired path; and four choices a person sees are accepted: a line keeps its own kind's controls, so "Reload again" is a plugin line's only; a narrow plugin row hides its version and date; a connector row's image stands first; an install of a catalog item reads on both its Skills card and its Plugins card, because the page keeps one entry for it. Decided at the milestones: the empty state is the place's and its words the search's; a shelf of Discover › All is drawn while its kind holds anything; the two detail boxes of an image keep their sizes, 44 px and 56 px; a sheet or a dialog belongs to one visit of a place. On the branch review: `catalog.pluginIcons` takes each plugin with its marketplace's source, and main keys an icon by the verified repository and plugin name, so a marketplace that reuses a seed's name with another repository draws no seed icon. Rejected: the build's first rule for the shelves, "no empty shelf"; both detail boxes at 56 px; a check in main of the marketplace's source against a second read of the engine's marketplaces, which would add a second reader of that list and protect nothing while only this app's window calls the command.
- **2026-11-15 · Three points of U3b's execution document on find and the keys, accepted (U3, l2-transcript-08, l2-transcript-09).** (1) ⌘G with a page of the in-app browser focused does nothing: ⌘F is taken there for the browser's bar, ⌘G goes through to the menu, and its action does nothing while another web contents has the keyboard; one tracker row names the browser bar's owner, U12. (2) A new query's current match is the first match at or below the view's top edge, else the last above it. Rejected: Claude Desktop's rule, the first match of the session, which sends a reader at the end of a long session to its start. (3) No message row is a tab stop: ↑ and ↓ between messages act on a focus a jump gave, never on one a click gave; with a tracker row. U3's sentence that the focus could arrive "by the keyboard" is corrected. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · A receipt's header, and desk web's placeholder (U1, O-21, l4-approvals-08).** Two of U1b's rulings. (1) On the drawn example a question receipt's header of about fourteen characters was cut beside "Dismissed"; a header is now cut only when header and answer together exceed the receipt's widest allowed width. (2) Desk web no longer drawing the placeholder for a waiting request is accepted: its words named a composer that desk web does not answer in, and desk web draws its own cards. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The first run no longer sets aside a plugin for its manifest name (U11, #476).** The design of the connector switches left a plugin unresolved at the first run when its name in its manifest differs from the name in its key, because which of the two the engine uses in a connector's name had not been run (its check 10). The probe of a plugin's connectors on engine `2.1.322` has run it (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-uxwave-u11-plugin-connectors.txt`, verdict 1, on master): the name is the manifest's, in `mcp_status` and in init's `mcp_servers` alike. So such a plugin is no longer unresolved for that reason, check 10 is closed, and the other reasons for an unresolved plugin stay as written. Why: the rule was changed by the probe's result. - **2026-11-15 · The slot row's derivation goes and its kind stays (U1; UC-3).** U1's file said that the slot row made from the engine's request events is retired. U1b's document found that the row kind cannot be: desk web builds rows of that kind itself for the tail of its session view and draws them through the transcript's one seam for a row its host draws. Ruled on that document: the derivation is retired, the kind and the seam stay, and a waiting row's form and the end row are drawn through that same seam, so nothing is added to what the transcript exports. Rejected: a second registered renderer for forms and a component slot for the end, two more public names for the same act.
- **2026-11-15 · U1b's branch review: six rules of a request drawn in the transcript (U1, l4-approvals-08 and -02, O-21; UC-6).** The review panel on U1b's branch found six faults; the wave's architect confirmed each and set its rule. (1) Every request's card takes its keys in the step that first draws it. Why: a prompt could be painted with its chords untaken, and an Esc in that frame reached the composer and stopped the turn. (2) In view means that some area of the card shows, by one test for the press and for the guard's observer. Why: a card that only touched the view's edge started its 400 ms. (3) A late answer's receipt is written from what the reply to it says of the settlement, in the same words as from the session's own list of settlements. Why: with that list not yet caught up the row read "Already answered elsewhere". (4) A prompt's reason for denying and a plan's feedback are kept by request in the window's memory and dropped when the request stops waiting; a box drawn open again by a remount does not take the keyboard. Why: they were lost when the open card at the end was exchanged or a plan's form scrolled out of the mounted rows. (5) Inside the floating card the transcript's region keeps 200 px while the session has a request waiting that its transcript draws, in its call's row or at its end. This replaces the rule of the same day for a request at the end only, which also held the height for the three-second note alone. (6) With the floating card in front and nothing focused, the card's prompt takes Esc and the chords that allow when the pane the card stands for is the focused one, and at most one prompt takes the press. Why: the card's prompt never took them. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · U1c's branch review: when an approval reads as read, and what is an approval's row (U1, l4-approvals-12).** Four rulings on the review panel's findings. (1) In a terminal session a waiting approval reads approved at the result that completes the round, which is when every call the agent made in its last response has its result. This replaces "the next result of a tool call of the main agent" in this log's entry of the same day on U1c's build. Why: with two calls made at once the row read approved at the first result while the engine still waited for the second, and an interrupt of that second call left the row reading approved for an approval the engine had not read. Rejected: the engine's own records of its requests to the model as the boundary, which another machine's rows never carry, so the routes would disagree. Left, with a tracker row each: a fast call's result written while the same response still produces a later call can make the approval read approved one call early; and an interruption's result is taken as no boundary only in the forms a recording holds. (2) A waiting message is an approval's row only when the grant sentence is everything the message holds; one with an attachment beside the sentence is an ordinary message with its ordinary controls, wherever it is drawn. Why: an image beside the sentence was drawn as an approval, without Edit, while the image stayed in what was sent. (3) A blocked row reads approved only once the engine has started the approval's message; in every state before that, and in any state the window cannot place, the approval waits and the row keeps its button. Why: an approval written and not yet accepted read approved, and its button was gone. (4) The engine's notice of a classifier block is left out only where the row drawn for that call shows the block; a blocked Agent call's row does not, so its notice stays. Why: after a blocked Agent call only "delegate finished with errors" remained. Where no other alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · A joined answer is split where a message becomes an event (U4, X-049; board ticket 704).** U4b repaired the joined answer where the transcript's model reads a user row. The readers it did not reach still held the envelope as the person's text: the text a rewind puts back in the box and its boundary's tooltip, the resume chooser's preview, the search text, a session's title where it falls back to a later prompt, the description Rename sends, and the events a window of an older build receives. Decided for ticket 704's landing: one function splits a person's message where a producer is about to emit it, and each envelope becomes an event of its own of today's shape, directly after the person's message and under its uuid, so no key, kind or wire minor is added and the two older builds record the answers and draw the person's words with no change to them. Ruled by the wave's architect the same day: outside the transcript an answer reads as nothing; the window applies the same split once where events enter it, so a message an older build's host made is split too; the split is by the text behind one gate at every maker and does not tell who typed, and the first draft's promise that a prompt typed at a terminal is never split was withdrawn, because a terminal's prompt carries the same origin as MAWS's own; and a waiting message that holds an envelope beside an attachment is never the stack's answer row. Why there: it is the last place that still holds the row's own marks and the first where every reader after it is served at once. Rejected: the split at the window's ingest alone, which leaves every reader outside a window needing a call of its own and a window of an older build on this build's host wrong; an `answer` entry on the person's message, from which no older build would record the answers; a gate by the key that the file's joined line carries, `mergedSendUuids`, which exists only in the file and would give the file and the live wire different events for one message.
- **2026-11-15 · U3b's branch panel: five rules (U3, O-18, l2-transcript-07, #146).** The panel on U3b's first landing found five faults, each reproduced, and each got a rule. (1) A row of a turn that a rewind discarded gets no action row at all, and every message an action names is found among the live rows only, whether the rewind is shown or not. Why: with a rewind shown, the answer above it was cut at the discarded prompt and the discarded answer offered a fork of a conversation that does not hold it. (2) A command's output stands alone unless the row drawn directly before it is its command's bubble, read from that row and not from the turn. Why: a typed `/compact` draws its bubble, the boundary, then its output, and the output lost the command's name although a row stood between the two. (3) Closed, a command's output is never drawn taller than its bound, whatever it holds and whenever its content finishes loading. Where its lines can be measured the bound stays the bottom of the second drawn line (R19); where what it draws is taller than that without being lines of text (an image, a table, a rule), the bound is two lines of the output's own type, with "Show more"; and the measure runs again at any change of the output's size, its height included. Why: an output with no line of text had no bound, and content that finished loading late was never measured again. (4) By the session that keeps master, binding: the one-click allow for the web plugin's fetch requires the server's identity, not its name, and fails closed. No recording shows a field that tells the plugin's server from a person's HTTP server named `web`, so `mcp__web__fetch` is not on the read-only list and gets the ordinary prompt on every surface; drawing the call as a web fetch by its name stays, and a tracker row says a server of that name is drawn as the plugin's fetch. This withdraws one sentence of #146's design. Rejected: the offer by the tool's name, which any server of that name would get. (5) An answer of a turn that has not ended gets no action row in any view of the rows. Why: under a record line that said working, Copy and Fork were enabled before the turn ended. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-15 · The record line's summary: one maker, one store, a switch that stops new summaries (U2, O-30; UC-14).** Designed into U2b's second pull request on the owner's typed answer to "Fold line", and ruled on its adversarial review. (1) A stretch's summary is made by the to-human mod, in the engine process that runs the session, and by nothing else; MAWS asks no model. (2) It is kept in the mod's own file for the session, which MAWS reads and never writes; the file is a contract (replaced in one step, keeping what another process stored, marked with its form), and MAWS reads the contract's form alone, so a release of the mod before its pull request shows no summaries in MAWS. (3) The window reads the file on a short schedule after each close of a stretch, each end of a turn and each mount, and holds the last read that succeeded, whole. (4) Under MAWS the mod summarises only where it draws MAWS's view, at 9. (5) The switch is a fact the daemon keeps among the index's own facts and carries in a launch's settings: UC-14's rule of a layout key is set aside for it, and law 3 is amended for this one switch. (6) Where MAWS draws, the line of a stretch that is still growing reads its newest call, by the owner's answer, which asked for what the terminal shows; a session on another machine and the desk web app read `working record` in a summary's place at this landing. Why: under the law that one side draws, one program at most asks a model for a stretch, and a settled stretch reads one text where the mod draws it and where MAWS draws it, before and after a reload. Rejected: MAWS making the summary itself (a second maker wherever a terminal also runs the session, and MAWS has no route to a model); a copy in MAWS's index, or a row in the transcript; a watcher in the daemon with an event; summaries at 8 too; the switch as a key of the machine settings file, as a layout key, as a file of the mod's that MAWS writes, or as the mod's value alone with no control in MAWS; `working record` until the summary where MAWS draws; MAWS also reading the older form of the file, by which a read that succeeds could be a file half replaced.
- **2026-11-15 · Rulings on the six added frames of Source control's page (U5, l5-code-06).** U5b-2's document added six frames of the page to the drawn example, at 360 and 280 px, and the wave's architect ruled fourteen points the words had not settled, then amended six of them after the frames were redrawn and measured; where a frame and a ruling differ, the ruling governs. The commit list's control never says "all" when it cannot show all: the read keeps its limit of 50, the control reads "Show all N commits" for 9 to 50 commits and "Show 50 of N commits" above 50, then "M older commits not shown"; "Show fewer" stands directly after the list and brings the Commits heading into view. The Where line stays at the top while the parts under it scroll as one, and it gives way in a fixed order, never by cutting its end. Facts that call for an act are never cut: the conflicts stand whole and `+A −R` leaves first; "· N behind" stays whole and "since `<base>`" is left out whole. With no base the heading reads "Commits · latest on `<branch>`", or "Commits · latest" on a detached `HEAD`, and has no "Review ›". A failed part keeps its bare heading and its sentence, with a control that asks again only where a new read can change the answer. A cancelled check is drawn neutral, as a pending one is; a refusal whose only detail is a code word draws no tooltip; every control's hit area is at least 24 px tall. New words a person reads: "Show 50 of N commits" and "Commits · latest". Why: frames at real sizes showed what the words had left open. A second "Show fewer" in the Commits heading is a tracker row, not built; where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · An approval is read only in the person's own words (U1, l4-approvals-12).** Rulings at U1c's merge onto the landings before it. (1) In a message that joins several texts, a line inside the answer to a question and a line inside a pasted body are never read as an approval; a message that is the sentence alone is read as before, and the reading fails closed: a sentence MAWS does not read as an approval leaves the blocked row with its button. Why: an answer's lines carried the question's header, which the agent writes, and a header holding the exact sentence on a line of its own read as an approval the person never sent. (2) No row above the composer reads an approval from the text it shows; each carries the reading the transcript's reader made of the original message. Why: since the merge a row's text is the message with its answers taken out, and for a message that held the sentence between two answers a row showed an approval's words while the transcript recorded none. (3) Corrected the same day from the merged code: on a session MAWS runs on another machine a waiting approval's row offers Send now where that machine lists the send-now capability and Delete where it lists the session acts. U1's file had said that Send now is not offered over the link, which held until the composer's second landing (U4b) brought it. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · One function decides what is an answer envelope, and every reader takes it (U4, X-049; board ticket 704).** The review panel of ticket 704's branch found three faults of one kind: a reader that decided "this is an envelope" by a parse of its own, apart from the split. An envelope spread over two text blocks beside an image was left whole by the split, yet its answer was recorded and the message hidden, the image with it. A shell line that held a literal envelope lost it from the search text while the transcript kept the whole command. And a prompt that opened with a held question's tag and ended in a block of no answers was kept as the person's by the split, yet stored as the answer and hidden, and the real answer was then refused. Ruled by the session that keeps master: an answer is recorded, and a message hidden, only where the split itself took an envelope out of that message (the writer's exact opening, a whole text block, no spanning); the readers take the split's result, never a parse of their own; a message that carries an attachment is never hidden whole; the search text skips shell rows as the transcript file's reader does. Built as one test of "one envelope alone" beside the split, which the split uses for its own check, and a guard test that fails on an envelope parse outside the four files allowed to hold one. Registered for later: #713, #714 and #715 (U4's file says what each is). Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · No text the model wrote stands in a user-role message that MAWS writes for the person (U4, X-049; board ticket 711).** The envelope that carries an asynchronous ask's answers is a user-role message, and it quoted each question's header or words, the labels picked and the questions' ids, all written by the model. The gap was found at the chain merge of U1c, whose own reader of the person's grants is corrected on its branch; the engine's auto mode classifier reads the conversation too and is not MAWS's to correct. A probe on engine `2.1.322` recorded the classifier allowing the retry of a blocked call when the engine's consent sentence stood in the envelope's quoted question, in 2 of 15 runs for a form a real ask can produce. Ruled by the session that keeps master: the fix is a pull request of its own from master (#717), ahead of every landing of the chain; the envelope carries no text of the model's, on its numbered lines or in its block; envelopes of earlier builds are still read. Three rulings followed from the probe and the review. A picked option is written by its place, `option <k>`, after a label written as a quoted string was still taken for consent in 5 of 5 runs; by its place, 0 of 5. A question is named in the block by its place, `q<n>`, because its own id is up to 64 characters of the model's words; the reader joins by place and hands on the question's own id. And an envelope is of the new form only when every answer's `question` is empty, where the first build took any empty one for the mark. What an older build's window shows when the envelope is its only source for an answer ("Not answered" for a question whose id the model chose, `option <k>` for one whose id is its place) was decided without a card, and the owner was told. Rejected: the header written as a JSON string, the ticket's other offer; a new marker in the block, which the two older builds' strict parse would not read.
- **2026-11-16 · An ask that quotes a permission grant is refused, and a picked name that quotes one is left out (U4, X-049; board ticket 711).** Two second layers behind the envelope's form, which is the boundary, ruled between the session that keeps master and the wave's architect. (1) The classifier also reads the model's own ask call, so where an ask's arguments are admitted the host refuses an ask any of whose strings holds the opening of the engine's consent sentence, with a refusal the model reads: "An ask may not quote a permission grant. Ask the question in your own words." The reviews found three ways past earlier forms of the test, each closed: marks, tags and zero-width or full-width characters inside the opening; words of the opening inside a pair of angle brackets, once tags were removed; and a tag around one word together with words inside another pair. The test now reads letters and digits alone after compatibility and case folding, and each pair of angle brackets both ways by itself; it claims no paraphrase, no words put between the opening's words by other means, and no look-alike letters of another script. (2) A name or path the person picked may have been written by a model: a design system's name is an artifact's title, a folder's name can be made by a shell command, and a short grant sentence fits in one path component. The writer puts each picked name and path through the same test and leaves out one that quotes the grant; what the person typed is written as it is. This revises the first ruling, which had accepted picked names as written. The refusal's words and the fixed words that stand for a path left out were decided without a card, and the owner was told. The full closure, a picked name reaching the model through a tool result, is #716. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · U3b at its merge with the chain: a command turn's own line, the answer's action row in the to-human view, and the compaction's row (U3, O-18, l2-transcript-07, #331).** (1) Confirming a point the panel's fix wave built for its second finding: the command turn's own line, drawn between the command's bubble and its output, counts as the bubble's. Why: otherwise an output directly under its command would gain and lose the command's name as the turn ends; a boundary or any other row still separates the two. (2) In the to-human view the answer's action row stands under the last drawn row of the turn that is one of its answer blocks or, for a turn that has an answer, a closed stretch's head text; a turn that draws neither has none until its record is opened; and a record line that folds rows of a running turn holds the row back, which carries the panel's fifth rule over to that view. Why: a closed stretch can fold the whole answer under its head text, and Copy already takes the same text in either view. (3) The row for a compaction in progress stands before the region at the transcript's end where U1b draws a waiting prompt's card, and in the to-human view it stays in view, in no working record. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · The owner's two answers on the summaries' switch (U2, O-30).** "Summary switch": "A switch in MAWS (Recommended)". "Old summaries": "Only new folds (Recommended)". Both are what the design of 2026-11-15 built toward, so nothing of it moves: the switch is MAWS's, and it stops new summaries and hides none. Rejected by the owner's choice: one switch for the whole Mac, which would have MAWS write into the engine's configuration folder; no switch in MAWS, with one line in the engine's own settings; and every fold reading `working record` while the switch is off.
- **2026-11-16 · Four rulings before the build of U2b's second pull request (U2, O-30).** The document was read again on the merged chain, and four points went to the wave's architect. (1) An answer's action row stands only under a row with a drawn body: where the mod's answer for a row draws nothing there is none, laid out nowhere and not reachable by the keyboard; the placement stays U3b's and Copy still takes the turn's whole answer. (2) A row of browser activity under a call follows its call, with no ask of its own, shown exactly when its call's row is shown. (3) The id a row is kept under, by which a stored summary finds its rows, comes from the derivation the row's own ask uses, and a merged read keeps every member's id. (4) The summaries' switch is drawn in the mods list only, as the picture the owner answered showed it; the same row under Customize is unchanged. Why: each changes what a person sees or the cut of a milestone, so the document's author brought it to the wave's architect and decided none. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · A running launch's recorded advertisement survives a refused relaunch (U2, O-13; UC-13).** Found in the build of U2b's second pull request and ruled by the wave's architect: a relaunch reserved its advertisement over the one the running launch was bound to; when the host refused the relaunch, the running launch read as 8 while the mod drew at 9, and both sides would have drawn. The record now holds the reservation last bound to a launch and at most one not yet bound; a new reservation replaces only the unbound one, and binding moves it into the bound place. Why: the law that one side draws, never both. Rejected: undoing the reservation when the host refuses, which a daemon that ends before the undoing loses.
- **2026-11-16 · A plugin's connector row: whose report it reads, and which sign-in it shows (U11, X-184; U11h).** Rulings on U11h's branch review, which found three faults in the row's state, and on the review of their fixes. (1) A session's list of connectors is its latest report, whole: once the attached session's status has answered, that answer is its list, and a connector the answer omits is no longer read from what the session reported at its start; another session's list is what it last reported, at its start or after a plugin reload. A status answer asked before the session's latest report does not stand, and a fresh read follows the report. (2) A later report from the session a sign-in succeeded through replaces the success on the row, whichever session is admitted for the row, where only the attached session's report could before; a report older than the success never does. (3) A sign-in under way on a plugin's row is bound to the row it was started on and to that one operation: its step and Cancel stay on that row until it ends, whatever the engine's name reads meanwhile, and the binding ends when the operation ends or is replaced, whether the row is drawn or not. No sentence a person reads changes, and no field is added to the wire. Why: the list was the union of the start's report and the status answer, so a connector the answer omitted was still read; and the first fix let a read under way across a plugin reload answer with the older list. Rejected: keeping the union; the one case it served, a reload after a plugin change that nothing read again, is closed by reading the attached session's status again whenever its report moves.
- **2026-11-16 · A session that loaded no plugin supplying a connector says so on the row (U11, X-184; U11h).** Ruled in U11h's build: where a session lists a plugin's connector by name and loaded no plugin that supplies it, the row in My items reads "That session loaded no plugin that supplies this connector." and offers no Sign in; the sentence is the services' own for that refusal, in the row's wording, and stands after the line for another version of the plugin and before "Send a message in that session, then sign in." A decision made in the owner's place and reported to the owner. Rejected: the row drawing nothing for that case, as the build first had it.
- **2026-11-16 · A framed file that is not in view neither runs nor plays (U6, O-37; U6b-2).** Ruled on the first finding of U6b-2's branch review and widened during its fix wave. The rule is by condition, whatever took the file out of view: another tab in view, the Files tree standing in the file's place, the panel collapsed, a page covering the tab, and for a file's tab in the main area the side column maximized, floating over it or drawn as a sheet over a narrow window. While hidden the frame's element is not in the document; on return a video or a sound is at its kept time and paused, an HTML page starts again, and a file the transcript names or a failed file is read again. A partial cover is no hide, and neither is a minimized window or one behind another window: the rule is about what MAWS takes out of view inside its own window. Why: the document had named a hide by its causes and two were missing, and a body that is hidden while it stays mounted is not seen by a rule written as "a tab not in view has no body mounted"; the rule that held is the condition, read at one place for each surface that draws a framed file. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · What is read of an HTML page's head (U6, O-37; U6b-2).** Three rulings. (1) A value that is only a query or only a fragment names the page itself and is no file beside it. (2) The count of files beside a page reads a tag inside a comment or inside a script's text, so the line "Shown without the files beside it" can show for a page whose only such reference is commented out: accepted, with a tracker row that names the remedy. (3) The charset check skips comments and reads every other tag whole, by the HTML standard's encoding prescan. Why, for the third: Chromium's own scan skips comments, so a commented-out declaration made MAWS take the page as declared where Chromium found no declaration and decoded it as windows-1252. Rejected, for the third: keeping the limit as a tracker row, as it had stood for the charset check and as it stays for the count of files beside a page.
- **2026-11-16 · A moved tab, a short copy and the path's Save sentence (U6, O-37; U6b-2).** Three rulings on U6b-2's build and its merge into the chain. (1) The tab of a file the transcript names may ask for the file again when it moves between the side column and the main area, on one condition: the person sees the same file at the same place, a video or a sound keeps its position, and no failure line or empty frame shows in between where the file still reads. (2) A copy of another machine's file that ends before the size its first page gave is not written; the copy runs again within its tries and then fails as a file that kept changing, and an empty file still copies. (3) A file's path says where Save writes only for a body that can be saved, which as built is a text or Markdown file's; a framed file, an image and a file shown as a row show the plain path, the sentence being false where there is no Save. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · Rulings from the build of Source control's page and of a commit in Review (U5, l5-code-06, l5-code-01, l5-code-03).** Ruled by the wave's architect during U5b-2's build; the sentences a person reads are decisions made in the owner's place and reported to the owner. (1) A comment made on a commit, while Review shows any other scope, is listed in the row under the toolbar under "This comment is on commit `<sha7>`.", the hash a button that opens that commit at the comment's file; a comment made on no commit, while Review shows a commit's scope, is listed there under "This comment is not on this commit." and is never placed by its quote in that commit's diff. Why: the commit's filter had left both kinds drawn nowhere, so the unsent-comments chip had no row to open them in. Rejected: the standing sentences for a file or a line that is not in the diff, which are not true in every state that reaches these comments. (2) A commit with no change reads "No changes in this commit". (3) While the branch read has failed and has never answered, "This branch" stays enabled and shows the failure with Retry. Rejected: the chip disabled with no title, as first built, which gave the failure no sentence and no way to retry. (4) In the Pull request part, when no check failed, was cancelled or is pending, the counts line is not drawn and the folded line stands in its place; the part's first line always opens with the muted word "Checks"; a check's "↗" is named "Open `<check name>` on GitHub" for assistive technology. Why: with every check passed or skipped the page drew the same words on two lines, one over the other, and a part does not lose its label by state.
- **2026-11-16 · What a tab has loaded it keeps, and a read names a holder that is there (U5, l5-code-06, l5-code-03).** The review panel on U5b-2's branch confirmed five findings, and the review of their fixes three more in the lifetime those fixes introduced; the session that keeps master and the wave's architect ruled each by its condition, not by the one cited line. (1) A tab that has loaded a commit's read keeps it until the tab closes, its selection changes or its session is gone; losing a capability (a disconnect, or the reads no longer listed) stops new reads only, and Source control's commit list with an answer in hand stays shown. Why: showing another side tab and coming back had released the read and read it again. (2) Every read Review's store asks, now, later or again, is addressed by a holder that is there at the moment it is asked, never by the requester as it was; a tab retargeted to another session on the same commit keeps its answer and rebinds to that session. Why: the branch read went on asking in a folder that might be gone, and a kept commit's reads named a session that might be gone. (3) The working tree's unmerged paths mark a file "Conflict" in the working-tree scopes only; a commit's file kind comes from the commit alone. (4) The branch read resolves `HEAD` and the upstream once and passes those ids to every git run of the read. Why: a history that moved between the runs could give more not-pushed commits than commits and a negative pushed count. Not relaxed: a hidden tab keeps its read, and a release that arrives late deletes no newer hold. Where no alternative is named, none was rejected.
- **2026-11-16 · When a landing owes cross-build runs (UC-10, UC-14).** Restated by the session that keeps master: two cross-build runs, one against each of the two older builds, for a landing that takes a wire minor or changes a stored form; the same two for a landing that newly sends an entry on the wire between the app and its host (the rule of 2026-11-15 for U3b's command entry), and for one that changes a form it sends that an older window parses or a form stored in the engine's transcript (ruled the same day for the fix of the answer envelope's form, ticket 711, which takes no wire minor and stores nothing new); a landing that takes only a link minor owes none. Why: a pull request draft of the composer's second landing (U4b), which takes only a link minor, had listed two runs, against that landing's own ruled decision; and the envelope fix changes what an older window reads without changing the wire's manifest. Rejected: two runs asked of U4b, a line withdrawn the same day.

**Rulings reversed.** A later word of the owner's wins over an earlier ruling; each reversal is recorded in the document that held the ruling when the change lands (Parent-level acceptance 4). Each child's file gives the place and the sentence to record.
